\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\mathbb{E}\left[\overline{\Sigma}\right]}\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{\overline{\Sigma}^{2}}{\gamma^{*}-\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{{\mathcal{T}}(\overline{Y})}{a^{*}-{\mathcal{T}}(\overline{Y})}\right]\overline{\Sigma}}\right]\sqrt{\frac{1-w_{2}}{(1-w_{2})z_{1}+w_{1}z_{2}}},

(5.33)

\displaystyle\sigma_{U}

\displaystyle=\sqrt{\frac{1/\delta}{(1-w_{2})z_{1}+w_{1}z_{2}}}\left(\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{\overline{\Sigma}^{3}}{\left(\gamma^{*}-\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{{\mathcal{T}}(\overline{Y})}{a^{*}-{\mathcal{T}}(\overline{Y})}\right]\overline{\Sigma}\right)^{2}}\right]-\frac{1}{\mathbb{E}\left[\overline{\Sigma}\right]}\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{\overline{\Sigma}^{2}}{\gamma^{*}-\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{{\mathcal{T}}(\overline{Y})}{a^{*}-{\mathcal{T}}(\overline{Y})}\right]\overline{\Sigma}}\right]^{2}\right.

\displaystyle\phantom{=}~+\left.\frac{1}{\mathbb{E}\left[\overline{\Sigma}\right]^{2}}\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{\overline{\Sigma}^{2}}{\left(\gamma^{*}-\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{{\mathcal{T}}(\overline{Y})}{a^{*}-{\mathcal{T}}(\overline{Y})}\right]\overline{\Sigma}\right)^{2}}\right]\mathbb{E}\left[\overline{G}^{2}{\mathcal{F}}(\overline{Y})^{2}\right]\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{\overline{\Sigma}^{2}}{\gamma^{*}-\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{{\mathcal{T}}(\overline{Y})}{a^{*}-{\mathcal{T}}(\overline{Y})}\right]\overline{\Sigma}}\right]^{2}\right)^{1/2}.

(5.34)

Note that all these quantities are well-defined provided
a^{*}>a^{\circ}
.
Indeed,
w_{1}>0
and
1-w_{2}>0
under the latter condition.
Also, the second factor in the definition of
\sigma_{U}
is positive since the sum of the first two terms is non-negative by Cauchy-Schwarz and the third term is positive.
Define also
\gamma^{\sharp}
as the unique solution in
\left(s(a^{*}),\infty\right)
to

\displaystyle 1

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\delta}\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\frac{{\mathcal{T}}(\overline{Y})}{a^{*}-{\mathcal{T}}(\overline{Y})}\right)^{2}\right]\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{\overline{\Sigma}^{2}}{\left(\gamma^{\sharp}-\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{{\mathcal{T}}(\overline{Y})}{a^{*}-{\mathcal{T}}(\overline{Y})}\right]\overline{\Sigma}\right)^{2}}\right].

(5.35)

The well-posedness of
\gamma^{\sharp}
follows the same reasoning after
Equation

3.3
.

We now characterize the fixed points of state evolution and show that the recursion can be initialized precisely at the fixed point. The proof of the next two lemmas are obtained via a series of manipulations which are deferred to
Sections

A.2
and
A.3
.

Lemma 5.2

(Fixed points of state evolution)
.

The quintuple
(\mu_{t},\sigma_{U,t},\chi_{t+1},\sigma_{V,t+1},\gamma_{t+1})
in the recursion given by
Equations

5.30
and
5.27
has
3
fixed points
\mathsf{FP}_{+},\mathsf{FP}_{-},\mathsf{FP}_{0}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{5}
:

\displaystyle\mathsf{FP}_{+}=(\mu,\sigma_{U},\chi,\sigma_{V},\gamma^{*}),\quad\mathsf{FP}_{-}=(-\mu,\sigma_{U},-\chi,\sigma_{V},\gamma^{*}),

\displaystyle\mathsf{FP}_{0}=\left(0,\frac{1}{\sqrt{\delta}},0,\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{\overline{\Sigma}^{2}}{\left(\gamma^{\sharp}-\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{{\mathcal{T}}(\overline{Y})}{a^{*}-{\mathcal{T}}(\overline{Y})}\right]\overline{\Sigma}\right)^{2}}\right]^{-1/2},\gamma^{\sharp}\right),

where the parameters on the right are given in
Equations

5.32
,
5.33
,
5.34
,
5.35
and
5.24
.

We initialize the AMP iteration with

\displaystyle\widetilde{u}^{-1}=0_{n},\quad\widetilde{v}^{0}\coloneqq\mu\,\widetilde{\beta}^{*}+\sqrt{1-\mu^{2}\mathbb{E}\left[\overline{\Sigma}\right]}\,w\in{\mathbb{R}}^{d}

(5.36)

where we have set
\widetilde{\beta}^{*}=\Sigma^{1/2}\beta^{*}
,
w\sim{\mathcal{N}}(0_{d},I_{d})
is independent of everything else and
\mu
is given in
Equation

5.33
.
This choice is valid since from the proof of
Lemma

5.3
one can deduce that
1-\mu^{2}\mathbb{E}\left[\overline{\Sigma}\right]>0
.
The scaling ensures that

\plim_{d\to\infty}\left\|\widetilde{v}^{0}\right\|_{2}^{2}/d=1

almost surely.
According to
Equation

5.15
,
Equation

5.36
gives that the state evolution parameters are initialized as

\displaystyle\mu_{0}

\displaystyle=\frac{\delta}{\mathbb{E}\left[\overline{\Sigma}\right]}\lim_{n\to\infty}\frac{\mu}{n}\mathbb{E}\left[\left\langle\widetilde{\mathfrak{B}}^{*},\widetilde{\mathfrak{B}}^{*}\right\rangle\right]=\mu,\,\,\sigma_{U,0}^{2}=\plim_{n\to\infty}\frac{1}{n}\left\langle\widetilde{v}^{0},\widetilde{v}^{0}\right\rangle-\frac{\mathbb{E}\left[\overline{\Sigma}\right]}{\delta}\mu_{0}^{2}=\frac{1}{\delta}-\frac{\mathbb{E}\left[\overline{\Sigma}\right]}{\delta}\mu^{2}.

(5.37)

Lemma 5.3

(State evolution stays put)
.

Initialized with
Equation

5.37
, the parameters
(\mu_{t},\sigma_{U,t},\chi_{t+1},\sigma_{V,t+1})_{t\geq 0}
of the state evolution recursion in
Equations

5.30
and
5.27
stay at the initialization, that is, for every
t\geq 0
:

\displaystyle\mu_{t}

\displaystyle=\mu,\quad\sigma_{U,t}=\sigma_{U},\quad\chi_{t+1}=\chi,\quad\sigma_{V,t+1}=\sigma_{V},\quad\gamma_{t+1}=\gamma^{*},

where the right-hand sides are defined in
Equations

5.32
,
5.33
,
5.34
and
5.24
.

5.4
Right edge of the bulk of
D

Let

\displaystyle\widehat{D}

\displaystyle=\Sigma^{1/2}\widehat{X}^{\top}T\widehat{X}\Sigma^{1/2},\quad\mbox{with }\ T=\mathrm{diag}({\mathcal{T}}(y))=\mathrm{diag}({\mathcal{T}}(q(\widetilde{X}\Sigma^{1/2}\beta^{*},\varepsilon))),

(5.38)

and
\widehat{X}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{n\times d}
has i.i.d.
{\mathcal{N}}(0,1/n)
entries, independent of
T
.
One should think of
\widehat{D}
as a “decoupled” version of
D
in the sense that
\widehat{X}
and
T
are independent and no outlier eigenvalue is expected to show up in the spectrum of
\widehat{D}
.
This is to be contrasted with
D=\Sigma^{1/2}\widetilde{X}^{\top}T\widetilde{X}\Sigma^{1/2}
in which
T
depends on
\widetilde{X}
(see
Equation

5.38
),
and the top eigenvalue of
D
will be detached from the bulk of the spectrum provided that
a^{*}>a^{\circ}
.

Given the above intuition, one expects that the behaviour of the right edge of the bulk of
\widehat{D}
resembles that of
D
.
This is made formal in the following lemma, which is proved in
Section

A.4
.
The idea is to first show that
\lambda_{3}(\widehat{D})\leq\lambda_{2}(D)\leq\lambda_{1}(\widehat{D})
using the variational representation of eigenvalues, and then use
[
FSW21
,
Zha07
]
to show that both
\lambda_{1}(\widehat{D})
and
\lambda_{3}(\widehat{D})
converge to the right edge of the bulk of
\widehat{D}
.
We comment on the second step.
Building on the almost sure weak convergence result of the empirical spectral distribution of
\widehat{D}

[
Zha07
, Theorem 1.2.1]
, it was proved in
[
PS09
, Theorem 1]
that almost surely there exists no eigenvalue outside the support of the limiting spectral distribution, and
[
CH14
, Section 3]
further characterized the support.
However, both
[
PS09
,
CH14
]
assumed a positive semidefinite
T
which corresponds to
{\mathcal{T}}\geq 0
. Thus, we build on
[
Zha07
, Theorem 1.2.1]
and use a recent strong asymptotic freeness result of GOE and deterministic matrices
[
FSW21
, Theorem 4.3]
which guarantees the absence of eigenvalues outside the support of the limiting spectral distribution.
Of particular benefit to our purposes is that neither
[
Zha07
, Theorem 1.2.1]
nor
[
FSW21
, Theorem 4.3]
requires
T
to be PSD.

Lemma 5.4
.

Consider the matrices
D
and
\widehat{D}
in
Equations

1.2
and
5.38
, respectively.
Denote by
\overline{\mu}_{\widehat{D}}
the limiting spectral distribution of
\widehat{D}
.
Then, we have

\displaystyle\lim_{d\to\infty}\lambda_{2}(D)

\displaystyle=\sup\supp(\overline{\mu}_{\widehat{D}})\quad\text{almost surely}.

(5.39)

Next, we characterize the right edge of the support of
\overline{\mu}_{\widehat{D}}
. The detailed proof of the lemma below is given in
Appendix

E
, and it generalizes the analysis in
[
CH14
, Section 3]
, showing that the same characterization of the support therein also holds for a possibly non-positive
T
(or equivalently
{\mathcal{T}}
). The critical obstacle for non-positive
T
is that the Stieltjes-like transform
z\mapsto\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{{\mathcal{T}}(\overline{Y})}{{\mathcal{T}}(\overline{Y})-z}\right]
no longer maps the complex upper-half plane into itself, rendering parts of
[
CH14
]
using this property unusable. We treat this problem by considering meromorphic generalizations of various concepts in
[
CH14
]
(e.g.,
Proposition

E.8
in
Appendix

E
plays the role of Proposition 1.2 in
[
CH14
]
).

Lemma 5.5
.

Let
a^{\circ}>\sup\supp({\mathcal{T}}(\overline{Y}))
be the largest critical point of
\psi
.
Then, we have

\displaystyle\sup\supp(\overline{\mu}_{\widehat{D}})=\psi(a^{\circ}).

(5.40)

5.5
Concluding the proof of
Theorem

3.1

In this final section, we show that, if
a^{*}>a^{\circ}
,

\displaystyle\lim_{t\to\infty}\plim_{d\to\infty}\frac{\left\langle\widehat{v}^{t},v_{1}(D)\right\rangle^{2}}{\left\|\widehat{v}^{t}\right\|_{2}^{2}}=1,\qquad\plim_{d\to\infty}\lambda_{1}(D)=\lambda_{1}>\lambda_{2},

(5.41)

where
\lambda_{1},\lambda_{2}
are defined in
Equation

3.7
,
\widehat{v}^{t}\coloneqq\Sigma^{-1/2}(\gamma^{*}I_{d}-c\Sigma)^{-1}\Sigma v^{t}
and
v^{t}
is obtained from the GAMP iteration in
Equation

5.1
with denoisers in
Equations

5.25
and
5.26
and initializers in
Equation

5.36
. Then,
Equation

5.41
directly gives the first part of
Equation

3.11
in
Theorem

3.1
; the second part follows from
Lemmas

5.4
and
5.5
; and the expression in
Equation

3.12
for the overlap is a consequence of state evolution, whose proof is given at the end of this section.

Recall the following definitions:
B_{t+1}
in
Equation

5.28
,
f_{t+1}(v^{t+1})=B_{t+1}v^{t+1}
(see
Equation

5.26
) and
c=\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathcal{F}}(\overline{Y})\right]
(see
Equation

5.25
).
Let

\displaystyle b\coloneqq\frac{1}{\delta}\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{\overline{\Sigma}}{\gamma^{*}-c\overline{\Sigma}}\right],\quad B\coloneqq(\gamma^{*}I_{d}-c\Sigma)^{-1}\Sigma,

(5.42)

be the fixed points of
b_{t+1},B_{t+1}
, respectively, where
\gamma^{*}
(together with
a^{*}
) satisfies
Equation

5.7
.
Note that
b=1
by
Equation

3.3
.
For
t\geq 1
, define

\displaystyle e_{1}^{t}

\displaystyle\coloneqq u^{t}-u^{t-1}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{n},\quad e_{2}^{t}\coloneqq v^{t+1}-v^{t}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{d}.

(5.43)

The GAMP iteration in
Equation

5.1
can be written as

\displaystyle u^{t}

\displaystyle=\widetilde{X}B_{t}v^{t}-b_{t}Fu^{t-1},\quad v^{t+1}=\widetilde{X}^{\top}Fu^{t}-c_{t}B_{t}v^{t}.

(5.44)

Using the first equation in the second, we get

\displaystyle v^{t+1}

\displaystyle=(\widetilde{X}^{\top}F\widetilde{X}-c_{t}I_{d})B_{t}v^{t}-b_{t}\widetilde{X}^{\top}F^{2}u^{t-1}.

(5.45)

Using the definition of
e_{1}^{t}
in the iteration for
u^{t}
, we have

\displaystyle u^{t-1}

\displaystyle=\widetilde{X}B_{t}v^{t}-b_{t}Fu^{t-1}-e_{1}^{t}.

Solving for
u^{t-1}
yields:

\displaystyle u^{t-1}

\displaystyle=(b_{t}F+I_{n})^{-1}\widetilde{X}B_{t}v^{t}-(b_{t}F+I_{n})^{-1}e_{1}^{t}.

Then, we can eliminate
u^{t-1}
in the iteration for
v^{t+1}
by substituting the right-hand side above in
Equation

5.45
and, after some manipulations, we obtain

\displaystyle v^{t+1}

\displaystyle=\left[\widetilde{X}^{\top}F(b_{t}F+I_{n})^{-1}\widetilde{X}-c_{t}I_{d}\right]B_{t}v^{t}+b_{t}\widetilde{X}^{\top}F^{2}(b_{t}F+I_{n})^{-1}e_{1}^{t}.

We expand
b_{t}
and
B_{t}
respectively around their fixed points
b
and
B
to write

\displaystyle v^{t+1}

\displaystyle=\left[\widetilde{X}^{\top}F(bF+I_{n})^{-1}\widetilde{X}-cI_{d}\right]Bv^{t}

\displaystyle\phantom{=}+(b-b_{t})\widetilde{X}^{\top}F(b_{t}F+I_{n})^{-1}(bF+I_{n})^{-1}\widetilde{X}B_{t}v^{t}

\displaystyle\phantom{=}+(\gamma^{*}-\gamma_{t})\widetilde{X}^{\top}F(bF+I_{n})^{-1}\widetilde{X}(\gamma_{t}I_{d}-c\Sigma)^{-1}(\gamma^{*}I_{d}-c\Sigma)^{-1}\Sigma v^{t}

\displaystyle\phantom{=}+(c_{t}-c)B_{t}v^{t}+c(\gamma_{t}-\gamma^{*})(\gamma_{t}I_{d}-c\Sigma)^{-1}(\gamma^{*}I_{d}-c\Sigma)^{-1}\Sigma v^{t}

\displaystyle\phantom{=}+b_{t}\widetilde{X}^{\top}F^{2}(b_{t}F+I_{n})^{-1}e_{1}^{t}.

Using the definition of
e_{2}^{t}
, we further have

\displaystyle\begin{split}(I_{d}+cB)v^{t+1}&=\widetilde{X}^{\top}F(bF+I_{n})^{-1}\widetilde{X}Bv^{t}+cBe_{2}^{t}\\
&\phantom{=}+(b-b_{t})\widetilde{X}^{\top}F(b_{t}F+I_{n})^{-1}(bF+I_{n})^{-1}\widetilde{X}B_{t}v^{t}\\
&\phantom{=}+(\gamma^{*}-\gamma_{t})\widetilde{X}^{\top}F(bF+I_{n})^{-1}\widetilde{X}(\gamma_{t}I_{d}-c\Sigma)^{-1}(\gamma^{*}I_{d}-c\Sigma)^{-1}\Sigma v^{t}\\
&\phantom{=}+(c_{t}-c)B_{t}v^{t}+c(\gamma_{t}-\gamma^{*})(\gamma_{t}I_{d}-c\Sigma)^{-1}(\gamma^{*}I_{d}-c\Sigma)^{-1}\Sigma v^{t}\\
&\phantom{=}+b_{t}\widetilde{X}^{\top}F^{2}(b_{t}F+I_{n})^{-1}e_{1}^{t}.\end{split}

(5.46)

Define
e^{t}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{d}
by

\displaystyle\begin{split}e^{t}&\coloneqq c\Sigma^{1/2}Be_{2}^{t}+(b-b_{t})\Sigma^{1/2}\widetilde{X}^{\top}F(b_{t}F+I_{n})^{-1}(bF+I_{n})^{-1}\widetilde{X}B_{t}v^{t}\\
&\phantom{=}+(\gamma^{*}-\gamma_{t})\Sigma^{1/2}\widetilde{X}^{\top}F(bF+I_{n})^{-1}\widetilde{X}(\gamma_{t}I_{d}-c\Sigma)^{-1}(\gamma^{*}I_{d}-c\Sigma)^{-1}\Sigma v^{t}\\
&\phantom{=}+(c_{t}-c)\Sigma^{1/2}B_{t}v^{t}+c(\gamma_{t}-\gamma^{*})\Sigma^{1/2}(\gamma_{t}I_{d}-c\Sigma)^{-1}(\gamma^{*}I_{d}-c\Sigma)^{-1}\Sigma v^{t}\\
&\phantom{=}+b_{t}\Sigma^{1/2}\widetilde{X}^{\top}F^{2}(b_{t}F+I_{n})^{-1}e_{1}^{t}.\end{split}

(5.47)

Multiplying both sides of
Equation

5.46
by
\Sigma^{1/2}
, we arrive at

\displaystyle\Sigma^{1/2}(I_{d}+cB)v^{t+1}

\displaystyle=\Sigma^{1/2}\widetilde{X}^{\top}F(bF+I_{n})^{-1}\widetilde{X}Bv^{t}+e^{t}.

By the definition of
D
(see
Equation

1.2
) and the choice of
{\mathcal{F}}
(see
Equation

5.25
), we note that
\Sigma^{1/2}\widetilde{X}^{\top}F(bF+I_{n})^{-1}\widetilde{X}\Sigma^{1/2}=\frac{1}{a^{*}}D
(recall from
Equation

5.42
that
b=1
).
Also, by the definition of
B
(see
Equation

5.42
), we have the identity

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\gamma^{*}}\Sigma^{1/2}(I_{d}+cB)=\Sigma^{-1/2}B,

(5.48)

both sides of which we define to be
\widetilde{B}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{d\times d}
.
Using the above observations and letting

\displaystyle\widehat{v}^{t+1}\coloneqq\widetilde{B}v^{t+1}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{d},

(5.49)

we obtain

\displaystyle\widehat{v}^{t+1}

\displaystyle=M\widehat{v}^{t}+\frac{1}{\gamma^{*}}e^{t},\quad\text{where}\ M\coloneqq\frac{D}{\lambda_{1}},\ \lambda_{1}\coloneqq a^{*}\gamma^{*},

(5.50)

which takes the form of a power iteration with an error term.

It is now convenient to
shift the spectrum of
M
to the right so that all of its eigenvalues are positive.
Specifically, choose
\ell>0
to be a sufficiently large constant.
By
Equation

A.71
, it suffices to take

\ell=C_{D}+1>\left\|D\right\|_{2}+1
,
where the constant
C_{D}\in(0,\infty)
is defined in
Equation

A.70
.
Adding
\frac{\ell}{\lambda_{1}}\widehat{v}^{t+1}
on both sides of
Equation

5.50
and using the definitions of
\widehat{v}^{t}
in
Equation

5.49
and
e_{2}^{t}
in
Equation

5.43
, we have

\displaystyle\left(1+\frac{\ell}{\lambda_{1}}\right)\widehat{v}^{t+1}

\displaystyle=\frac{D+\ell I_{d}}{\lambda_{1}}\,\widehat{v}^{t}+\frac{\ell}{\lambda_{1}}\widetilde{B}e_{2}^{t}+\frac{1}{\gamma^{*}}e^{t}.

Using the following notation:

\displaystyle\widehat{M}

\displaystyle\coloneqq\frac{D+\ell I_{d}}{\lambda_{1}+\ell},\quad\widehat{e}^{t}\coloneqq\frac{\ell}{\lambda_{1}+\ell}\widetilde{B}e_{2}^{t}+\frac{a^{*}}{\lambda_{1}+\ell}e^{t},

(5.51)

we write the iteration as

\displaystyle\widehat{v}^{t+1}

\displaystyle=\widehat{M}\widehat{v}^{t}+\widehat{e}^{t}.

(5.52)

By construction,
\widehat{M}
is strictly positive definite, and
all results concerning the spectral properties of
\widehat{M}
can be easily translated to those of
M
by cancelling the shift
\ell
.

Suppose that the iteration in
Equation

5.52
has been run for a certain large constant
t>0
steps.
We further run it for an additional
t^{\prime}
steps for some large constant
t^{\prime}>0
.
By unrolling the iteration down to time
t
, we obtain

\displaystyle\widehat{v}^{t+t^{\prime}}

\displaystyle=\widehat{M}^{t^{\prime}}\widehat{v}^{t}+\widehat{e}^{t,t^{\prime}},

(5.53)

where

\displaystyle\widehat{e}^{t,t^{\prime}}

\displaystyle\coloneqq\sum_{s=1}^{t^{\prime}}\widehat{M}^{t^{\prime}-s}\widehat{e}^{t+s-1}.

(5.54)

Taking the normalized squared norm
\frac{1}{d}\left\|\cdot\right\|_{2}^{2}
on both sides of
Equation

5.53
and sending first
d
then
t
and finally
t^{\prime}
to infinity, we get the left-hand side

\displaystyle\phantom{=}~\lim_{t^{\prime}\to\infty}\lim_{t\to\infty}\plim_{d\to\infty}\frac{1}{d}\left\|\widehat{v}^{t+t^{\prime}}\right\|_{2}^{2}=\lim_{t^{\prime}\to\infty}\lim_{t\to\infty}\plim_{d\to\infty}\frac{1}{d}\left\|\widetilde{B}v^{t+t^{\prime}}\right\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle=\lim_{t^{\prime}\to\infty}\lim_{t\to\infty}\plim_{d\to\infty}\frac{1}{d}\left\|\Sigma^{-1/2}(\gamma^{*}I_{d}-c\Sigma)^{-1}\Sigma v^{t+t^{\prime}}\right\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle=\lim_{t^{\prime}\to\infty}\lim_{t\to\infty}\lim_{d\to\infty}\frac{1}{d}\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\Sigma^{-1/2}(\gamma^{*}I_{d}-c\Sigma)^{-1}\Sigma V_{t+t^{\prime}}\right\|_{2}^{2}\right]

\displaystyle=\lim_{t^{\prime}\to\infty}\lim_{t\to\infty}\lim_{d\to\infty}\frac{1}{d}\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\Sigma^{-1/2}(\gamma^{*}I_{d}-c\Sigma)^{-1}\Sigma\widetilde{\mathfrak{B}}^{*}\right\|_{2}^{2}\right]\chi_{t+t^{\prime}}^{2}

\displaystyle\phantom{=\lim_{t^{\prime}\to\infty}\lim_{t\to\infty}\lim_{d\to\infty}}~+\frac{1}{d}\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\Sigma^{-1/2}(\gamma^{*}I_{d}-c\Sigma)^{-1}\Sigma W_{V,t+t^{\prime}}\right\|_{2}^{2}\right]\sigma_{V,t+t^{\prime}}^{2}

\displaystyle=\lim_{t^{\prime}\to\infty}\lim_{t\to\infty}\lim_{d\to\infty}\frac{1}{d}\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathfrak{B}^{*}}^{\top}\Sigma^{1/2}\Sigma(\gamma^{*}I_{d}-c\Sigma)^{-1}\Sigma^{-1}(\gamma^{*}I_{d}-c\Sigma)^{-1}\Sigma\Sigma^{1/2}\mathfrak{B}^{*}\right]\chi_{t+t^{\prime}}^{2}

\displaystyle\phantom{=\lim_{t^{\prime}\to\infty}\lim_{t\to\infty}\lim_{d\to\infty}}~+\frac{1}{d}\mathbb{E}\left[W_{V,t+t^{\prime}}^{\top}\Sigma(\gamma^{*}I_{d}-c\Sigma)^{-1}\Sigma^{-1}(\gamma^{*}I_{d}-c\Sigma)^{-1}\Sigma W_{V,t+t^{\prime}}\right]\sigma_{V,t+t^{\prime}}^{2}

\displaystyle=\lim_{t^{\prime}\to\infty}\lim_{t\to\infty}\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{\overline{\Sigma}^{2}}{(\gamma^{*}-c\overline{\Sigma})^{2}}\right]\chi_{t+t^{\prime}}^{2}+\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{\overline{\Sigma}}{(\gamma^{*}-c\overline{\Sigma})^{2}}\right]\sigma_{V,t+t^{\prime}}^{2}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{\overline{\Sigma}^{2}}{(\gamma^{*}-c\overline{\Sigma})^{2}}\right]\chi^{2}+\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{\overline{\Sigma}}{(\gamma^{*}-c\overline{\Sigma})^{2}}\right]\sigma_{V}^{2}\eqqcolon\nu^{2},

(5.55)

where we use the state evolution result (
Proposition

5.1
) in the third equality.
Taking
\frac{1}{d}\left\|\cdot\right\|_{2}^{2}
and the same sequential limits on the right-hand side, we have:

\displaystyle\lim_{t^{\prime}\to\infty}\lim_{t\to\infty}\plim_{d\to\infty}\frac{1}{d}\left\|\widehat{M}^{t^{\prime}}\widehat{v}^{t}+\widehat{e}^{t,t^{\prime}}\right\|_{2}^{2}.

(5.56)

We claim that

\displaystyle\lim_{t^{\prime}\to\infty}\lim_{t\to\infty}\plim_{d\to\infty}\frac{1}{d}\left\|\widehat{e}^{t,t^{\prime}}\right\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle=0,

(5.57)

which implies, by the triangle inequality, that

Equation

5.56
is equal to

\displaystyle\lim_{t^{\prime}\to\infty}\lim_{t\to\infty}\plim_{d\to\infty}\frac{1}{d}\left\|\widehat{M}^{t^{\prime}}\widehat{v}^{t}\right\|_{2}^{2}.

(5.58)

The proof of
Equation

5.57
requires the technical analysis of various error terms, and it is deferred to
Section

A.5
.
The quantity in
Equation

5.58
can be decomposed as

\displaystyle\frac{1}{d}\left\|\widehat{M}^{t^{\prime}}\widehat{v}^{t}\right\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{d}\left\|\widehat{M}^{t^{\prime}}(\Pi+\Pi^{\perp})\widehat{v}^{t}\right\|_{2}^{2}=\frac{1}{d}\left\|\widehat{M}^{t^{\prime}}\Pi\widehat{v}^{t}\right\|_{2}^{2}+\frac{1}{d}\left\|\widehat{M}^{t^{\prime}}\Pi^{\perp}\widehat{v}^{t}\right\|_{2}^{2}+\frac{2}{d}\left\langle\widehat{M}^{t^{\prime}}\Pi\widehat{v}^{t},\widehat{M}^{t^{\prime}}\Pi^{\perp}\widehat{v}^{t}\right\rangle,

(5.59)

where
\Pi\coloneqq v_{1}(D)v_{1}(D)^{\top}
and
\Pi^{\perp}\coloneqq I_{d}-\Pi
.
Note that the eigendecomposition of
\widehat{M}^{t^{\prime}}
is

\displaystyle\widehat{M}^{t^{\prime}}

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=1}^{d}\lambda_{i}(\widehat{M}^{t^{\prime}})v_{i}(\widehat{M}^{t^{\prime}})v_{i}(\widehat{M}^{t^{\prime}})^{\top}=\sum_{i=1}^{d}\lambda_{i}(\widehat{M})^{t^{\prime}}v_{i}(D)v_{i}(D)^{\top},

since for any univariate polynomial
P
with real coefficients and any matrix
K\in{\mathbb{R}}^{d\times d}
,
P(K)
shares the same eigenspace with
K
and its eigenvalues are
\{P(\lambda_{i}(K))\}_{i\in\{1,\ldots,d\}}
.
Therefore, the first term on the right-hand side of
Equation

5.59
equals

\displaystyle\frac{1}{d}\left\|\widehat{M}^{t^{\prime}}\Pi\widehat{v}^{t}\right\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{d}\left\|\sum_{i=1}^{d}\lambda_{i}(\widehat{M})^{t^{\prime}}v_{i}(D)v_{i}(D)^{\top}\Pi\widehat{v}^{t}\right\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{d}\left\|\lambda_{1}(\widehat{M})^{t^{\prime}}v_{1}(D)v_{1}(D)^{\top}\widehat{v}^{t}\right\|_{2}^{2}=\lambda_{1}(\widehat{M})^{2t^{\prime}}\frac{\left\langle v_{1}(D),\widehat{v}^{t}\right\rangle^{2}}{d}.

(5.60)

The third term on the right-hand side of
Equation

5.59
vanishes:

\displaystyle\frac{1}{d}\left\langle\widehat{M}^{t^{\prime}}\Pi\widehat{v}^{t},\widehat{M}^{t^{\prime}}\Pi^{\perp}\widehat{v}^{t}\right\rangle

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{d}\left\langle\lambda_{1}(\widehat{M})^{t^{\prime}}\left\langle v_{1}(D),\widehat{v}^{t}\right\rangle\,v_{1}(D),\sum_{i=2}^{d}\lambda_{i}(\widehat{M})^{t^{\prime}}\left\langle v_{i}(D),\widehat{v}^{t}\right\rangle v_{i}(D)\right\rangle=0.

(5.61)

To analyze the second term on the right-hand side of
Equation

5.59
, we define the matrix

\displaystyle\widetilde{M}

\displaystyle\coloneqq\widehat{M}\Pi^{\perp}=\sum_{i=2}^{d}\lambda_{i}(\widehat{M})v_{i}(D)v_{i}(D)^{\top}.

We then have

\displaystyle\frac{1}{d}\left\|\widehat{M}^{t^{\prime}}\Pi^{\perp}\widehat{v}^{t}\right\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{d}\left\|\sum_{i=2}^{d}\lambda_{i}(\widehat{M})^{t^{\prime}}v_{i}(D)v_{i}(D)^{\top}\widehat{v}^{t}\right\|_{2}^{2}=\frac{1}{d}\left\|\widetilde{M}^{t^{\prime}}\widehat{v}^{t}\right\|_{2}^{2}\leq\frac{\left\|\widehat{v}^{t}\right\|_{2}^{2}}{d}\max_{v\in{\mathbb{S}}^{d-1}}\left\|\widetilde{M}^{t^{\prime}}v\right\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{\left\|\widehat{v}^{t}\right\|_{2}^{2}}{d}\sigma_{1}(\widetilde{M}^{t^{\prime}})^{2}=\frac{\left\|\widehat{v}^{t}\right\|_{2}^{2}}{d}\lambda_{1}(\widetilde{M}^{t^{\prime}})^{2}=\frac{\left\|\widehat{v}^{t}\right\|_{2}^{2}}{d}\lambda_{1}(\widetilde{M})^{2t^{\prime}}=\frac{\left\|\widehat{v}^{t}\right\|_{2}^{2}}{d}\lambda_{2}(\widehat{M})^{2t^{\prime}},

where the passages in the second line follow from the positive definiteness of
\widetilde{M}
.

We have proved in
Section

5.4
(see
Lemmas

5.4
and
5.5
) that almost surely

\displaystyle\lim_{d\to\infty}\lambda_{2}(D)

\displaystyle=\lambda_{2}\coloneqq a^{\circ}\gamma^{\circ}.

Recalling from
Equations

3.2
and
3.5
the definitions of
\psi,\zeta
, we can alternatively write
\lambda_{2}=\psi(a^{\circ})=\zeta(a^{\circ})
as in
Equation

3.7
.
Also recall from
Equation

5.50
that
\lambda_{1}=a^{*}\gamma^{*}=\psi(a^{*})
.
Under the condition
a^{*}>a^{\circ}
, we further have
\lambda_{1}=\zeta(a^{*})
as in
Equation

3.7
.
Thus, by the monotonicity of
\psi
(see
Lemma

E.1
), we obtain the strict inequality
\lambda_{1}>\lambda_{2}
in the second part of
Equation

5.41
.

In words, the limiting value of
\lambda_{2}(D)
is strictly less than
\lambda_{1}
.
In view of
Equation

5.51
, this gives that
\lim_{d\to\infty}\lambda_{2}(\widehat{M})<1
, which implies

\displaystyle\lim_{t^{\prime}\to\infty}\lim_{t\to\infty}\plimsup_{d\to\infty}\frac{1}{d}\left\|\widehat{M}^{t^{\prime}}\Pi^{\perp}\widehat{v}^{t}\right\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\lim_{t^{\prime}\to\infty}\lim_{t\to\infty}\plimsup_{d\to\infty}\frac{\left\|\widehat{v}^{t}\right\|_{2}^{2}}{d}\lambda_{2}(\widehat{M})^{2t^{\prime}}

\displaystyle\leq\lim_{t^{\prime}\to\infty}\left(\lim_{t\to\infty}\plim_{d\to\infty}\frac{\left\|\widehat{v}^{t}\right\|_{2}^{2}}{d}\right)\left(\lim_{d\to\infty}\lambda_{2}(\widehat{M})^{2t^{\prime}}\right)=0.

(5.62)

The last equality holds since the limit in the first parentheses is finite (see
Equation

5.55
).

Combining
Equations

5.60
,
5.62
and
5.61
, we obtain that the quantity in
Equation

5.58
equals:

\displaystyle\lim_{t^{\prime}\to\infty}\lim_{t\to\infty}\plim_{d\to\infty}\frac{1}{d}\left\|\widehat{M}^{t^{\prime}}\widehat{v}^{t}\right\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle=\lim_{t^{\prime}\to\infty}\lim_{t\to\infty}\plim_{d\to\infty}\lambda_{1}(\widehat{M})^{2t^{\prime}}\frac{\left\langle v_{1}(D),\widehat{v}^{t}\right\rangle^{2}}{d}

\displaystyle=\lim_{t^{\prime}\to\infty}\lim_{t\to\infty}\left(\plim_{d\to\infty}\lambda_{1}(\widehat{M})^{2t^{\prime}}\right)\left(\plim_{d\to\infty}\frac{\left\langle v_{1}(D),\widehat{v}^{t}\right\rangle^{2}}{d}\right)

\displaystyle=\left(\lim_{t^{\prime}\to\infty}\plim_{d\to\infty}\lambda_{1}(\widehat{M})^{2t^{\prime}}\right)\left(\lim_{t\to\infty}\plim_{d\to\infty}\frac{\left\langle v_{1}(D),\widehat{v}^{t}\right\rangle^{2}}{d}\right).

(5.63)

Now, putting
Equations

5.55
and
5.63
together, we arrive at the following relation:

\displaystyle\nu^{2}

\displaystyle=\left(\lim_{t^{\prime}\to\infty}\plim_{d\to\infty}\lambda_{1}(\widehat{M})^{2t^{\prime}}\right)\left(\lim_{t\to\infty}\plim_{d\to\infty}\frac{\left\langle v_{1}(D),\widehat{v}^{t}\right\rangle^{2}}{d}\right).

By
Equation

5.55
, this is equivalent to

\displaystyle 1

\displaystyle=\left(\lim_{t^{\prime}\to\infty}\plim_{d\to\infty}\lambda_{1}(\widehat{M})^{2t^{\prime}}\right)\left(\lim_{t\to\infty}\plim_{d\to\infty}\frac{\left\langle v_{1}(D),\widehat{v}^{t}\right\rangle^{2}}{\left\|\widehat{v}^{t}\right\|_{2}^{2}}\right).

(5.64)

This allows us to conclude:

\displaystyle\plim_{d\to\infty}\lambda_{1}(\widehat{M})

\displaystyle=1,\quad\lim_{t\to\infty}\plim_{d\to\infty}\frac{\left\langle v_{1}(D),\widehat{v}^{t}\right\rangle^{2}}{\left\|\widehat{v}^{t}\right\|_{2}^{2}}=1.

(5.65)

Indeed, otherwise if the limit of
\lambda_{1}(\widehat{M})^{2}
is different from
1
, the right-hand side of
Equation

5.64
will either be
0
(if
\plim\limits_{d\to\infty}\lambda_{1}(\widehat{M})^{2}\in[0,1)
) or
\infty
(if
\plim\limits_{d\to\infty}\lambda_{1}(\widehat{M})^{2}\in(1,\infty)
) once the limit with respect to
t^{\prime}\to\infty
is taken.
However, this contradicts the left-hand side of
Equation

5.64
.
Since
\widehat{M}
is positive definite,
\lambda_{1}(\widehat{M})
must converge to
1
(instead of
-1
).
Finally, note that by
Equation

5.51
, the first identity in
Equation

5.65
gives that
\plim_{d\to\infty}\lambda_{1}(D)=\lambda_{1}

and the second equation says that
\widehat{v}^{t}
is asymptotically aligned with
v_{1}(D)
.
This concludes the proof of
Equation

5.41
.

Proof of
Equation

3.12
.

Since
\widehat{v}^{t}
is asymptotically aligned with
v_{1}(D)
by
Equation

5.41
, the overlap between
v_{1}(D)
and
\beta^{*}
is the same as that between
\widehat{v}^{t}
and
\beta^{*}
in the large
t
limit.
Specifically,

\displaystyle\frac{\left\langle v_{1}(D),\beta^{*}\right\rangle^{2}}{\left\|\beta^{*}\right\|_{2}^{2}}

\displaystyle=\left\langle\frac{\widehat{v}^{t}}{\left\|\widehat{v}^{t}\right\|_{2}},\frac{\beta^{*}}{\sqrt{d}}\right\rangle^{2}+\left\langle{v_{1}(D)}-\frac{\widehat{v}^{t}}{\left\|\widehat{v}^{t}\right\|_{2}},\frac{\beta^{*}}{\sqrt{d}}\right\rangle^{2}

\displaystyle\phantom{=}+2\left\langle\frac{\widehat{v}^{t}}{\left\|\widehat{v}^{t}\right\|_{2}},\frac{\beta^{*}}{\sqrt{d}}\right\rangle\left\langle v_{1}(D)-\frac{\widehat{v}^{t}}{\left\|\widehat{v}^{t}\right\|_{2}},\frac{\beta^{*}}{\sqrt{d}}\right\rangle.

(5.66)

Note that
Equation

5.41
implies

\displaystyle\lim_{t\to\infty}\plim_{d\to\infty}\left\|\frac{\widehat{v}^{t}}{\left\|\widehat{v}^{t}\right\|_{2}}-v_{1}(D)\right\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle=0.

Therefore, we have

\displaystyle 0

\displaystyle\leq\lim_{t\to\infty}\plim_{d\to\infty}\left\langle v_{1}(D)-\frac{\widehat{v}^{t}}{\left\|\widehat{v}^{t}\right\|_{2}},\frac{\beta^{*}}{\sqrt{d}}\right\rangle^{2}\leq\lim_{t\to\infty}\plim_{d\to\infty}\left\|v_{1}(D)-\frac{\widehat{v}^{t}}{\left\|\widehat{v}^{t}\right\|_{2}}\right\|_{2}^{2}=0,

and

\displaystyle 0

\displaystyle\leq\lim_{t\to\infty}\plim_{d\to\infty}\left|\left\langle\frac{\widehat{v}^{t}}{\left\|\widehat{v}^{t}\right\|_{2}},\frac{\beta^{*}}{\sqrt{d}}\right\rangle\left\langle v_{1}(D)-\frac{\widehat{v}^{t}}{\left\|\widehat{v}^{t}\right\|_{2}},\frac{\beta^{*}}{\sqrt{d}}\right\rangle\right|\leq\lim_{t\to\infty}\plim_{d\to\infty}\left\|v_{1}(D)-\frac{\widehat{v}^{t}}{\left\|\widehat{v}^{t}\right\|_{2}}\right\|_{2}=0.

Then, taking the limit with respect to
d
and
t
on both sides of
Equation

5.66
, we obtain

\displaystyle\plim_{d\to\infty}\frac{\left\langle v_{1}(D),\beta^{*}\right\rangle^{2}}{\left\|\beta^{*}\right\|_{2}^{2}}

\displaystyle=\lim_{t\to\infty}\plim_{d\to\infty}\frac{\left\langle\widehat{v}^{t},\beta^{*}\right\rangle^{2}}{\left\|\widehat{v}^{t}\right\|_{2}^{2}\cdot d}=\frac{\lim\limits_{t\to\infty}\plim\limits_{d\to\infty}\frac{1}{d^{2}}\left\langle\widehat{v}^{t},\beta^{*}\right\rangle^{2}}{\lim\limits_{t\to\infty}\plim\limits_{d\to\infty}\frac{1}{d}\left\|\widehat{v}^{t}\right\|_{2}^{2}},

the right-hand side of which we compute below.

Note that the denominator has already been computed in
Equation

5.55
and equals
\nu^{2}
.
The numerator can be computed in a similar way using state evolution.
Recalling from
Equations

5.49
and
5.48
that
\widehat{v}^{t}=\Sigma^{-1/2}Bv^{t}
, we have

\displaystyle\lim_{t\to\infty}\plim_{d\to\infty}\frac{\left\langle\widehat{v}^{t},\beta^{*}\right\rangle^{2}}{d^{2}}

\displaystyle=\lim\limits_{t\to\infty}\lim\limits_{d\to\infty}\frac{1}{d^{2}}\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathfrak{B}^{*}}^{\top}\Sigma^{-1/2}BV_{t}\right]^{2}

\displaystyle=\lim\limits_{t\to\infty}\chi_{t}^{2}\lim\limits_{d\to\infty}\frac{1}{d^{2}}\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathfrak{B}^{*}}^{\top}\Sigma^{-1/2}B\widetilde{\mathfrak{B}}^{*}\right]^{2}

\displaystyle=\left(\lim\limits_{t\to\infty}\chi_{t}^{2}\right)\left(\lim\limits_{d\to\infty}\frac{1}{d^{2}}\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathfrak{B}^{*}}^{\top}\Sigma^{-1/2}(\gamma^{*}I_{d}-c\Sigma)^{-1}\Sigma\Sigma^{1/2}\mathfrak{B}^{*}\right]^{2}\right)

\displaystyle=\chi^{2}\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{\overline{\Sigma}}{\gamma^{*}-c\overline{\Sigma}}\right]^{2}.

Finally, recalling the expressions of
\chi,\sigma_{V}
in
Equation

5.32
, we obtain

\displaystyle\plim_{d\to\infty}\frac{\left\langle v_{1}(D),\beta^{*}\right\rangle^{2}}{\left\|\beta^{*}\right\|_{2}^{2}}

\displaystyle=\frac{\chi^{2}\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{\overline{\Sigma}}{\gamma^{*}-c\overline{\Sigma}}\right]^{2}}{\nu^{2}}

\displaystyle=\frac{\chi^{2}\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{\overline{\Sigma}}{\gamma^{*}-c\overline{\Sigma}}\right]^{2}}{\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{\overline{\Sigma}^{2}}{(\gamma^{*}-c\overline{\Sigma})^{2}}\right]\chi^{2}+\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{\overline{\Sigma}}{(\gamma^{*}-c\overline{\Sigma})^{2}}\right]\sigma_{V}^{2}}

\displaystyle=\frac{(1-w_{2})\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{\overline{\Sigma}}{\gamma^{*}-c\overline{\Sigma}}\right]^{2}}{(1-w_{2})\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{\overline{\Sigma}^{2}}{(\gamma^{*}-c\overline{\Sigma})^{2}}\right]+w_{1}\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{\overline{\Sigma}}{(\gamma^{*}-c\overline{\Sigma})^{2}}\right]}=\eta^{2},

as defined in
Equation

3.8
.

6
Discussion

Information-theoretic limits.

In some settings (e.g., phase retrieval), spectral estimators saturate information-theoretic limits when the design matrix is either i.i.d. Gaussian
[
MM19
]
or obtained from a uniformly random orthogonal matrix
[
DMM20
]
. That is, below the optimal spectral threshold,
no estimator
can achieve weak recovery, i.e., strictly positive asymptotic overlap with
\beta^{*}
.
Thus, it is natural to ask whether the spectral threshold in
Equation

3.13
is information-theoretically optimal for weak recovery in problems such as phase retrieval with correlated design.
Positive evidence in this regard comes from the comparison with
[
MLKZ20
]
which heuristically derives the information-theoretic weak recovery threshold for general right rotationally invariant designs. As mentioned in
Section

3.2
, by taking a Gaussian prior on
\beta^{*}
, the model in
Equation

1.1
is equivalent to one in which
X
is right rotationally invariant, and the threshold derived in
[
MLKZ20
]
in fact coincides with the expression in
Equation

3.13
(see
Remark

G.1
).
An interesting future direction would be to establish whether (and under what conditions) spectral estimators achieve the information-theoretic weak recovery threshold, or conversely to provide evidence of the existence of a statistical-to-computational gap.

Optimal covariance design.

Since our results characterize the performance of spectral estimators for a Gaussian design with any covariance
\Sigma
, a natural question is to characterize the
\Sigma
that induces the maximal overlap. A similar problem is considered in
[
MXM21
]
which studies the impact of the spectrum of a bi-rotationally invariant design matrix on the performance of a family of algorithms known as expectation propagation. In contrast, we consider spectral estimators, and our general Gaussian design is only left rotationally invariant.
In our context, given the characterization of the limiting overlap
\eta=\eta(\delta,\overline{\Sigma},{\mathcal{T}})
in
Equation

3.8
and the expression for the optimal preprocessing
{\mathcal{T}}^{*}
in
Equation

3.14
, the problem can be formulated as maximizing
\eta(\overline{\Sigma},{\mathcal{T}}^{*},\delta)
over
\overline{\Sigma}
, for any fixed
\delta
.
Remarkably,
Figure

3
in
Section

4
shows that picking
\Sigma=I_{d}
may not be optimal for the phase retrieval problem. This is in contrast with
[
MXM21
]
, where it is proved that “spikier” spectra are better for phase retrieval.

Discovering spikes in random matrices via AMP.

Our proof strategy offers a new, general methodology for analyzing large spiked random matrices.
We expect this strategy to be useful in a variety of statistical inference problems beyond GLMs with correlated Gaussian designs, including rotationally invariant designs
[
MKLZ22
]
, mixtures of GLMs
[
ZMV22
]
, principal component analysis with inhomogeneous noise
[
PKK23
]
, and the universality of spiked random matrices
[
DLS23
,
WZF22
]
.
For many models, the “null” setting in which no information is present can be understood using tools from random matrix theory.
When statistically informative components emerge as spectral outliers, our proof recipe can be carried out – as long as an AMP iteration can be designed to simulate the desired power iteration. Suitably combining the analysis for AMP with the random matrix theory arguments for the bulk then allows one to determine the exact outlier locations and estimation accuracy.

Organization of the appendix.

Appendix

A
contains the proofs of a number of intermediate results useful to show
Theorem

3.1
.
Appendix

B
contains the proof of
Theorem

3.2
.
Appendix

C
shows how to remove
Items

(A6)
and
(A7)
.
Appendix

D
states and proves a few useful properties of auxiliary functions and parameters.
Appendix

E

contains the proof of
Lemma

5.5
.
Appendix

F
establishes the performance of the whitened spectral estimator.
Appendix

G
presents some useful auxiliary results.

Appendix A
Details of the proof of Theorem
3.1

A.1
Proof of
Proposition

5.1

We start by defining the state evolution random vectors
(U_{t},V_{t+1})_{t\geq 0}
in a different, but equivalent form.
Let
U_{0}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{n}
be a Gaussian random vector whose joint distribution with
G
is given by

\begin{bmatrix}G\\
U_{0}\end{bmatrix}\sim{\mathcal{N}}(0_{2n},\Omega_{0}\otimes I_{n})
,
where
\Omega_{0}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{2\times 2}
is defined as

\displaystyle\Omega_{0}

\displaystyle=\begin{bmatrix}\frac{1}{\delta}\mathbb{E}\left[\overline{\Sigma}\right]&\displaystyle\lim_{n\to\infty}\frac{1}{n}\mathbb{E}\left[\left\langle\widetilde{\mathfrak{B}}^{*},f_{0}(\widetilde{\mathfrak{B}}^{*})\right\rangle\right]\\
\displaystyle\lim_{n\to\infty}\frac{1}{n}\mathbb{E}\left[\left\langle\widetilde{\mathfrak{B}}^{*},f_{0}(\widetilde{\mathfrak{B}}^{*})\right\rangle\right]&\displaystyle\frac{1}{\delta}\left(\plim_{d\to\infty}\frac{1}{\sqrt{d}}\left\|\widetilde{v}^{0}\right\|_{2}\right)^{2}\end{bmatrix}.

(A.1)

For each
t\geq 0
,
define the random vectors
U_{t}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{n}
and
V_{t+1}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{d}
such that

\displaystyle\begin{bmatrix}G\\
U_{t}\end{bmatrix}

\displaystyle\sim{\mathcal{N}}\left(0_{2n},\Omega_{t}\otimes I_{n}\right),\quad V_{t+1}=\chi_{t+1}\widetilde{\mathfrak{B}}^{*}+\sigma_{V,t+1}W_{V,t+1},

(A.2)

where
W_{V,t+1}\sim{\mathcal{N}}(0_{d},I_{d})
is independent of
\widetilde{\mathfrak{B}}^{*}
and
\Omega_{t}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{2\times 2},\chi_{t+1}\in{\mathbb{R}},\sigma_{V,t+1}\in{\mathbb{R}}
are defined recursively as

\displaystyle\Omega_{t}

\displaystyle=\begin{bmatrix}\displaystyle\frac{1}{\delta}\mathbb{E}\left[\overline{\Sigma}\right]&\displaystyle\lim_{n\to\infty}\frac{1}{n}\mathbb{E}\left[\left\langle\widetilde{\mathfrak{B}}^{*},f_{t}(V_{t})\right\rangle\right]\\
\displaystyle\lim_{n\to\infty}\frac{1}{n}\mathbb{E}\left[\left\langle\widetilde{\mathfrak{B}}^{*},f_{t}(V_{t})\right\rangle\right]&\displaystyle\lim_{n\to\infty}\frac{1}{n}\mathbb{E}\left[\left\langle f_{t}(V_{t}),f_{t}(V_{t})\right\rangle\right]\end{bmatrix},

(A.3)

\displaystyle\chi_{t+1}

\displaystyle=\lim_{n\to\infty}\frac{1}{n}\mathbb{E}\left[\div_{G}\widetilde{g}_{t}(U_{t},G,\varepsilon)\right],\quad\sigma_{V,t+1}^{2}=\lim_{n\to\infty}\frac{1}{n}\mathbb{E}\left[\left\langle g_{t}(U_{t};Y),g_{t}(U_{t};Y)\right\rangle\right].

(A.4)

Here the function
\widetilde{g}_{t}\colon({\mathbb{R}}^{n})^{3}\to{\mathbb{R}}^{n}
is given by
\widetilde{g}_{t}(U_{t},G,\varepsilon)=g_{t}(U_{t};q(G,\varepsilon))
.

We now show that the alternative representations of
U_{t}
and
\chi_{t+1}
in
Equations

A.1
,
A.2
,
A.3
and
A.4
are equivalent to
Equations

5.14
,
5.15
,
5.16
and
5.17
.

Proposition A.1
.

The random vectors
(G,U_{t})
defined in
Equation

A.2
can be alternatively written as

\displaystyle U_{t}

\displaystyle=\mu_{t}G+\sigma_{U,t}W_{U,t},

(A.5)

where
(G,W_{U,t})\sim{\mathcal{N}}\left(0_{n},\frac{\mathbb{E}\left[\overline{\Sigma}\right]}{\delta}I_{n}\right)\otimes{\mathcal{N}}(0_{n},I_{n})
; for
t=0
,

\displaystyle\mu_{0}

\displaystyle=\frac{\delta}{\mathbb{E}\left[\overline{\Sigma}\right]}\lim_{n\to\infty}\frac{1}{n}\mathbb{E}\left[\left\langle\widetilde{\mathfrak{B}}^{*},f_{0}(\widetilde{\mathfrak{B}}^{*})\right\rangle\right],\quad\sigma_{U,0}^{2}=\plim_{n\to\infty}\frac{1}{n}\left\langle\widetilde{v}^{0},\widetilde{v}^{0}\right\rangle-\frac{\mathbb{E}\left[\overline{\Sigma}\right]}{\delta}\mu_{0}^{2}

(A.6)

and for
t\geq 1
,

\displaystyle\mu_{t}

\displaystyle=\frac{\delta}{\mathbb{E}\left[\overline{\Sigma}\right]}\lim_{n\to\infty}\frac{1}{n}\mathbb{E}\left[\left\langle\widetilde{\mathfrak{B}}^{*},f_{t}(V_{t})\right\rangle\right],\quad\sigma_{U,t}^{2}=\lim_{n\to\infty}\frac{1}{n}\mathbb{E}\left[\left\langle f_{t}(V_{t}),f_{t}(V_{t})\right\rangle\right]-\frac{\mathbb{E}\left[\overline{\Sigma}\right]}{\delta}\mu_{t}^{2}.

(A.7)

Furthermore, the scalar
\chi_{t+1}
defined in
Equation

A.4
can be alternatively written as

\displaystyle\chi_{t+1}

\displaystyle=\frac{\delta}{\mathbb{E}\left[\overline{\Sigma}\right]}\lim_{n\to\infty}\frac{1}{n}\mathbb{E}\left[\left\langle G,g_{t}(U_{t};Y)\right\rangle\right]-\mu_{t}\lim_{n\to\infty}\frac{1}{n}\mathbb{E}\left[\div_{U_{t}}g_{t}(U_{t};Y)\right].

(A.8)

Proof.

The decomposition of
U_{t}
in
Equation

A.5
and the expressions of
\mu_{t},\sigma_{U,t}
in
Equations

A.6
and
A.7
can be easily obtained from
Equations

A.1
and
A.3
using the following elementary proprty of Gaussian random variables.
If

\displaystyle(G_{1},G_{2})\sim{\mathcal{N}}\left(0_{2},\begin{bmatrix}\sigma_{1,1}&\sigma_{1,2}\\
\sigma_{1,2}&\sigma_{2,2}\end{bmatrix}\right),

then their joint law can be realized as

\displaystyle(G_{1},G_{2})\overset{\mathrm{d}}{=}\left(G_{1},\frac{\sigma_{1,2}}{\sigma_{1,1}}\,G_{1}+\sqrt{\sigma_{2,2}-\frac{\sigma_{1,2}^{2}}{\sigma_{1,1}}}\,W\right),

(A.9)

where
W\sim{\mathcal{N}}(0,1)
is independent of
G_{1}
.

To show
Equation

A.8
, we use the chain rule and Stein’s lemma. We have:

\displaystyle\chi_{t+1}

\displaystyle=\lim_{n\to\infty}\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{\partial}{\partial G_{i}}\widetilde{g}_{t}(U_{t},G,\varepsilon)_{i}\right]

\displaystyle=\lim_{n\to\infty}\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{\partial}{\partial G_{i}}g_{t}(U_{t};q(G,\varepsilon))_{i}\right]

\displaystyle=\lim_{n\to\infty}\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left(\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{\partial}{\partial G_{i}}g_{t}(\mu_{t}G+\sigma_{U,t}W_{U,t};q(G,\varepsilon))_{i}\right]-\mu_{t}\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{\partial}{\partial U_{t,i}}g_{t}(U_{t};Y)_{i}\right]\right)

(A.10)

\displaystyle=\lim_{n\to\infty}\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left(\frac{\delta}{\mathbb{E}\left[\overline{\Sigma}\right]}\mathbb{E}\left[G_{i}g_{t}(\mu_{t}G+\sigma_{U,t}W_{U,t};q(G,\varepsilon))_{i}\right]-\mu_{t}\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{\partial}{\partial U_{t,i}}g_{t}(U_{t};Y)_{i}\right]\right)

(A.11)

\displaystyle=\frac{\delta}{\mathbb{E}\left[\overline{\Sigma}\right]}\lim_{n\to\infty}\frac{1}{n}\mathbb{E}\left[\left\langle G,g_{t}(U_{t};Y)\right\rangle\right]-\mu_{t}\lim_{n\to\infty}\frac{1}{n}\mathbb{E}\left[\div_{U_{t}}g_{t}(U_{t};Y)\right].

Equation

A.10
follows from the chain rule of derivatives:

\displaystyle\frac{\partial}{\partial G_{i}}g_{t}(U_{t};q(G,\varepsilon))_{i}=\frac{\partial}{\partial G_{i}}g_{t}(\mu_{t}G+\sigma_{U,t}W_{U,t};q(G,\varepsilon))_{i}

\displaystyle=\mu_{t}\frac{\partial}{\partial U_{t,i}}g_{t}(U_{t};Y)_{i}+\frac{\partial}{\partial G_{i}}g_{t}(U_{t};q(G,\varepsilon))_{i}.

Equation

A.11
is by Stein’s lemma, noting that
G\sim{\mathcal{N}}\left(0_{n},\frac{\mathbb{E}\left[\overline{\Sigma}\right]}{\delta}I_{n}\right)
.
∎

Next, we show the desired state evolution result.

Proof of
Proposition

5.1
.

Define the rescaled version of
\widetilde{X}
as
\widecheck{X}\coloneqq\sqrt{\frac{n}{n+d}}\,\widetilde{X}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{n\times d}
.
Note that each entry of
\widecheck{X}
is i.i.d. according to
{\mathcal{N}}(0,1/(n+d))
and that

g=X\beta^{*}=\widetilde{X}\widetilde{\beta}^{*}
.
Consider a pair of matrix-valued iterates
p^{t}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{n\times 2}
and
q^{t}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{d\times 2}
defined as

\displaystyle p^{t}

\displaystyle=\begin{bmatrix}\widecheck{u}^{t}&g\end{bmatrix}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{n\times 2},\quad q^{t}=\begin{bmatrix}\widecheck{v}^{t}-\widecheck{\chi}_{t-1}\widetilde{\beta}^{*}&\,0_{d}\end{bmatrix}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{d\times 2},

(A.12)

where
(\widecheck{u}^{t},\widecheck{v}^{t},\widecheck{\chi}_{t-1})_{t\geq 0}\subset{\mathbb{R}}^{n+d+1}
will be specified later in
Equation

A.27
.
For
(i,j)\in\{1,\ldots,n\}\times\{1,2\}
, we use
p_{j}^{t}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{n}
and
p_{i,j}^{t}\in{\mathbb{R}}
to denote the
j
-th column and the
(i,j)
-th entry of the matrix
p^{t}
, respectively.
Similar notation is used for other matrix-valued iterates.
Consider also a pair of denoising functions
\pi_{t}\colon{\mathbb{R}}^{d\times 3}\to{\mathbb{R}}^{d\times 2}
and
\rho_{t}\colon{\mathbb{R}}^{n\times 3}\to{\mathbb{R}}^{n\times 2}
defined as

\displaystyle\begin{split}\pi_{t}(q^{t};\widetilde{\beta}^{*})&=\sqrt{\frac{n+d}{n}}\begin{bmatrix}\widecheck{f}_{t}(q_{1}^{t}+\widecheck{\chi}_{t-1}\widetilde{\beta}^{*})&\,\widetilde{\beta}^{*}\end{bmatrix}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{d\times 2},\\
\rho_{t}(p^{t};\varepsilon)&=\begin{bmatrix}\sqrt{\frac{n+d}{n}}\,\widecheck{g}_{t}(p_{1}^{t};q(p_{2}^{t};\varepsilon))&\,0_{n}\end{bmatrix}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{n\times 2},\end{split}

(A.13)

where
(\widecheck{f}_{t},\widecheck{g}_{t})_{t\geq 0}
will be specified later in
Equation

A.27
.
We claim that the iteration

\displaystyle\begin{split}p^{t+1}&=\widecheck{X}\widetilde{q}^{t}-\widetilde{p}^{t-1}\ell_{t}^{\top},\quad\widetilde{p}^{t}=\rho_{t}(p^{t};\varepsilon),\quad\ell_{t}=\frac{1}{n+d}\sum_{i=1}^{d}\begin{bmatrix}\frac{\partial\pi_{t}(q^{t};\widetilde{\beta}^{*})_{i,1}}{\partial q^{t}_{i,1}}&\frac{\partial\pi_{t}(q^{t};\widetilde{\beta}^{*})_{i,1}}{\partial q^{t}_{i,2}}\\
\frac{\partial\pi_{t}(q^{t};\widetilde{\beta}^{*})_{i,2}}{\partial q^{t}_{i,1}}&\frac{\partial\pi_{t}(q^{t};\widetilde{\beta}^{*})_{i,2}}{\partial q^{t}_{i,2}}\end{bmatrix},\\
q^{t+1}&=\widecheck{X}^{\top}\widetilde{p}^{t}-\widetilde{q}^{t-1}m_{t}^{\top},\quad\widetilde{q}^{t}=\pi_{t}(q^{t};\widetilde{\beta}^{*}),\quad m_{t}=\frac{1}{n+d}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\begin{bmatrix}\frac{\partial\rho_{t}(p^{t};\varepsilon)_{i,1}}{\partial p^{t}_{i,1}}&\frac{\partial\rho_{t}(p^{t};\varepsilon)_{i,1}}{\partial p^{t}_{i,2}}\\
\frac{\partial\rho_{t}(p^{t};\varepsilon)_{i,2}}{\partial p^{t}_{i,1}}&\frac{\partial\rho_{t}(p^{t};\varepsilon)_{i,2}}{\partial p^{t}_{i,2}}\end{bmatrix},\end{split}

(A.14)

initialized with
\pi_{-1}=0,\rho_{-1}=0
and
p^{0}=\begin{bmatrix}\widecheck{u}^{0}&g\end{bmatrix},q^{0}=\begin{bmatrix}\widecheck{v}^{0}&0_{d}\end{bmatrix}
(for some
\widecheck{u}^{0}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{n},\widecheck{v}^{0}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{d}
to be specified later in
Equation

A.27
),
is equivalent to the following iteration:

\displaystyle\begin{split}\widecheck{u}^{t+1}&=\widetilde{X}\widecheck{f}_{t}(\widecheck{v}^{t})-\widecheck{b}_{t}\widecheck{g}_{t-1}(\widecheck{u}^{t-1};y),\quad\widecheck{b}_{t}=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{d}\frac{\partial\widecheck{f}_{t}(\widecheck{v}^{t})_{i}}{\partial\widecheck{v}_{i}^{t}},\\
\widecheck{v}^{t+1}&=\widetilde{X}^{\top}\widecheck{g}_{t}(\widecheck{u}^{t};y)-\widecheck{c}_{t}\widecheck{f}_{t-1}(\widecheck{v}^{t-1}),\quad\widecheck{c}_{t}=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\frac{\partial\widecheck{g}_{t}(\widecheck{u}^{t};y)_{i}}{\partial\widecheck{u}_{i}^{t}},\end{split}

(A.15)

initialized with
\widecheck{f}_{-1}=0,\widecheck{g}_{-1}=0
and
\widecheck{u}^{0}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{n},\widecheck{v}^{0}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{d}
.

Let us verify the equivalence.
By the design of the matrix-valued iterates in
Equation

A.12
and the matrix-valued denoisers in
Equation

A.13
, we have

\displaystyle\widetilde{p}^{t}

\displaystyle=\rho_{t}\left(\begin{bmatrix}\widecheck{u}^{t}&g\end{bmatrix};\varepsilon\right)=\begin{bmatrix}\sqrt{\frac{n+d}{n}}\,\widecheck{g}_{t}(\widecheck{u}^{t};q(g;\varepsilon))&0_{n}\end{bmatrix}=\begin{bmatrix}\sqrt{\frac{n+d}{n}}\,\widecheck{g}_{t}(\widecheck{u}^{t};y)&0_{n}\end{bmatrix},

\displaystyle\widetilde{q}^{t}

\displaystyle=\pi_{t}\left(\begin{bmatrix}\widecheck{v}^{t}-\widecheck{\chi}_{t-1}\widetilde{\beta}^{*}&0_{d}\end{bmatrix};\widetilde{\beta}^{*}\right)=\sqrt{\frac{n+d}{n}}\begin{bmatrix}\widecheck{f}_{t}(\widecheck{v}^{t})&\widetilde{\beta}^{*}\end{bmatrix}.

Furthermore, by chain rule of derivatives, the matrices
\ell_{t},m_{t}
specialize to

\displaystyle\ell_{t}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n+d}\sum_{i=1}^{d}\begin{bmatrix}\sqrt{\frac{n+d}{n}}\frac{\partial\widecheck{f}_{t}(\widecheck{v}^{t})_{i}}{\partial\widecheck{v}_{i}^{t}}&0\\
0&0\end{bmatrix}=\sqrt{\frac{n}{n+d}}\begin{bmatrix}\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{d}\frac{\partial\widecheck{f}_{t}(\widecheck{v}^{t})_{i}}{\partial\widecheck{v}_{i}^{t}}&0\\
0&0\end{bmatrix}=\sqrt{\frac{n}{n+d}}\begin{bmatrix}\widecheck{b}_{t}&0\\
0&0\end{bmatrix},

\displaystyle m_{t}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n+d}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\begin{bmatrix}\sqrt{\frac{n+d}{n}}\frac{\partial\widecheck{g}_{t}(\widecheck{u}^{t};y)_{i}}{\partial\widecheck{u}_{i}^{t}}&\sqrt{\frac{n+d}{n}}\frac{\partial\widecheck{g}_{t}(\widecheck{u}^{t};q(g;\varepsilon))_{i}}{\partial g_{i}}\\
0&0\end{bmatrix}=\sqrt{\frac{n}{n+d}}\begin{bmatrix}\widecheck{c}_{t}&\widecheck{\chi}_{t}\\
0&0\end{bmatrix}.

Using these expressions, we write the iteration in
Equation

A.14
as

\displaystyle\begin{bmatrix}\widecheck{u}^{t+1}&g\end{bmatrix}

\displaystyle=\sqrt{\frac{n+d}{n}}\widecheck{X}\begin{bmatrix}\widecheck{f}_{t}(\widecheck{v}^{t})&\widetilde{\beta}^{*}\end{bmatrix}-\begin{bmatrix}\sqrt{\frac{n+d}{n}}\,\widecheck{g}_{t-1}(\widecheck{u}^{t-1};y)&0_{n}\end{bmatrix}\sqrt{\frac{n}{n+d}}\begin{bmatrix}\widecheck{b}_{t}&0\\
0&0\end{bmatrix},

\displaystyle\begin{bmatrix}\widecheck{v}^{t+1}-\widecheck{\chi}_{t}\widetilde{\beta}^{*}&0_{d}\end{bmatrix}

\displaystyle=\widecheck{X}^{\top}\begin{bmatrix}\sqrt{\frac{n+d}{n}}\,\widecheck{g}_{t}(\widecheck{u}^{t};y)&0_{n}\end{bmatrix}-\sqrt{\frac{n+d}{n}}\begin{bmatrix}\widecheck{f}_{t-1}(\widecheck{v}^{t-1})&\widetilde{\beta}^{*}\end{bmatrix}\sqrt{\frac{n}{n+d}}\begin{bmatrix}\widecheck{c}_{t}&0\\
\widecheck{\chi}_{t}&0\end{bmatrix}.

Expanding the above equations into vector form and using the relation between
\widetilde{X}
and
\widecheck{X}
, we obtain:

\displaystyle\widecheck{u}^{t+1}

\displaystyle=\widetilde{X}\widecheck{f}_{t}(\widecheck{v}^{t})-\widecheck{b}_{t}\widecheck{g}_{t-1}(\widecheck{u}^{t-1};y),\quad g=\widetilde{X}\widetilde{\beta}^{*},

\displaystyle\widecheck{v}^{t+1}

\displaystyle=\widetilde{X}^{\top}\widecheck{g}_{t}(\widecheck{u}^{t};y)-\widecheck{c}_{t}\widecheck{f}_{t-1}(\widecheck{v}^{t-1}),

which matches
Equation

A.15
and the definition of
g
.

The iteration in
Equation

A.14
is an instance of the abstract graph-based AMP iteration proposed in
[
GB23
]
. To see this, consider a simple graph on two vertices
v_{1},v_{2}
with two directed edges
\vec{e}=(v_{1},v_{2})
to
\reflectbox{$\vec{\reflectbox{$e$}}$}=(v_{2},v_{1})
between them.
The tuple
(\widetilde{X},p^{t},\pi_{t})
is associated with the edge
\vec{e}
and the tuple
(\widetilde{X}^{\top},q^{t},\rho_{t})
is associated with

\vec{\reflectbox{$e$}}

.
We record below the state evolution results in
[
GB23
, Section 3.3]
for our special case of
Equation

A.14
, and then translate them to
Equation

A.15
.
For each
t\geq 1
, define two sequences of random matrices

\displaystyle(P_{0},P_{1},\cdots,P_{t})

\displaystyle\sim{\mathcal{N}}(0_{2n(t+1)},\Theta_{t}\otimes I_{n}),\quad(Q_{0},Q_{1},\cdots,Q_{t})\sim{\mathcal{N}}(0_{2d(t+1)},\Xi_{t}\otimes I_{d}),

(A.16)

where
P_{r}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{n\times 2},Q_{r}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{d\times 2}
(
0\leq r\leq t
),
and the entries of the covariance matrices
\Theta_{t},\Xi_{t}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{2(t+1)\times 2(t+1)}
are specified recursively as follows:
for
0\leq r,s\leq t
,

\displaystyle(\Theta_{t})_{r+1,s+1}

\displaystyle=\lim_{n\to\infty}\frac{1}{n+d}\mathbb{E}\left[\pi_{r}(Q_{r};\widetilde{\mathfrak{B}}^{*})^{\top}\pi_{s}(Q_{s}\widetilde{\mathfrak{B}}^{*})\right]\in{\mathbb{R}}^{2\times 2},

\displaystyle(\Xi_{t})_{r+1,s+1}

\displaystyle=\lim_{n\to\infty}\frac{1}{n+d}\mathbb{E}\left[\rho_{r}(P_{r};\varepsilon)^{\top}\rho_{s}(P_{s};\varepsilon)\right]\in{\mathbb{R}}^{2\times 2}.

The notation
(P_{0},P_{1},\cdots,P_{t})\in({\mathbb{R}}^{n\times 2})^{t+1}
should be interpreted as a
2n(t+1)
-dimensional vector given by

\displaystyle\begin{bmatrix}(P_{0})_{1}\\
(P_{0})_{2}\\
\vdots\\
(P_{t})_{1}\\
(P_{t})_{2}\end{bmatrix}

where
(P_{r})_{j}
(
0\leq r\leq t,j\in\{1,2\}
) denotes the
j
-th column of
P_{r}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{n\times 2}
.
The notation
(Q_{0},Q_{1},\cdots,Q_{t})\in({\mathbb{R}}^{d\times 2})^{t+1}
should be interpreted in a similar way.
Accordingly,
\Theta_{t},\Xi_{t}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{2(t+1)\times 2(t+1)}
are block matrices whose
(r+1,s+1)
-st (
0\leq r,s\leq t
) block has size
2\times 2
.

The state evolution result in
[
GB23
, Theorem 1 and Section 3.3]
asserts that for any uniformly pseudo-Lipschitz functions
h_{1}\colon{\mathbb{R}}^{2n(t+1)}\to{\mathbb{R}},h_{2}\colon{\mathbb{R}}^{2d(t+1)}\to{\mathbb{R}}
of finite order,

\displaystyle\begin{split}\plim_{n\to\infty}h_{1}(p^{0},p^{1},\cdots,p^{t})-\mathbb{E}\left[h_{1}(P_{0},P_{1},\cdots,P_{t})\right]&=0,\\
\plim_{d\to\infty}h_{2}(q^{0},q^{1},\cdots,q^{t})-\mathbb{E}\left[h_{2}(Q_{0},Q_{1},\cdots,Q_{t})\right]&=0.\end{split}

(A.17)

With the reduction in
Equations

A.12
and
A.13
, the state evolution iterates become

\displaystyle P_{t}

\displaystyle=\begin{bmatrix}\widecheck{U}_{t}&G\end{bmatrix},\quad Q_{t}=\begin{bmatrix}\widecheck{V}_{t}-\widecheck{\chi}_{t-1}\widetilde{\mathfrak{B}}^{*}&0_{d}\end{bmatrix},

whose covariance structure specializes to

\displaystyle(\Theta_{t})_{r+1,s+1}

\displaystyle=\lim_{n\to\infty}\frac{1}{n+d}\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{n+d}{n}\begin{bmatrix}\widecheck{f}_{r}(\widecheck{V}_{r})&\widetilde{\beta}^{*}\end{bmatrix}^{\top}\begin{bmatrix}\widecheck{f}_{s}(\widecheck{V}_{s})&\widetilde{\beta}^{*}\end{bmatrix}\right]

\displaystyle=\begin{bmatrix}\lim\limits_{n\to\infty}\frac{1}{n}\mathbb{E}\left[\widecheck{f}_{r}(\widecheck{V}_{r})^{\top}\widecheck{f}_{s}(\widecheck{V}_{s})\right]&\lim\limits_{n\to\infty}\frac{1}{n}\mathbb{E}\left[\widecheck{f}_{r}(\widecheck{V}_{r})^{\top}\widetilde{\mathfrak{B}}^{*}\right]\\
\lim\limits_{n\to\infty}\frac{1}{n}\mathbb{E}\left[\widecheck{f}_{s}(\widecheck{V}_{s})^{\top}\widetilde{\mathfrak{B}}^{*}\right]&\lim\limits_{n\to\infty}\frac{1}{n}\mathbb{E}\left[(\widetilde{\mathfrak{B}}^{*})^{\top}\widetilde{\mathfrak{B}}^{*}\right]\end{bmatrix},

(A.18)

\displaystyle(\Xi_{t})_{r+1,s+1}

\displaystyle=\lim_{n\to\infty}\frac{1}{n+d}\mathbb{E}\left[\begin{bmatrix}\sqrt{\frac{n+d}{n}}\widecheck{g}_{r}(\widecheck{U}_{r};Y)&0_{n}\end{bmatrix}^{\top}\begin{bmatrix}\sqrt{\frac{n+d}{n}}\widecheck{g}_{s}(\widecheck{U}_{s};Y)&0_{n}\end{bmatrix}\right]

\displaystyle=\begin{bmatrix}\lim\limits_{n\to\infty}\frac{1}{n}\mathbb{E}\left[\widecheck{g}_{r}(\widecheck{U}_{r};Y)^{\top}\widecheck{g}_{s}(\widecheck{U}_{s};Y)\right]&0\\
0&0\end{bmatrix}.

(A.19)

Reorganizing the elements of
P_{t},Q_{t}
and
\Theta_{t},\Xi_{t}
, we obtain

\displaystyle(G,\widecheck{U}_{0},\cdots,\widecheck{U}_{t})\sim{\mathcal{N}}(0_{n(t+2)},\widecheck{\Theta}_{t}\otimes I_{n}),

\displaystyle(\widecheck{V}_{0}-\widecheck{\chi}_{-1}\widetilde{\mathfrak{B}}^{*},\cdots,\widecheck{V}_{t}-\widecheck{\chi}_{t-1}\widetilde{\mathfrak{B}}^{*})\sim{\mathcal{N}}(0_{d(t+1)},\widecheck{\Xi}_{t}\otimes I_{d}),

(A.20)

where the entries of
\widecheck{\Theta}_{t}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{(t+2)\times(t+2)}
and
\widecheck{\Xi}_{t}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{(t+1)\times(t+1)}
are obtained as follows from
\Theta_{t}
and
\Xi_{t}
. Recalling that each entry
(\Theta_{t})_{r,s},(\Xi_{t})_{r,s}
of
\Theta_{t},\Xi_{t}
, respectively, is itself a
2\times 2
matrix, we use
((\Theta_{t})_{r,s})_{i,j},((\Xi_{t})_{r,s})_{i,j}
to denote the
(i,j)
-th (
i,j\in\{1,2\}
) entry of
(\Theta_{t})_{r,s},(\Xi_{t})_{r,s}
, respectively:

\displaystyle(\widecheck{\Theta}_{t})_{1,1}=((\Theta_{t})_{1,1})_{2,2},\quad(\widecheck{\Theta}_{t})_{1,s}=((\Theta_{t})_{s-1,s-1})_{1,2},\quad 2\leq s\leq t+2,

\displaystyle(\widecheck{\Theta}_{t})_{r,s}=(\widecheck{\Theta}_{t})_{s,r}=((\Theta_{t})_{r-1,s-1})_{1,1},\quad 2\leq r\leq s\leq t+2,

\displaystyle(\widecheck{\Xi}_{t})_{r,s}=(\widecheck{\Xi}_{t})_{s,r}=((\Xi_{t})_{r,s})_{1,1},\quad 1\leq r\leq s\leq t+1.

We further transform
\widecheck{\Theta}_{t}
by introducing
\widecheck{\Omega}_{t}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{2\times 2},\widecheck{\Phi}_{t}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{(t+1)\times(t+1)}
.
First, we have
(G,\widecheck{U}_{t})\sim{\mathcal{N}}(0_{2},\widecheck{\Omega}_{t})
where

\displaystyle\widecheck{\Omega}_{t}

\displaystyle=\begin{bmatrix}(\widecheck{\Theta}_{t})_{1,1}&(\widecheck{\Theta}_{t})_{1,t+2}\\
(\widecheck{\Theta}_{t})_{1,t+2}&(\widecheck{\Theta}_{t})_{t+2,t+2}\end{bmatrix}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{2\times 2}.

(A.21)

Next, applying the representation in
Equation

A.5
to
(G,\widecheck{U}_{t})
, we write
\widecheck{U}_{t}=\widecheck{\mu}_{t}G+\widecheck{\sigma}_{U,t}\widecheck{W}_{U,t}
.
Here
\widecheck{\mu}_{t}
can be derived in a way similar to
Proposition

A.1
:

\displaystyle\widecheck{\mu}_{t}

\displaystyle=\frac{(\widecheck{\Theta}_{t})_{1,t+2}}{(\widecheck{\Theta}_{t})_{1,1}}=\frac{((\Theta_{t})_{t+1,t+1})_{1,2}}{(\widecheck{\Theta}_{t})_{1,1}}=\frac{\delta}{\mathbb{E}\left[\overline{\Sigma}\right]}\lim\limits_{n\to\infty}\frac{1}{n}\mathbb{E}\left[\widecheck{f}_{t}(\widecheck{V}_{t})^{\top}\widetilde{\mathfrak{B}}^{*}\right],

(A.22)

where the last equality is obtained by recalling
Equation

A.18
.
Moreover,
(\widecheck{\sigma}_{U,0}\widecheck{W}_{U,0},\cdots,\widecheck{\sigma}_{U,t}\widecheck{W}_{U,t})\sim{\mathcal{N}}(0_{n(t+1)},\widecheck{\Phi}_{t}\otimes I_{n})
are jointly Gaussian whose covariance can be derived from
\widecheck{\Theta}_{t}
.
For any
0\leq r,s\leq t
,

\displaystyle(\widecheck{\Theta}_{t})_{r+2,s+2}=\frac{1}{n}\mathbb{E}\left[\left\langle\widecheck{U}_{r},\widecheck{U}_{s}\right\rangle\right]

\displaystyle=\widecheck{\mu}_{r}\widecheck{\mu}_{s}(\widecheck{\Theta}_{t})_{1,1}+\frac{1}{n}\mathbb{E}\left[\left\langle\widecheck{\sigma}_{U,r}\widecheck{W}_{U,r},\widecheck{\sigma}_{U,s}\widecheck{W}_{U,s}\right\rangle\right],

from which we obtain

\displaystyle(\widecheck{\Phi}_{t})_{r+1,s+1}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n}\mathbb{E}\left[\left\langle\widecheck{\sigma}_{U,r}\widecheck{W}_{U,r},\widecheck{\sigma}_{U,s}\widecheck{W}_{U,s}\right\rangle\right]=(\widecheck{\Theta}_{t})_{r+2,s+2}-\widecheck{\mu}_{r}\widecheck{\mu}_{s}(\widecheck{\Theta}_{t})_{1,1}

\displaystyle=((\Theta_{t})_{r+1,s+1})_{1,1}-\frac{((\Theta_{t})_{r+1,r+1})_{1,2}((\Theta_{t})_{s+1,s+1})_{1,2}}{((\Theta_{t})_{1,1})_{2,2}}.

(A.23)

We claim that the the above expression equals

\displaystyle\lim_{n\to\infty}\frac{1}{n}\mathbb{E}\left[\left\langle\widecheck{f}_{r}(\widecheck{V}_{r})-\widecheck{\mu}_{r}\widetilde{\mathfrak{B}}^{*},\widecheck{f}_{s}(\widecheck{V}_{s})-\widecheck{\mu}_{s}\widetilde{\mathfrak{B}}^{*}\right\rangle\right].

(A.24)

Indeed,

\displaystyle\phantom{=}~\lim_{n\to\infty}\frac{1}{n}\mathbb{E}\left[\left\langle\widecheck{f}_{r}(\widecheck{V}_{r})-\widecheck{\mu}_{r}\widetilde{\mathfrak{B}}^{*},\widecheck{f}_{s}(\widecheck{V}_{s})-\widecheck{\mu}_{s}\widetilde{\mathfrak{B}}^{*}\right\rangle\right]

\displaystyle=\lim_{n\to\infty}\frac{1}{n}\bigg(\mathbb{E}\left[\left\langle\widecheck{f}_{r}(\widecheck{V}_{r}),\widecheck{f}_{s}(\widecheck{V}_{s})\right\rangle\right]-\widecheck{\mu}_{s}\mathbb{E}\left[\left\langle\widecheck{f}_{r}(\widecheck{V}_{r}),\widetilde{\mathfrak{B}}^{*}\right\rangle\right]

\displaystyle\hskip 60.00009pt-\widecheck{\mu}_{r}\mathbb{E}\left[\left\langle\widecheck{f}_{s}(\widecheck{V}_{s}),\widetilde{\mathfrak{B}}^{*}\right\rangle\right]+\widecheck{\mu}_{r}\widecheck{\mu}_{s}\mathbb{E}\left[\left\langle\widetilde{\mathfrak{B}}^{*},\widetilde{\mathfrak{B}}^{*}\right\rangle\right]\bigg)

\displaystyle=\lim_{n\to\infty}\frac{1}{n}\mathbb{E}\left[\left\langle\widecheck{f}_{r}(\widecheck{V}_{r}),\widecheck{f}_{s}(\widecheck{V}_{s})\right\rangle\right]-\frac{\delta}{\mathbb{E}\left[\overline{\Sigma}\right]}\left(\lim_{n\to\infty}\frac{1}{n}\mathbb{E}\left[\left\langle\widecheck{f}_{r}(\widecheck{V}_{r}),\widetilde{\mathfrak{B}}^{*}\right\rangle\right]\right)\left(\lim_{n\to\infty}\frac{1}{n}\mathbb{E}\left[\left\langle\widecheck{f}_{s}(\widecheck{V}_{s}),\widetilde{\mathfrak{B}}^{*}\right\rangle\right]\right),

which agrees with
Equation

A.23
.
In the last equality, we use
Equation

A.22
.

Finally, for
t\geq 0
, let
\widecheck{\sigma}_{V,t}\widecheck{W}_{V,t}\coloneqq\widecheck{V}_{t}-\widecheck{\chi}_{t-1}\widetilde{\mathfrak{B}}^{*}
where
\widecheck{W}_{V,t}\sim{\mathcal{N}}(0,1)
is independent of
\widetilde{\mathfrak{B}}^{*}
.
From
Equation

A.20
, we have
(\widecheck{\sigma}_{V,0}\widecheck{W}_{V,0},\cdots,\widecheck{\sigma}_{V,t}\widecheck{W}_{V,t})\sim{\mathcal{N}}(0_{d(t+1)},\widecheck{\Xi}_{t}\otimes I_{d})
where
\widecheck{\Xi}_{t}
has entries

\displaystyle(\widecheck{\Xi}_{t})_{r+1,s+1}

\displaystyle=((\Xi_{t})_{r+1,s+1})_{1,1}=\lim_{n\to\infty}\frac{1}{n}\mathbb{E}\left[\left\langle\widecheck{g}_{r}(\widecheck{U}_{r};Y),\widecheck{g}_{s}(\widecheck{U}_{s};Y)\right\rangle\right].

(A.25)

With
(\widecheck{\mu}_{t},\widecheck{\sigma}_{U,t})
(or equivalently
\widecheck{\Omega}_{t}
),
\widecheck{\Phi}_{t},\widecheck{\chi}_{t-1},\widecheck{\Xi}_{t}
at hand,
Equation

A.17
naturally translates to the following state evolution result.
For any uniformly pseudo-Lipschitz functions
h_{1}\colon{\mathbb{R}}^{n(t+2)}\to{\mathbb{R}},h_{2}\colon{\mathbb{R}}^{d(t+2)}\to{\mathbb{R}}
of finite order,

\displaystyle\begin{split}\plim_{n\to\infty}h_{1}(g,\widecheck{u}_{0},\cdots,\widecheck{u}_{t})-\mathbb{E}\left[h_{1}(G,\widecheck{U}_{0},\cdots,\widecheck{U}_{t})\right]&=0,\\
\plim_{d\to\infty}h_{2}(\widetilde{\beta}^{*},\widecheck{v}_{0},\cdots,\widecheck{v}_{t})-\mathbb{E}\left[h_{2}(\widetilde{\mathfrak{B}}^{*},\widecheck{V}_{0},\cdots,\widecheck{V}_{t})\right]&=0.\end{split}

(A.26)

Note that the AMP iteration in
Equation

A.15
is almost the same as that in
Equation

5.1
albeit with a difference in time indices.
Indeed, the following relabeling maps
Equation

A.15
to
Equation

5.1
precisely:

\begin{gathered}\widecheck{u}^{2t-1}=u^{t-1},\quad\widecheck{v}^{2t}=v^{t},\quad t\geq 1,\\
\widecheck{u}^{0}=0_{n},\quad\widecheck{f}_{0}(\widecheck{v}^{0})=\widetilde{v}^{0},\\
\widecheck{g}_{2t-1}=g_{t},\quad\widecheck{g}_{2t}=0,\quad\widecheck{f}_{2t-1}=0,\quad\widecheck{f}_{2t}=f_{t},\quad\widecheck{\chi}_{2t-2}=0,\quad\widecheck{\chi}_{2t-1}=\chi_{t},\quad t\geq 1,\\
\widecheck{g}_{0}=0,\quad\widecheck{f}_{0}=0,\quad\widecheck{\chi}_{-1}=0.\end{gathered}

(A.27)

The change of indices above is similar to that presented in
[
GB23
, Appendix A]
.

The change of time index in
Equation

A.27
also maps respectively
(\widecheck{\mu}_{2t-1},\widecheck{\sigma}_{U,2t-1})
(or equivalently
\widecheck{\Omega}_{2t-1}
),
\widecheck{\Phi}_{2t-1},\widecheck{\chi}_{2t-1},\widecheck{\Xi}_{2t}
in
Equations

A.21
,
A.22
,
A.23
and
A.25
to
(\mu_{t},\sigma_{U,t})
(or equivalently
\Omega_{t}
),
\Phi_{t},\chi_{t},\Psi_{t}
in
Equations

A.3
,
5.17
,
5.19
,
5.20
and
5.21
.
Thus, the convergence result in
Equation

A.26
translates to
Equation

5.22
, which completes the proof.
∎

A.2
Proof of
Lemma

5.2

We start by simplifying the recursion in
Equation

5.30
using the distributional properties of various random variables/vectors in
Equations

2.3
,
5.11
and
5.14
.
First,

\displaystyle\mu_{t}

\displaystyle=\frac{\delta}{\mathbb{E}\left[\overline{\Sigma}\right]}\lim_{n\to\infty}\frac{1}{n}\mathbb{E}\left[(\widetilde{\mathfrak{B}}^{*})^{\top}(\gamma_{t}I_{d}-c\Sigma)^{-1}\Sigma(\chi_{t}\widetilde{\mathfrak{B}}^{*}+\sigma_{V,t}W_{V,t})\right]

(A.28)

\displaystyle=\chi_{t}\frac{\delta}{\mathbb{E}\left[\overline{\Sigma}\right]}\lim_{n\to\infty}\frac{1}{n}\mathbb{E}\left[(\widetilde{\mathfrak{B}}^{*})^{\top}(\gamma_{t}I_{d}-c\Sigma)^{-1}\Sigma\widetilde{\mathfrak{B}}^{*}\right]

(A.29)

\displaystyle=\chi_{t}\frac{\delta}{\mathbb{E}\left[\overline{\Sigma}\right]}\lim_{n\to\infty}\frac{1}{n}\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathfrak{B}^{*}}^{\top}\Sigma^{1/2}(\gamma_{t}I_{d}-c\Sigma)^{-1}\Sigma\Sigma^{1/2}\mathfrak{B}^{*}\right]

(A.30)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\mathbb{E}\left[\overline{\Sigma}\right]}\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{\overline{\Sigma}^{2}}{\gamma_{t}-\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathcal{F}}(\overline{Y})\right]\overline{\Sigma}}\right]\chi_{t}.

(A.31)

Equation

A.28
is by the definition of
B_{t}
(see
Equation

5.28
) and
V_{t}
(see
Equation

5.14
).

Equation

A.29
holds since
W_{V,t}
is independent of
\widetilde{\mathfrak{B}}^{*}
.

Equation

A.30
is by the definition of
\widetilde{\mathfrak{B}}^{*}
(see
Equation

5.11
).
In
Equation

A.31
we use
Proposition

G.2
, the distribution of
\mathfrak{B}^{*}
(see
Equation

5.11
) and the assumption
d/n\to 1/\delta
.

Second,

\displaystyle\sigma_{U,t}^{2}

\displaystyle=\lim_{n\to\infty}\frac{1}{n}\mathbb{E}\left[(\chi_{t}\widetilde{\mathfrak{B}}^{*}+\sigma_{V,t}W_{V,t})^{\top}\Sigma(\gamma_{t}I_{d}-c\Sigma)^{-2}\Sigma(\chi_{t}\widetilde{\mathfrak{B}}^{*}+\sigma_{V,t}W_{V,t})\right]-\frac{\mathbb{E}\left[\overline{\Sigma}\right]}{\delta}\mu_{t}^{2}

\displaystyle=\chi_{t}^{2}\lim_{n\to\infty}\frac{1}{n}\mathbb{E}\left[(\mathfrak{B}^{*})^{\top}\Sigma^{1/2}\Sigma(\gamma_{t}I_{d}-c\Sigma)^{-2}\Sigma\Sigma^{1/2}\mathfrak{B}^{*}\right]

\displaystyle\phantom{=}~+\sigma_{V,t}^{2}\lim_{n\to\infty}\frac{1}{n}\mathbb{E}\left[W_{V,t}^{\top}\Sigma(\gamma_{t}I_{d}-c\Sigma)^{-2}\Sigma W_{V,t}\right]-\frac{\mathbb{E}\left[\overline{\Sigma}\right]}{\delta}\mu_{t}^{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\delta}\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{\overline{\Sigma}^{3}}{(\gamma_{t}-\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathcal{F}}(\overline{Y})\right]\overline{\Sigma})^{2}}\right]\chi_{t}^{2}+\frac{1}{\delta}\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{\overline{\Sigma}^{2}}{(\gamma_{t}-\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathcal{F}}(\overline{Y})\right]\overline{\Sigma})^{2}}\right]\sigma_{V,t}^{2}-\frac{1}{\delta}\mathbb{E}\left[\overline{\Sigma}\right]\mu_{t}^{2}

(A.32)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\delta}\left(\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{\overline{\Sigma}^{3}}{(\gamma_{t}-\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathcal{F}}(\overline{Y})\right]\overline{\Sigma})^{2}}\right]-\frac{1}{\mathbb{E}\left[\overline{\Sigma}\right]}\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{\overline{\Sigma}^{2}}{\gamma_{t}-\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathcal{F}}(\overline{Y})\right]\overline{\Sigma}}\right]^{2}\right)\,\chi_{t}^{2}

\displaystyle\phantom{=}~+\frac{1}{\delta}\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{\overline{\Sigma}^{2}}{(\gamma_{t}-\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathcal{F}}(\overline{Y})\right]\overline{\Sigma})^{2}}\right]\sigma_{V,t}^{2},

(A.33)

where we use
Equation

A.31
in
Equation

A.33
.

Third,

\displaystyle\chi_{t+1}

\displaystyle=\frac{\delta}{\mathbb{E}\left[\overline{\Sigma}\right]}\lim_{n\to\infty}\frac{1}{n}\mathbb{E}\left[G^{\top}\mathrm{diag}({\mathcal{F}}(Y))(\mu_{t}G+\sigma_{U,t}W_{U,t})\right]-\mu_{t}\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathcal{F}}(\overline{Y})\right]

(A.34)

\displaystyle=\frac{\delta}{\mathbb{E}\left[\overline{\Sigma}\right]}\lim_{n\to\infty}\frac{1}{n}\mathbb{E}\left[G^{\top}\mathrm{diag}({\mathcal{F}}(Y))G\right]\mu_{t}-\mu_{t}\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathcal{F}}(\overline{Y})\right]

(A.35)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\frac{\delta}{\mathbb{E}\left[\overline{\Sigma}\right]}\overline{G}^{2}-1\right){\mathcal{F}}(\overline{Y})\right]\mu_{t}

(A.36)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\mathbb{E}\left[\overline{\Sigma}\right]}\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\frac{\delta}{\mathbb{E}\left[\overline{\Sigma}\right]}\overline{G}^{2}-1\right){\mathcal{F}}(\overline{Y})\right]\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{\overline{\Sigma}^{2}}{\gamma_{t}-\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathcal{F}}(\overline{Y})\right]\overline{\Sigma}}\right]\chi_{t}.

(A.37)

Equation

A.34
is by the definition of
U_{t}
(see
Equation

A.5
).

Equation

A.35
holds since
W_{U,t}
is independent of
G
and hence also independent of
Y
.

Equation

A.36
follows since each entry of
G
and
{\mathcal{F}}(Y)
is i.i.d. and hence

\displaystyle\lim_{n\to\infty}\frac{1}{n}\mathbb{E}\left[G^{\top}\mathrm{diag}({\mathcal{F}}(Y))G\right]=\lim_{n\to\infty}\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}\left[G_{i}^{2}{\mathcal{F}}(Y_{i})\right]=\mathbb{E}\left[\overline{G}^{2}{\mathcal{F}}(\overline{Y})\right].

Equation

A.37
follows from
Equation

A.31
.

Fourth,

\displaystyle\sigma_{V,t+1}^{2}

\displaystyle=\lim_{n\to\infty}\frac{1}{n}\mathbb{E}\left[(\mu_{t}G+\sigma_{U,t}W_{U,t})^{\top}\mathrm{diag}({\mathcal{F}}(Y))^{2}(\mu_{t}G+\sigma_{U,t}W_{U,t})\right]

\displaystyle=\mu_{t}^{2}\lim_{n\to\infty}\frac{1}{n}\mathbb{E}\left[G^{\top}\mathrm{diag}({\mathcal{F}}(Y))^{2}G\right]+\sigma_{U,t}^{2}\lim_{n\to\infty}\frac{1}{n}\mathbb{E}\left[W_{U,t}^{\top}\mathrm{diag}({\mathcal{F}}(Y))^{2}W_{U,t}\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[\overline{G}^{2}{\mathcal{F}}(\overline{Y})^{2}\right]\mu_{t}^{2}+\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathcal{F}}(\overline{Y})^{2}\right]\sigma_{U,t}^{2}

(A.38)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\mathbb{E}\left[\overline{\Sigma}\right]^{2}}\mathbb{E}\left[\overline{G}^{2}{\mathcal{F}}(\overline{Y})^{2}\right]\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{\overline{\Sigma}^{2}}{\gamma_{t}-\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathcal{F}}(\overline{Y})\right]\overline{\Sigma}}\right]^{2}\chi_{t}^{2}

\displaystyle\phantom{=}~+\frac{\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathcal{F}}(\overline{Y})^{2}\right]}{\delta}\left(\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{\overline{\Sigma}^{3}}{(\gamma_{t}-\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathcal{F}}(\overline{Y})\right]\overline{\Sigma})^{2}}\right]-\frac{1}{\mathbb{E}\left[\overline{\Sigma}\right]}\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{\overline{\Sigma}^{2}}{\gamma_{t}-\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathcal{F}}(\overline{Y})\right]\overline{\Sigma}}\right]^{2}\right)\,\chi_{t}^{2}

\displaystyle\phantom{=}~+\frac{\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathcal{F}}(\overline{Y})^{2}\right]}{\delta}\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{\overline{\Sigma}^{2}}{(\gamma_{t}-\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathcal{F}}(\overline{Y})\right]\overline{\Sigma})^{2}}\right]\sigma_{V,t}^{2}

(A.39)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\delta}\Bigg(\frac{1}{\mathbb{E}\left[\overline{\Sigma}\right]}\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\frac{\delta}{\mathbb{E}\left[\overline{\Sigma}\right]}\overline{G}^{2}-1\right){\mathcal{F}}(\overline{Y})^{2}\right]\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{\overline{\Sigma}^{2}}{\gamma_{t}-\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathcal{F}}(\overline{Y})\right]\overline{\Sigma}}\right]^{2}

\displaystyle+\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathcal{F}}(\overline{Y})^{2}\right]\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{\overline{\Sigma}^{3}}{(\gamma_{t}-\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathcal{F}}(\overline{Y})\right]\overline{\Sigma})^{2}}\right]\Bigg)\,\chi_{t}^{2}+\frac{\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathcal{F}}(\overline{Y})^{2}\right]}{\delta}\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{\overline{\Sigma}^{2}}{(\gamma_{t}-\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathcal{F}}(\overline{Y})\right]\overline{\Sigma})^{2}}\right]\sigma_{V,t}^{2}.

(A.40)

Equation

A.39
is by
Equations

A.31
and
A.33
.

Furthermore, the right-hand side of
Equation

5.27
equals:

\displaystyle\phantom{=}~\lim_{d\to\infty}\frac{1}{d}\mathbb{E}\left[V_{t+1}^{\top}B_{t+1}^{\top}B_{t+1}V_{t+1}\right]

\displaystyle=\lim_{d\to\infty}\frac{1}{d}\mathbb{E}\left[(\chi_{t+1}\widetilde{\mathfrak{B}}^{*}+\sigma_{V,t+1}W_{V,t+1})^{\top}\Sigma(\gamma_{t+1}I_{d}-c\Sigma)^{-2}\Sigma(\chi_{t+1}\widetilde{\mathfrak{B}}^{*}+\sigma_{V,t+1}W_{V,t+1})\right]

\displaystyle=\chi_{t+1}^{2}\lim_{d\to\infty}\frac{1}{d}\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathfrak{B}^{*}}^{\top}\Sigma^{3/2}(\gamma_{t+1}I_{d}-c\Sigma)^{-2}\Sigma^{3/2}\mathfrak{B}^{*}\right]

\displaystyle\hskip 40.00006pt+\sigma_{V,t+1}^{2}\lim_{d\to\infty}\frac{1}{d}\mathbb{E}\left[W_{V,t+1}^{\top}\Sigma(\gamma_{t+1}I_{d}-c\Sigma)^{-2}\Sigma W_{V,t+1}\right]

\displaystyle=\chi_{t+1}^{2}\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{\overline{\Sigma}^{3}}{(\gamma_{t+1}-\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathcal{F}}(\overline{Y})\right]\overline{\Sigma})^{2}}\right]+\sigma_{V,t+1}^{2}\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{\overline{\Sigma}^{2}}{(\gamma_{t+1}-\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathcal{F}}(\overline{Y})\right]\overline{\Sigma})^{2}}\right].

We therefore obtain the following more transparent expression for
\gamma_{t+1}
(cf.
Equation

5.27
):

\displaystyle 1

\displaystyle=\chi_{t+1}^{2}\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{\overline{\Sigma}^{3}}{(\gamma_{t+1}-\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathcal{F}}(\overline{Y})\right]\overline{\Sigma})^{2}}\right]+\sigma_{V,t+1}^{2}\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{\overline{\Sigma}^{2}}{(\gamma_{t+1}-\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathcal{F}}(\overline{Y})\right]\overline{\Sigma})^{2}}\right],

(A.41)

where
\chi_{t+1},\sigma_{V,t+1}
are computed via
Equations

A.37
and
A.40
.
Again, using a similar monotonicity argument as that following
Equation

3.3
, we readily have that the solution to the above equation must exist in
(s(a^{*}),\infty)
and is unique (where we use
(b)
and
(c)
in
Equation

2.6
), and therefore
\gamma_{t+1}
is well-defined.

Next, we solve the fixed points of the above state evolution recursion.
Suppose the state evolution parameters
\mu_{t},\sigma_{U,t},\chi_{t+1},\sigma_{V,t+1},\gamma_{t+1}
converge to
\mu,\sigma_{U},\chi,\sigma_{V},\gamma
, respectively, as
t\to\infty
.
Then the latter quantities satisfy the following set of equations which are obtained by removing the time indices in
Equations

A.31
,
A.33
,
A.37
,
A.40
and
A.41
:

\displaystyle\mu

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\mathbb{E}\left[\overline{\Sigma}\right]}\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{\overline{\Sigma}^{2}}{\gamma-\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathcal{F}}(\overline{Y})\right]\overline{\Sigma}}\right]\chi,

(A.42)

\displaystyle\sigma_{U}^{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\delta}\left(\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{\overline{\Sigma}^{3}}{(\gamma-\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathcal{F}}(\overline{Y})\right]\overline{\Sigma})^{2}}\right]-\frac{1}{\mathbb{E}\left[\overline{\Sigma}\right]}\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{\overline{\Sigma}^{2}}{\gamma-\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathcal{F}}(\overline{Y})\right]\overline{\Sigma}}\right]^{2}\right)\,\chi^{2}+\frac{1}{\delta}\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{\overline{\Sigma}^{2}}{(\gamma-\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathcal{F}}(\overline{Y})\right]\overline{\Sigma})^{2}}\right]\sigma_{V}^{2},

(A.43)

\displaystyle\chi

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\mathbb{E}\left[\overline{\Sigma}\right]}\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\frac{\delta}{\mathbb{E}\left[\overline{\Sigma}\right]}\overline{G}^{2}-1\right){\mathcal{F}}(\overline{Y})\right]\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{\overline{\Sigma}^{2}}{\gamma-\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathcal{F}}(\overline{Y})\right]\overline{\Sigma}}\right]\chi,

(A.44)

\displaystyle\sigma_{V}^{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\delta}\Bigg(\frac{1}{\mathbb{E}\left[\overline{\Sigma}\right]}\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\frac{\delta}{\mathbb{E}\left[\overline{\Sigma}\right]}\overline{G}^{2}-1\right){\mathcal{F}}(\overline{Y})^{2}\right]\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{\overline{\Sigma}^{2}}{\gamma-\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathcal{F}}(\overline{Y})\right]\overline{\Sigma}}\right]^{2}

\displaystyle+\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathcal{F}}(\overline{Y})^{2}\right]\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{\overline{\Sigma}^{3}}{(\gamma-\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathcal{F}}(\overline{Y})\right]\overline{\Sigma})^{2}}\right]\Bigg)\,\chi^{2}+\frac{\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathcal{F}}(\overline{Y})^{2}\right]}{\delta}\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{\overline{\Sigma}^{2}}{(\gamma-\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathcal{F}}(\overline{Y})\right]\overline{\Sigma})^{2}}\right]\sigma_{V}^{2},

(A.45)

\displaystyle 1

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{\overline{\Sigma}^{3}}{(\gamma-\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathcal{F}}(\overline{Y})\right]\overline{\Sigma})^{2}}\right]\chi^{2}+\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{\overline{\Sigma}^{2}}{(\gamma-\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathcal{F}}(\overline{Y})\right]\overline{\Sigma})^{2}}\right]\sigma_{V}^{2}.

(A.46)

We observe from
Equation

A.44
that a trivial fixed point of
\chi
is
\chi=0
.
This implies, via
Equation

A.42
, that
\mu=0
.

Equations

A.45
and
A.46
then become

\displaystyle\sigma_{V}^{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathcal{F}}(\overline{Y})^{2}\right]}{\delta}\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{\overline{\Sigma}^{2}}{(\gamma-\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathcal{F}}(\overline{Y})\right]\overline{\Sigma})^{2}}\right]\sigma_{V}^{2},\quad 1=\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{\overline{\Sigma}^{2}}{(\gamma-\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathcal{F}}(\overline{Y})\right]\overline{\Sigma})^{2}}\right]\sigma_{V}^{2},

from which
\gamma
and
\sigma_{V}^{2}
can be solved.
Specifically,
\gamma
is the unique solution in
(s(a^{*}),\infty)
to:

\displaystyle 1

\displaystyle=\frac{\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathcal{F}}(\overline{Y})^{2}\right]}{\delta}\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{\overline{\Sigma}^{2}}{(\gamma-\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathcal{F}}(\overline{Y})\right]\overline{\Sigma})^{2}}\right],

and
\sigma_{V}^{2}
is given by

\displaystyle\sigma_{V}^{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{\overline{\Sigma}^{2}}{(\gamma-\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathcal{F}}(\overline{Y})\right]\overline{\Sigma})^{2}}\right]}.

Finally,
\sigma_{U}^{2}
can be solved using
Equation

A.43
:
\sigma_{U}^{2}=\frac{1}{\delta}
.

Now assume
\chi\neq 0
.

Equation

A.44
implies

\displaystyle 1

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\mathbb{E}\left[\overline{\Sigma}\right]}\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\frac{\delta}{\mathbb{E}\left[\overline{\Sigma}\right]}\overline{G}^{2}-1\right){\mathcal{F}}(\overline{Y})\right]\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{\overline{\Sigma}^{2}}{\gamma-\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathcal{F}}(\overline{Y})\right]\overline{\Sigma}}\right],

(A.47)

from which
\gamma
can be solved:
\gamma=\gamma^{*}
.
Recall that
\gamma^{*}
(together with
a^{*}
) is well-defined through
Equation

5.7
and
a^{*}
is taken to be the largest solution.

Given
\gamma
,
Equations

A.42
,
A.43
,
A.45
and
A.46

form a linear system with unknowns
\mu^{2},\sigma_{U}^{2},\chi^{2},\sigma_{V}^{2}
.
Combining
Equations

A.46
and
A.45
and using the definitions of
w_{1},w_{2},z_{1},z_{2}
in
Equations

3.9
,
3.10
and
5.31
, we obtain

\displaystyle\chi^{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{1-w_{2}}{(1-w_{2})z_{1}+w_{1}z_{2}},\quad\sigma_{V}^{2}=\frac{w_{1}}{(1-w_{2})z_{1}+w_{1}z_{2}}.

(A.48)

Note that the above solution is valid since
1-w_{2},w_{1},z_{1},z_{2}
are all positive, provided
a^{*}>a^{\circ}
(see
Item

3
in
Proposition

D.6
and
Proposition

G.1
).
According to
Equations

A.42
and
A.43
, this immediately implies

\displaystyle\mu^{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\mathbb{E}\left[\overline{\Sigma}\right]^{2}}\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{\overline{\Sigma}^{2}}{\gamma^{*}-\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathcal{F}}(\overline{Y})\right]\overline{\Sigma}}\right]^{2}\frac{1-w_{2}}{(1-w_{2})z_{1}+w_{1}z_{2}},

(A.49)

\displaystyle\sigma_{U}^{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\delta}\left(\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{\overline{\Sigma}^{3}}{(\gamma^{*}-\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathcal{F}}(\overline{Y})\right]\overline{\Sigma})^{2}}\right]-\frac{1}{\mathbb{E}\left[\overline{\Sigma}\right]}\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{\overline{\Sigma}^{2}}{\gamma^{*}-\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathcal{F}}(\overline{Y})\right]\overline{\Sigma}}\right]^{2}\right)\frac{1-w_{2}}{(1-w_{2})z_{1}+w_{1}z_{2}}

\displaystyle\phantom{=}~+\frac{1}{\delta}\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{\overline{\Sigma}^{2}}{(\gamma^{*}-\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathcal{F}}(\overline{Y})\right]\overline{\Sigma})^{2}}\right]\frac{w_{1}}{(1-w_{2})z_{1}+w_{1}z_{2}}

\displaystyle=\frac{1/\delta}{(1-w_{2})z_{1}+w_{1}z_{2}}\left(\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{\overline{\Sigma}^{3}}{(\gamma^{*}-\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathcal{F}}(\overline{Y})\right]\overline{\Sigma})^{2}}\right]-\frac{1}{\mathbb{E}\left[\overline{\Sigma}\right]}\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{\overline{\Sigma}^{2}}{\gamma^{*}-\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathcal{F}}(\overline{Y})\right]\overline{\Sigma}}\right]^{2}\right.

\displaystyle\phantom{=}~+\left.\frac{1}{\mathbb{E}\left[\overline{\Sigma}\right]^{2}}\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{\overline{\Sigma}^{2}}{(\gamma^{*}-\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathcal{F}}(\overline{Y})\right]\overline{\Sigma})^{2}}\right]\mathbb{E}\left[\overline{G}^{2}{\mathcal{F}}(\overline{Y})^{2}\right]\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{\overline{\Sigma}^{2}}{\gamma^{*}-\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathcal{F}}(\overline{Y})\right]\overline{\Sigma}}\right]^{2}\right),

(A.50)

where the last equality follows from the definitions of
w_{1},w_{2}
. This concludes the proof.

A.3
Proof of
Lemma

5.3

For each
t\geq 0
, the next value of
(\mu_{t+1},\sigma_{U,t+1},\chi_{t+2},\sigma_{V,t+2},\gamma_{t+2})
only depends on the current value of
(\mu_{t},\sigma_{U,t},\chi_{t+1},\sigma_{V,t+1},\gamma_{t+1})
. Hence, to show that the state evolution parameters do not change, it suffices to check that
(\mu_{0},\sigma_{U,0},\chi_{1},\sigma_{V,1},\gamma_{1})
coincides with the fixed point
(\mu,\sigma_{U},\chi,\sigma_{V},\gamma^{*})
.

By the construction of the AMP initializer
(\widetilde{u}^{-1},\widetilde{v}^{0})\in{\mathbb{R}}^{n}\times{\mathbb{R}}^{d}
, we have
\mu_{0}=\mu
(see
Equation

5.37
).
It is easy to verify that
\sigma_{U,0}
given by
Equation

5.37
coincides with
\sigma_{U}
derived in
Equation

A.50
.
Indeed,

\displaystyle\sigma_{U,0}^{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\delta}\left(1-\mathbb{E}\left[\overline{\Sigma}\right]\mu^{2}\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\delta}\left(1-\frac{1}{\mathbb{E}\left[\overline{\Sigma}\right]}\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{\overline{\Sigma}^{2}}{\gamma^{*}-\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathcal{F}}(\overline{Y})\right]\overline{\Sigma}}\right]^{2}\frac{1-w_{2}}{(1-w_{2})z_{1}+w_{1}z_{2}}\right)

(A.51)

\displaystyle=\frac{1/\delta}{(1-w_{2})z_{1}+w_{1}z_{2}}\left((1-w_{2})z_{1}+w_{1}z_{2}-\frac{1}{\mathbb{E}\left[\overline{\Sigma}\right]}\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{\overline{\Sigma}^{2}}{\gamma^{*}-\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathcal{F}}(\overline{Y})\right]\overline{\Sigma}}\right]^{2}(1-w_{2})\right)
