\displaystyle=\frac{1/\delta}{(1-w_{2})z_{1}+w_{1}z_{2}}\left(\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{\overline{\Sigma}^{3}}{(\gamma^{*}-\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathcal{F}}(\overline{Y})\right]\overline{\Sigma})^{2}}\right]-\frac{1}{\mathbb{E}\left[\overline{\Sigma}\right]}\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{\overline{\Sigma}^{2}}{\gamma^{*}-\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathcal{F}}(\overline{Y})\right]\overline{\Sigma}}\right]^{2}\right.

\displaystyle\phantom{=}~+\left.\frac{1}{\mathbb{E}\left[\overline{\Sigma}\right]^{2}}\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{\overline{\Sigma}^{2}}{(\gamma^{*}-\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathcal{F}}(\overline{Y})\right]\overline{\Sigma})^{2}}\right]\mathbb{E}\left[\overline{G}^{2}{\mathcal{F}}(\overline{Y})^{2}\right]\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{\overline{\Sigma}^{2}}{\gamma^{*}-\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathcal{F}}(\overline{Y})\right]\overline{\Sigma}}\right]^{2}\right)

(A.52)

\displaystyle=\sigma_{U}^{2}.

(A.53)

We use the expression of
\mu
(see
Equation

A.49
) in
Equation

A.51
and the expressions of
w_{1},w_{2},z_{1},z_{2}
(see
Equations

3.9
,
3.10
and
5.31
) in
Equation

A.52
.

We then verify
\chi_{1}=\chi
.
By
Equation

A.36
,

\displaystyle\chi_{1}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\frac{\delta}{\mathbb{E}\left[\overline{\Sigma}\right]}\overline{G}^{2}-1\right){\mathcal{F}}(\overline{Y})\right]\mu_{0}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\frac{\delta}{\mathbb{E}\left[\overline{\Sigma}\right]}\overline{G}^{2}-1\right){\mathcal{F}}(\overline{Y})\right]\frac{1}{\mathbb{E}\left[\overline{\Sigma}\right]}\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{\overline{\Sigma}^{2}}{\gamma^{*}-\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathcal{F}}(\overline{Y})\right]\overline{\Sigma}}\right]\sqrt{\frac{1-w_{2}}{(1-w_{2})z_{1}+w_{1}z_{2}}}.

Comparing the above expression with
\chi
in
Equation

A.48
, we see that it suffices to verify

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\frac{\delta}{\mathbb{E}\left[\overline{\Sigma}\right]}\overline{G}^{2}-1\right){\mathcal{F}}(\overline{Y})\right]\frac{1}{\mathbb{E}\left[\overline{\Sigma}\right]}\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{\overline{\Sigma}^{2}}{\gamma^{*}-\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathcal{F}}(\overline{Y})\right]\overline{\Sigma}}\right]

\displaystyle=1,

which is true since the fixed point
\gamma=\gamma^{*}
satisfies
Equation

A.47
.

Next, we show
\sigma_{V,1}=\sigma_{V}
.
Using
Equation

A.38
, we have

\displaystyle\sigma_{V,1}^{2}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[\overline{G}^{2}{\mathcal{F}}(\overline{Y})^{2}\right]\mu_{0}^{2}+\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathcal{F}}(\overline{Y})^{2}\right]\sigma_{U,0}^{2}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[\overline{G}^{2}{\mathcal{F}}(\overline{Y})^{2}\right]\mu^{2}+\frac{\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathcal{F}}(\overline{Y})^{2}\right]}{\delta}\left(1-\mathbb{E}\left[\overline{\Sigma}\right]\mu^{2}\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{\mathbb{E}\left[\overline{\Sigma}\right]}{\delta}\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\frac{\delta}{\mathbb{E}\left[\overline{\Sigma}\right]}\overline{G}^{2}-1\right){\mathcal{F}}(\overline{Y})^{2}\right]\mu^{2}+\frac{\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathcal{F}}(\overline{Y})^{2}\right]}{\delta}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\delta\mathbb{E}\left[\overline{\Sigma}\right]}\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\frac{\delta}{\mathbb{E}\left[\overline{\Sigma}\right]}\overline{G}^{2}-1\right){\mathcal{F}}(\overline{Y})^{2}\right]\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{\overline{\Sigma}^{2}}{\gamma^{*}-\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathcal{F}}(\overline{Y})\right]\overline{\Sigma}}\right]^{2}\frac{1-w_{2}}{(1-w_{2})z_{1}+w_{1}z_{2}}+\frac{\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathcal{F}}(\overline{Y})^{2}\right]}{\delta}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{(1-w_{2})z_{1}+w_{1}z_{2}}\left(\left(w_{1}-\frac{\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathcal{F}}(\overline{Y})^{2}\right]}{\delta}z_{1}\right)(1-w_{2})+\frac{\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathcal{F}}(\overline{Y})^{2}\right]}{\delta}((1-w_{2})z_{1}+w_{1}z_{2})\right)

(A.54)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{(1-w_{2})z_{1}+w_{1}z_{2}}\left(w_{1}-w_{1}w_{2}+\frac{\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathcal{F}}(\overline{Y})^{2}\right]}{\delta}w_{1}z_{2}\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{w_{1}}{(1-w_{2})z_{1}+w_{1}z_{2}}

(A.55)

\displaystyle=\sigma_{V}^{2}.

Equation

A.54
is by the definitions of
w_{1},z_{1}
.

Equation

A.55
is by the definitions of
w_{2},z_{2}
, in particular,
w_{2}=\frac{\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathcal{F}}(\overline{Y})^{2}\right]}{\delta}z_{2}
.

Finally, it remains to verify
\gamma_{1}=\gamma^{*}
.
By
Equation

A.41
,
\gamma_{1}
is the unique solution to

\displaystyle 1

\displaystyle=\chi_{1}^{2}\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{\overline{\Sigma}^{3}}{(\gamma_{1}-\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathcal{F}}(\overline{Y})\right]\overline{\Sigma})^{2}}\right]+\sigma_{V,1}^{2}\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{\overline{\Sigma}^{2}}{(\gamma_{1}-\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathcal{F}}(\overline{Y})\right]\overline{\Sigma})^{2}}\right]

\displaystyle=\chi^{2}\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{\overline{\Sigma}^{3}}{(\gamma_{1}-\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathcal{F}}(\overline{Y})\right]\overline{\Sigma})^{2}}\right]+\sigma_{V}^{2}\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{\overline{\Sigma}^{2}}{(\gamma_{1}-\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathcal{F}}(\overline{Y})\right]\overline{\Sigma})^{2}}\right]

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{(1-w_{2})z_{1}+w_{1}z_{2}}\left((1-w_{2})\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{\overline{\Sigma}^{3}}{(\gamma_{1}-\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathcal{F}}(\overline{Y})\right]\overline{\Sigma})^{2}}\right]+w_{1}\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{\overline{\Sigma}^{2}}{(\gamma_{1}-\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathcal{F}}(\overline{Y})\right]\overline{\Sigma})^{2}}\right]\right).

Rearranging terms, we have

\displaystyle 0

\displaystyle=(1-w_{2})\left(z_{1}-\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{\overline{\Sigma}^{3}}{(\gamma_{1}-\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathcal{F}}(\overline{Y})\right]\overline{\Sigma})^{2}}\right]\right)+w_{1}\left(z_{2}-\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{\overline{\Sigma}^{2}}{(\gamma_{1}-\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathcal{F}}(\overline{Y})\right]\overline{\Sigma})^{2}}\right]\right).

(A.56)

We argue that
\gamma_{1}
has to equal
\gamma^{*}
for the above equation to hold.
Note that both
(1-w_{2})
and
w_{1}
are strictly positive (provided
a^{*}>a^{\circ}
; see
Item

3
in
Proposition

D.6
and
Proposition

G.1
).
If
\gamma_{1}<\gamma^{*}
, then by the definitions of
z_{1},z_{2}
,

\displaystyle z_{1}

\displaystyle<\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{\overline{\Sigma}^{3}}{(\gamma_{1}-\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathcal{F}}(\overline{Y})\right]\overline{\Sigma})^{2}}\right],\quad z_{2}<\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{\overline{\Sigma}^{2}}{(\gamma_{1}-\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathcal{F}}(\overline{Y})\right]\overline{\Sigma})^{2}}\right],

and hence the right-hand side of
Equation

A.56
is strictly positive, which is a contradiction.
A similar contradiction can be derived if
\gamma_{1}>\gamma^{*}
.
Thus,
\gamma_{1}=\gamma^{*}
. This concludes the proof.

A.4
Proof of
Lemma

5.4

Lemma A.2
.

Consider the matrix
D
in
Equation

1.2
.
Define another matrix
\breve{D}
as

\displaystyle\breve{D}

\displaystyle=\Sigma^{1/2}\breve{X}^{\top}\breve{T}\breve{X}\Sigma^{1/2}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{d\times d},

where
\breve{T}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{(n-1)\times(n-1)}
is a diagonal matrix satisfying:

\displaystyle\lambda_{1}(T)

\displaystyle\geq\lambda_{1}(\breve{T})\geq\lambda_{2}(T)\geq\lambda_{2}(\breve{T})\geq\cdots\geq\lambda_{n-1}(T)\geq\lambda_{n-1}(\breve{T})\geq\lambda_{n}(T),

and
\breve{X}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{(n-1)\times d}
consists of i.i.d.
{\mathcal{N}}(0,1/n)
entries, independent of
\breve{T}
.
Then for every
n,d\geq 1
, it holds almost surely that

\displaystyle\lambda_{3}(\breve{D})\leq\lambda_{2}(D)

\displaystyle\leq\lambda_{1}(\breve{D}).

(A.57)

Proof.

Recall
g=\widetilde{X}\widetilde{\beta}^{*}
and

\displaystyle D

\displaystyle=\Sigma^{1/2}\widetilde{X}^{\top}\mathrm{diag}({\mathcal{T}}(q(\widetilde{X}\Sigma^{1/2}\beta^{*},\varepsilon)))\widetilde{X}\Sigma^{1/2}=\Sigma^{1/2}\widetilde{X}^{\top}\mathrm{diag}({\mathcal{T}}(q(\widetilde{X}\widetilde{\beta}^{*},\varepsilon)))\widetilde{X}\Sigma^{1/2}.

We can decompose
\widetilde{X}
into the sum of two pieces: one along the direction of
g
and the other perpendicular to
g
.
Furthermore, by isotropy of Gaussians (see
[
MW23
, Lemma 3.1]
,
[
WZ23
, Lemma 2.1]
), the distribution of
\widetilde{X}
remains unchanged if the perpendicular part is replaced with an i.i.d. copy.
Specifically,

\displaystyle\widetilde{X}

\displaystyle\overset{\mathrm{d}}{=}\Pi_{g}\widetilde{X}+\Pi_{g}^{\perp}\widehat{X},

where

\displaystyle\Pi_{g}

\displaystyle\coloneqq\frac{1}{\left\|g\right\|_{2}^{2}}gg^{\top},\quad\Pi_{g}^{\perp}\coloneqq I_{n}-\Pi_{g},

and
\widehat{X}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{n\times d}
is an i.i.d. copy of
\widetilde{X}
.
Using the variational representation of eigenvalues, we can bound the second eigenvalue of
D
by the first eigenvalue of a related matrix in which
T
and
\widetilde{X}
are “decoupled”.
Indeed,

\displaystyle\lambda_{2}(D)

\displaystyle=\min_{\begin{subarray}{c}{\mathcal{V}}\subset{\mathbb{R}}^{d}\\
\dim({\mathcal{V}})=d-1\end{subarray}}\max_{v\in{\mathcal{V}}\cap{\mathbb{S}}^{d-1}}v^{\top}\Sigma^{1/2}\widetilde{X}^{\top}T\widetilde{X}\Sigma^{1/2}v

(A.58)

\displaystyle\overset{\mathrm{d}}{=}\min_{\begin{subarray}{c}{\mathcal{V}}\subset{\mathbb{R}}^{d}\\
\dim({\mathcal{V}})=d-1\end{subarray}}\max_{v\in{\mathcal{V}}\cap{\mathbb{S}}^{d-1}}v^{\top}\Sigma^{1/2}\left(\Pi_{g}\widetilde{X}+\Pi_{g}^{\perp}\widehat{X}\right)^{\top}T\left(\Pi_{g}\widetilde{X}+\Pi_{g}^{\perp}\widehat{X}\right)\Sigma^{1/2}v

\displaystyle=\hskip-10.00002pt\min_{\begin{subarray}{c}{\mathcal{V}}\subset{\mathbb{R}}^{d}\\
\dim({\mathcal{V}})=d-1\end{subarray}}\hskip-10.00002pt\max_{v\in{\mathcal{V}}\cap{\mathbb{S}}^{d-1}}\hskip-5.0ptv^{\top}\left(\frac{\Sigma^{1/2}\widetilde{X}^{\top}g}{\left\|g\right\|_{2}}\frac{g^{\top}}{\left\|g\right\|_{2}}+\Sigma^{1/2}\widehat{X}^{\top}\Pi_{g}^{\perp}\right)T\left(\frac{g}{\left\|g\right\|_{2}}\frac{g^{\top}\widetilde{X}\Sigma^{1/2}}{\left\|g\right\|_{2}}+\Pi_{g}^{\perp}\widehat{X}\Sigma^{1/2}\right)v

\displaystyle\leq\hskip-10.00002pt\max_{\begin{subarray}{c}v\in{\mathbb{S}}^{d-1}\\
\left\langle v,\Sigma^{1/2}\widetilde{X}^{\top}g/\left\|g\right\|_{2}\right\rangle=0\end{subarray}}\hskip-10.00002ptv^{\top}\left(\frac{\Sigma^{1/2}\widetilde{X}^{\top}g}{\left\|g\right\|_{2}}\frac{g^{\top}}{\left\|g\right\|_{2}}+\Sigma^{1/2}\widehat{X}^{\top}\Pi_{g}^{\perp}\right)T\left(\frac{g}{\left\|g\right\|_{2}}\frac{g^{\top}\widetilde{X}\Sigma^{1/2}}{\left\|g\right\|_{2}}+\Pi_{g}^{\perp}\widehat{X}\Sigma^{1/2}\right)v

(A.59)

\displaystyle\leq\max_{v\in{\mathbb{S}}^{d-1}}v^{\top}\left(\Sigma^{1/2}\widehat{X}^{\top}\Pi_{g}^{\perp}\right)T\left(\Pi_{g}^{\perp}\widehat{X}\Sigma^{1/2}\right)v

\displaystyle=\lambda_{1}\left(\Sigma^{1/2}\widehat{X}^{\top}\Pi_{g}^{\perp}T\Pi_{g}^{\perp}\widehat{X}\Sigma^{1/2}\right).

In
Equation

A.58
and subsequent steps, the minimization is over all
(d-1)
-dimensional subspaces
{\mathcal{V}}\subset{\mathbb{R}}^{d}
.
In
Equation

A.59
, instead of minimizing over all
(d-1)
-dimensional subspaces, we take a particular one:

\displaystyle{\mathcal{V}}_{0}=\left\{v\in{\mathbb{R}}^{d}:\left\langle v,\frac{\Sigma^{1/2}\widetilde{X}^{\top}g}{\left\|g\right\|_{2}}\right\rangle=0\right\}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{d}

Writing the eigendecomposition of
\Pi_{g}^{\perp}
as
\Pi_{g}^{\perp}=Q(I_{n}-e_{n}e_{n}^{\top})Q^{\top}
for some
Q\in{\mathbb{O}}(n)
and using the left rotational invariance of
\widehat{X}
, we continue as follows:

\displaystyle\lambda_{1}(\Sigma^{1/2}\widehat{X}^{\top}\Pi_{g}^{\perp}T\Pi_{g}^{\perp}\widehat{X}\Sigma^{1/2})

\displaystyle=\lambda_{1}(\Sigma^{1/2}\widehat{X}^{\top}Q(I_{n}-e_{n}e_{n}^{\top})Q^{\top}TQ(I_{n}-e_{n}e_{n}^{\top})Q^{\top}\widehat{X}\Sigma^{1/2})

\displaystyle\overset{\mathrm{d}}{=}\lambda_{1}(\Sigma^{1/2}\widehat{X}^{\top}(I_{n}-e_{n}e_{n}^{\top})Q^{\top}TQ(I_{n}-e_{n}e_{n}^{\top})\widehat{X}\Sigma^{1/2})

\displaystyle=\lambda_{1}(\Sigma^{1/2}\widehat{X}^{\top}(I_{n}-e_{n}e_{n}^{\top})\widetilde{T}(I_{n}-e_{n}e_{n}^{\top})\widehat{X}\Sigma^{1/2}),

(A.60)

where in
Equation

A.60
we define
\widetilde{T}\coloneqq Q^{\top}TQ
.
Although
\widetilde{T}
is no longer diagonal, we note that it has the same eigenvalues as
T
, i.e.,
\{{\mathcal{T}}(y_{1}),\cdots,{\mathcal{T}}(y_{n})\}
.

For convenience of the proceeding calculations, let us write
\widehat{X}
and
\widetilde{T}
in block forms:

\displaystyle\widehat{X}

\displaystyle=\begin{bmatrix}\widehat{X}_{-n}\\
x_{n}^{\top}\end{bmatrix},\quad\widetilde{T}=\begin{bmatrix}\widetilde{T}_{-n}&s\\
s^{\top}&\widetilde{t}_{n}\end{bmatrix},

where
\widehat{X}_{-n}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{(n-1)\times d}
consist of the first
n-1
rows of
\widehat{X}
;
\widetilde{T}_{-n}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{(n-1)\times(n-1)}
is the top-left
(n-1)\times(n-1)
-submatrix of
\widetilde{T}
and
\widetilde{t}_{n}\in{\mathbb{R}}
is the bottom-right element of
\widetilde{T}
.
Note that by the Cauchy interlacing theorem, the eigenvalues of
\widetilde{T}
(i.e., the diagonal elements of
T
) are interlaced with those of
\widetilde{T}_{-n}
, i.e.,

\displaystyle\lambda_{1}(\widetilde{T})

\displaystyle\geq\lambda_{1}(\widetilde{T}_{-n})\geq\lambda_{2}(\widetilde{T})\geq\lambda_{2}(\widetilde{T}_{-n})\geq\cdots\geq\lambda_{n-1}(\widetilde{T})\geq\lambda_{n-1}(\widetilde{T}_{-n})\geq\lambda_{n}(\widetilde{T}).

(A.61)

Now, returning to bounding
\lambda_{2}(D)
:

\displaystyle\phantom{=}~\lambda_{1}(\Sigma^{1/2}\widehat{X}^{\top}(I_{n}-e_{n}e_{n}^{\top})\widetilde{T}(I_{n}-e_{n}e_{n}^{\top})\widehat{X}\Sigma^{1/2})

\displaystyle=\lambda_{1}\left(\Sigma^{1/2}\widehat{X}^{\top}\begin{bmatrix}\widetilde{T}_{-n}&0_{n-1}\\
0_{n-1}^{\top}&0\end{bmatrix}\widehat{X}\Sigma^{1/2}\right)

\displaystyle=\lambda_{1}\left(\Sigma^{1/2}\begin{bmatrix}\widehat{X}_{-n}^{\top}&x_{n}\end{bmatrix}\begin{bmatrix}\widetilde{T}_{-n}&0_{n-1}\\
0_{n-1}^{\top}&0\end{bmatrix}\begin{bmatrix}\widehat{X}_{-n}\\
x_{n}^{\top}\end{bmatrix}\Sigma^{1/2}\right)

\displaystyle=\lambda_{1}(\Sigma^{1/2}\widehat{X}_{-n}^{\top}\widetilde{T}_{-n}\widehat{X}_{-n}\Sigma^{1/2})

\displaystyle\overset{\mathrm{d}}{=}\lambda_{1}(\Sigma^{1/2}\widehat{X}_{-n}^{\top}\mathrm{diag}(\lambda_{1}(\widetilde{T}_{-n}),\cdots,\lambda_{n-1}(\widetilde{T}_{-n}))\widehat{X}_{-n}\Sigma^{1/2}).

The last step follows from the left rotational invariance of
\widehat{X}_{-n}
.
Denoting
\breve{X}\coloneqq\widehat{X}_{-n}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{(n-1)\times d}
and
\breve{T}\coloneqq\mathrm{diag}(\lambda_{1}(\widetilde{T}_{-n}),\cdots,\lambda_{n-1}(\widetilde{T}_{-n}))\in{\mathbb{R}}^{(n-1)\times(n-1)}
, we obtain the upper bound in
Equation

A.57
.

We then prove a lower bound on
\lambda_{2}(D)
, again using the Courant–Fischer theorem.
Recall

\displaystyle\lambda_{2}(D)

\displaystyle\overset{\mathrm{d}}{=}\hskip-10.00002pt\min_{\begin{subarray}{c}{\mathcal{V}}\subset{\mathbb{R}}^{d}\\
\dim({\mathcal{V}})=d-1\end{subarray}}\hskip-10.00002pt\max_{v\in{\mathcal{V}}\cap{\mathbb{S}}^{d-1}}\hskip-5.0ptv^{\top}\left(\frac{\Sigma^{1/2}\widetilde{X}^{\top}g}{\left\|g\right\|_{2}}\frac{g^{\top}}{\left\|g\right\|_{2}}+\Sigma^{1/2}\widehat{X}^{\top}\Pi_{g}^{\perp}\right)T\left(\frac{g}{\left\|g\right\|_{2}}\frac{g^{\top}\widetilde{X}\Sigma^{1/2}}{\left\|g\right\|_{2}}+\Pi_{g}^{\perp}\widehat{X}\Sigma^{1/2}\right)v.

Let
{\mathcal{V}}^{*}\subset{\mathbb{R}}^{d}
be a minimizer.
Since
\dim({\mathcal{V}}^{*})=d-1
, it can be written as
{\mathcal{V}}^{*}=\left\{v\in{\mathbb{R}}^{d}:\left\langle v,v^{*}\right\rangle=0\right\}
for a vector
v^{*}\in{\mathbb{S}}^{d-1}
.
We proceed as follows

\displaystyle\lambda_{2}(D)

\displaystyle\overset{\mathrm{d}}{=}\max_{\begin{subarray}{c}v\in{\mathbb{S}}^{d-1}\\
\left\langle v,v^{*}\right\rangle=0\end{subarray}}v^{\top}\left(\frac{\Sigma^{1/2}\widetilde{X}^{\top}g}{\left\|g\right\|_{2}}\frac{g^{\top}}{\left\|g\right\|_{2}}+\Sigma^{1/2}\widehat{X}^{\top}\Pi_{g}^{\perp}\right)T\left(\frac{g}{\left\|g\right\|_{2}}\frac{g^{\top}\widetilde{X}\Sigma^{1/2}}{\left\|g\right\|_{2}}+\Pi_{g}^{\perp}\widehat{X}\Sigma^{1/2}\right)v

\displaystyle\geq\hskip-15.00002pt\max_{\begin{subarray}{c}v\in{\mathbb{S}}^{d-1}\\
\left\langle v,v^{*}\right\rangle=0\\
\left\langle v,\Sigma^{1/2}\widetilde{X}^{\top}g/\left\|g\right\|_{2}\right\rangle=0\end{subarray}}\hskip-15.00002ptv^{\top}\left(\frac{\Sigma^{1/2}\widetilde{X}^{\top}g}{\left\|g\right\|_{2}}\frac{g^{\top}}{\left\|g\right\|_{2}}+\Sigma^{1/2}\widehat{X}^{\top}\Pi_{g}^{\perp}\right)T\left(\frac{g}{\left\|g\right\|_{2}}\frac{g^{\top}\widetilde{X}\Sigma^{1/2}}{\left\|g\right\|_{2}}+\Pi_{g}^{\perp}\widehat{X}\Sigma^{1/2}\right)v

\displaystyle=\max_{\begin{subarray}{c}v\in{\mathbb{S}}^{d-1}\\
\left\langle v,v^{*}\right\rangle=0\\
\left\langle v,\Sigma^{1/2}\widetilde{X}^{\top}g/\left\|g\right\|_{2}\right\rangle=0\end{subarray}}v^{\top}\left(\Sigma^{1/2}\widehat{X}^{\top}\Pi_{g}^{\perp}\right)T\left(\Pi_{g}^{\perp}\widehat{X}\Sigma^{1/2}\right)v

\displaystyle=\max_{v\in{\mathcal{U}}_{0}\cap{\mathbb{S}}^{d-1}}v^{\top}\left(\Sigma^{1/2}\widehat{X}^{\top}\Pi_{g}^{\perp}\right)T\left(\Pi_{g}^{\perp}\widehat{X}\Sigma^{1/2}\right)v

(A.62)

\displaystyle\geq\min_{\begin{subarray}{c}{\mathcal{U}}\subset{\mathbb{R}}^{d}\\
\dim({\mathcal{U}})=d-2\end{subarray}}\max_{v\in{\mathcal{U}}\cap{\mathbb{S}}^{d-1}}v^{\top}\left(\Sigma^{1/2}\widehat{X}^{\top}\Pi_{g}^{\perp}\right)T\left(\Pi_{g}^{\perp}\widehat{X}\Sigma^{1/2}\right)v

\displaystyle=\lambda_{3}\left(\Sigma^{1/2}\widehat{X}^{\top}\Pi_{g}^{\perp}T\Pi_{g}^{\perp}\widehat{X}\Sigma^{1/2}\right).

In
Equation

A.62
, we let

\displaystyle{\mathcal{U}}_{0}\coloneqq\left\{v\in{\mathbb{R}}^{d}:\left\langle v,v^{*}\right\rangle=\left\langle v,\frac{\Sigma^{1/2}\widetilde{X}^{\top}g}{\left\|g\right\|_{2}}\right\rangle=0\right\}\subset{\mathbb{R}}^{d}.

If
v^{*}
and
\Sigma^{1/2}\widetilde{X}^{\top}g/\left\|g\right\|_{2}
happen to be collinear, then introduce an additional constraint
\left\langle v,u\right\rangle=0
for an arbitrary vector
u\in{\mathbb{S}}^{d-1}
orthogonal to
v^{*}
and the ‘
=
’ in
Equation

A.62
becomes ‘
\geq
’.
Furthermore, we have
\dim({\mathcal{U}}_{0})=d-2
.

Finally, by the same reasoning as for the upper bound (in particular
Equation

A.61
),

\displaystyle\lambda_{3}(\Sigma^{1/2}\widehat{X}^{\top}\Pi_{g}^{\perp}T\Pi_{g}^{\perp}\widehat{X}\Sigma^{1/2})

\displaystyle\overset{\mathrm{d}}{=}\lambda_{3}(\Sigma^{1/2}\widehat{X}_{-n}^{\top}\mathrm{diag}(\lambda_{1}(T),\cdots,\lambda_{n-1}(T))\widehat{X}_{-n}\Sigma^{1/2}),

where
\widehat{X}_{-n}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{(n-1)\times d}
has i.i.d.
{\mathcal{N}}(0,1/n)
entries and is independent of everything else.
This concludes the proof of
Lemma

A.2
.
∎

Note that
Equation

A.61
in the above proof implies that
\breve{T}
has the same limiting spectral distribution as
T
which is in turn given by
\mathrm{law}({\mathcal{T}}(\overline{Y}))
.
Now the only difference between the bound in
Lemma

A.2
and the one in
Lemma

5.4
is that
n
in the latter is replaced with
n-1
in the former.
However, this is immaterial asymptotically as
n,d\to\infty
with
n/d\to\delta
.

To prove
Lemma

5.4
, it then remains to show that both the upper and lower bounds in
Lemma

A.2
converge to the same limit
\sup\supp(\overline{\mu}_{\widehat{D}})
.
It suffices to consider
\lambda_{1,3}(\widehat{D})
(instead of
\lambda_{1,3}(\breve{D})
).

Since the following result may be of independent interest, we isolate the required assumptions and state it in a self-contained manner.

(A4)

n,d\to\infty
with
n/d\to\delta
.

(A12)

\left\|\Sigma\right\|_{2}
and
\left\|T\right\|_{2}
are uniformly bounded over
n
.

(A13)

The empirical spectral distributions
\mu_{T}
and
\mu_{\Sigma}
of
T
and
\Sigma
converge respectively to
\overline{\mu}_{T}
and
\overline{\mu}_{\Sigma}
, with
\overline{\mu}_{T},\overline{\mu}_{\Sigma}\neq\delta_{0}
.
Furthermore, for all
\varsigma>0
there exists
n_{0}\in\mathbb{N}
such that whenever
n\geq n_{0}
we have

\displaystyle\supp\mu_{T}\subset\supp\overline{\mu}_{T}+[-\varsigma,\varsigma],\qquad\supp\mu_{\Sigma}\subset\supp\overline{\mu}_{\Sigma}+[-\varsigma,\varsigma].

(A.63)

(A14)

The support of
\overline{\mu}_{T}
intersects with
(0,\infty)
, i.e.,

\sup\supp\overline{\mu}_{T}>0.

(A.64)

The uniform boundedness of
\left\|\Sigma\right\|_{2}
has been assumed in
Item

(A2)
.
The uniform boundedness of
\left\|T\right\|_{2}
follows from the boundedness of
{\mathcal{T}}
in
Item

(A5)
.
In
Item

(A13)
, the convergence of
\mu_{T}=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\delta_{{\mathcal{T}}(q(\langle x_{i},\beta^{*}\rangle,\varepsilon_{i}))}
and the first part of
Equation

A.63
follows from the law of large numbers; the convergence of
\mu_{\Sigma}
has been assumed in
Item

(A2)
and the second part of
Equation

A.63
is the same as
Equation

2.1
.
Neither
\overline{\mu}_{T}
nor
\overline{\mu}_{\Sigma}
can be
\delta_{0}
since
{\mathcal{T}}
is not constantly
0
by
Equation

2.4
, and
\overline{\Sigma}
is strictly positive.

Item

(A14)
is implied by
\sup\limits_{y\in\supp(\overline{Y})}{\mathcal{T}}(y)>0
in
Item

(A5)
.

Lemma A.3

(
\lambda_{1}(\widehat{D})
converges to right edge,
[
FSW21
, Theorem 4.3]
)
.

Suppose that
Items

(A4)
,
(A12)
,
(A13)
and
(A14)
hold true.
Consider the matrix
\widehat{D}
in
Equation

5.38
and let
\mu_{\widehat{D}}
denote its empirical spectral distribution.
Then, almost surely,
\mu_{\widehat{D}}
converges to a deterministic probability measure
\overline{\mu}_{\widehat{D}}
on
\mathbb{R}
and

\lim_{d\to\infty}\lambda_{1}(\widehat{D})=\sup\supp(\overline{\mu}_{\widehat{D}}).

Lemma A.4

(
\lambda_{3}(\widehat{D})
converges to right edge)
.

Suppose that
Items

(A4)
,
(A12)
,
(A13)
and
(A14)
hold true.
Then

\displaystyle\lim_{d\to\infty}\lambda_{3}(\widehat{D})

\displaystyle=\sup\supp(\overline{\mu}_{\widehat{D}}),\quad\text{almost surely}.

Proof.

To derive the limit, we show a pair of matching upper and lower bounds.
Denote
\lambda^{\circ}=\sup\supp(\overline{\mu}_{\widehat{D}})
.
The upper bound is straightforward:

\displaystyle\lim_{d\to\infty}\lambda_{3}(\widehat{D})

\displaystyle\leq\lim_{d\to\infty}\lambda_{1}(\widehat{D})=\sup\supp(\overline{\mu}_{\widehat{D}}),

where the equality is by
Lemma

A.3
.

As for the lower bound, we would like to show: for any
\lambda<\lambda^{\circ}
,
\lim\limits_{d\to\infty}\lambda_{3}(\widehat{D})\geq\lambda
almost surely.
By the choice of
\lambda
, there exists a constant
c>0
such that
\overline{\mu}_{\widehat{D}}(\lambda,\infty)\geq 2c
.
Recall that by
[
Zha07
, Theorem 1.2.1]
, almost surely
\mu_{\widehat{D}}
weakly converges to
\overline{\mu}_{\widehat{D}}
.
Therefore, with probability
1
, for every sufficiently large
d
,
\mu_{\widehat{D}}(\lambda,\infty)\geq c\geq 3/d
.
This means

\displaystyle\frac{1}{d}\left|\left\{i\in\{1,\ldots,d\}:\lambda_{i}(\widehat{D})\geq\lambda\right\}\right|

\displaystyle\geq\frac{3}{d},

that is,
\lambda_{3}(\widehat{D})\geq\lambda
, which completes the proof of the lower bound and hence the lemma.
∎

A.5
Proof of
Equation

5.57

Recall from
Equations

5.51
and
5.47
the definition of
\widehat{e}^{t}
.
We will first provide a suite of auxiliary bounds on the spectral norms of various matrices in
Section

A.5.1
.
They will prove useful in the sequel.
We then show in
Section

A.5.2
that

\displaystyle\lim_{t\to\infty}\plim_{n\to\infty}\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\left\|e_{1}^{t}\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle=0,\quad\lim_{t\to\infty}\plim_{d\to\infty}\frac{1}{\sqrt{d}}\left\|e_{2}^{t}\right\|_{2}=0.

(A.65)

Next, using this, we show in
Section

A.5.3
that

\displaystyle\lim_{t\to\infty}\plim_{d\to\infty}\frac{1}{\sqrt{d}}\left\|\widehat{e}^{t}\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle=0.

(A.66)

Finally, in
Section

A.5.4
we prove
Equation

5.57
, i.e.,

\displaystyle\lim_{t^{\prime}\to\infty}\lim_{t\to\infty}\plim_{d\to\infty}\frac{1}{\sqrt{d}}\left\|\widehat{e}^{t,t^{\prime}}\right\|_{2}=0.

A.5.1
Bounding the norms of various matrices

We first recall the following elementary facts regarding the spectral norm, singular values and eigenvalues of a matrix.
For any matrix
K\in{\mathbb{R}}^{n\times d}
,

\displaystyle\left\|K\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle=\sigma_{1}(K)=\sqrt{\lambda_{1}(K^{\top}K)}=\sqrt{\lambda_{1}(KK^{\top})}.

If
K
is symmetric (
n=d
), this is further equal to

\displaystyle\left\|K\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle=\sqrt{\lambda_{1}(K^{2})}=\max\left\{|\lambda_{1}(K)|,|\lambda_{n}(K)|\right\}.

If
K
is PSD, then singular values coincide with eigenvalues and hence
\left\|K\right\|_{2}=\lambda_{1}(K)
.

Using these facts, we have

\displaystyle\lim_{d\to\infty}\left\|\Sigma\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle=\lim_{d\to\infty}\lambda_{1}(\Sigma)=\sup\supp(\overline{\Sigma})\eqqcolon C_{\Sigma},

(A.67)

\displaystyle\lim_{n\to\infty}\left\|T\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle=\lim_{d\to\infty}\max_{i}|{\mathcal{T}}(y_{i})|=\max\left\{\left|\inf\supp({\mathcal{T}}(\overline{Y}))\right|,\left|\sup\supp({\mathcal{T}}(\overline{Y}))\right|\right\}\eqqcolon C_{T},

(A.68)

\displaystyle\lim_{d\to\infty}\left\|\widetilde{X}\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle=\lim_{d\to\infty}\sqrt{\lambda_{1}(\widetilde{X}^{\top}\widetilde{X})}=1+1/\sqrt{\delta}\eqqcolon C_{\widetilde{X}},

(A.69)

where the last two lines hold almost surely.
Note that
C_{\Sigma}<\infty
since
\left\|\Sigma\right\|_{2}
is uniformly bounded (see
Item

(A2)
) and
C_{T}<\infty
since
{\mathcal{T}}
is bounded (see
Item

(A5)
).
The last line follows since
\widetilde{X}^{\top}\widetilde{X}
is a Wishart matrix and its top eigenvalue converges almost surely to the right edge
(1+1/\sqrt{\delta})^{2}
of the support of its limiting spectral distribution, the Marchenko–Pastur law
[
YBK88
]
.
Additionally, note that
\left\|\Sigma^{k}\right\|_{2}=C_{\Sigma}^{k}
for any
k\in{\mathbb{R}}
, since
\Sigma
is PSD. Using the sub-multiplicativity of matrix norms, we then have the following bound for
D
:

\displaystyle\lim_{d\to\infty}\left\|D\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle=\lim_{d\to\infty}\left\|\Sigma^{1/2}\widetilde{X}^{\top}T\widetilde{X}\Sigma^{1/2}\right\|_{2}\leq\lim_{d\to\infty}\left\|\Sigma^{1/2}\right\|_{2}^{2}\left\|\widetilde{X}\right\|_{2}^{2}\left\|T\right\|_{2}=C_{\Sigma}C_{\widetilde{X}}^{2}C_{T}\eqqcolon C_{D}.

(A.70)

Since
D
is a symmetric matrix,
\left\|D\right\|_{2}=\max\left\{|\lambda_{1}(D)|,|\lambda_{d}(D)|\right\}
and therefore for every sufficiently large
d
, it holds almost surely that

\displaystyle-(C_{D}+1)

\displaystyle\leq\lambda_{d}(D)\leq\lambda_{1}(D)\leq C_{D}+1.

(A.71)

The extra
+1
term is to exclude fluctuation when
d\leq d_{0}
for some constant
d_{0}
.

Recall that
a^{*}>\sup\supp({\mathcal{T}}(\overline{Y}))
and denote

\displaystyle\widecheck{C}_{T}

\displaystyle\coloneqq\left|\inf\supp({\mathcal{T}}(\overline{Y}))\right|,\quad\widehat{C}_{T}\coloneqq\sup\supp({\mathcal{T}}(\overline{Y}))>0.

Then, we have the following bound for
F
:

\displaystyle\lim_{n\to\infty}\left\|F\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle=\lim_{n\to\infty}\max_{i}\frac{\left|{\mathcal{T}}(y_{i})\right|}{a^{*}-{\mathcal{T}}(y_{i})}\leq\lim_{n\to\infty}\frac{\max_{i}\left|{\mathcal{T}}(y_{i})\right|}{a^{*}-\max_{i}{\mathcal{T}}(y_{i})}\leq\frac{C_{T}}{a^{*}-\widehat{C}_{T}}\eqqcolon C_{F}.

(A.72)

Recall

\displaystyle B

\displaystyle=\left(\gamma^{*}I_{d}-\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{{\mathcal{T}}(\overline{Y})}{a^{*}-{\mathcal{T}}(\overline{Y})}\right]\Sigma\right)^{-1}\Sigma,

and
\gamma^{*}>s(a^{*})
.
Therefore
\gamma^{*}I_{d}-\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{{\mathcal{T}}(\overline{Y})}{a^{*}-{\mathcal{T}}(\overline{Y})}\right]\Sigma
is positive definite.
We can then bound the spectral norm of
B
as follows:

\displaystyle\lim_{d\to\infty}\left\|B\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\lim_{d\to\infty}\left\|\gamma^{*}I_{d}-\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{{\mathcal{T}}(\overline{Y})}{a^{*}-{\mathcal{T}}(\overline{Y})}\right]\Sigma\right\|_{2}^{-1}\left\|\Sigma\right\|_{2}\leq\frac{C_{\Sigma}}{\gamma^{*}-s(a^{*})}\eqqcolon C_{B}.

(A.73)

Recalling
\widetilde{B}=\Sigma^{-1/2}B
and using
Equations

A.67
and
A.73
, we have

\displaystyle\lim_{d\to\infty}\left\|\widetilde{B}\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\lim_{d\to\infty}\left\|\Sigma\right\|_{2}^{-1/2}\left\|B\right\|_{2}\leq\frac{C_{B}}{\sqrt{\inf\supp(\overline{\Sigma})}}\eqqcolon C_{\widetilde{B}}.

(A.74)

Note that
C_{\widetilde{B}}<\infty
since
\overline{\Sigma}>0
(see
Item

(A2)
).
Recalling
\widehat{M}=\frac{D+\ell I_{d}}{\lambda_{1}+\ell}
and using
Equation

A.70
, we have

\displaystyle\lim_{d\to\infty}\left\|\widehat{M}\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\lim_{d\to\infty}\frac{\left\|D\right\|_{2}+\left|\ell\right|}{\left|\lambda_{1}+\ell\right|}\leq\frac{C_{D}+\left|\ell\right|}{\left|\lambda_{1}+\ell\right|}\eqqcolon C_{\widehat{M}}.

(A.75)

A.5.2
Bounding
e_{1}^{t},e_{2}^{t}

To prove
Equation

A.65
, or equivalently,

\displaystyle\lim_{t\to\infty}\plim_{n\to\infty}\frac{1}{n}\left\|e_{1}^{t}\right\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle=0,\quad\lim_{t\to\infty}\plim_{d\to\infty}\frac{1}{d}\left\|e_{2}^{t}\right\|_{2}^{2}=0,

we follow the proof strategy of
[
MTV21
, Lemma 5.3]
.
The idea is to express these quantities as state evolution parameters and show that they converge to the desired fixed points.
Writing

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n}\left\|e_{1}^{t}\right\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n}\left\|u^{t}-u^{t-1}\right\|_{2}^{2}=\frac{1}{n}\left\|u^{t}\right\|_{2}^{2}+\frac{1}{n}\left\|u^{t-1}\right\|_{2}^{2}-\frac{2}{n}\left\langle u^{t},u^{t-1}\right\rangle,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{d}\left\|e_{2}^{t}\right\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{d}\left\|v^{t+1}-v^{t}\right\|_{2}^{2}=\frac{1}{d}\left\|v^{t+1}\right\|_{2}^{2}+\frac{1}{d}\left\|v^{t}\right\|_{2}^{2}-\frac{2}{d}\left\langle v^{t+1},v^{t}\right\rangle,

and using the state evolution result in
Proposition

5.1
, we have

\displaystyle\plim_{n\to\infty}\frac{1}{n}\left\|e_{1}^{t}\right\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle=\lim_{n\to\infty}\frac{1}{n}\mathbb{E}\left[\left\langle U_{t},U_{t}\right\rangle\right]+\lim_{n\to\infty}\frac{1}{n}\mathbb{E}\left[\left\langle U_{t-1},U_{t-1}\right\rangle\right]-2\lim_{n\to\infty}\frac{1}{n}\mathbb{E}\left[\left\langle U_{t},U_{t-1}\right\rangle\right]

\displaystyle=\frac{\mathbb{E}\left[\overline{\Sigma}\right]}{\delta}\mu_{t}^{2}+\sigma_{U,t}^{2}+\frac{\mathbb{E}\left[\overline{\Sigma}\right]}{\delta}\mu_{t-1}^{2}+\sigma_{U,t-1}^{2}

\displaystyle\phantom{=}~-2\left(\frac{\mathbb{E}\left[\overline{\Sigma}\right]}{\delta}\mu_{t}\mu_{t-1}+\lim_{n\to\infty}\frac{1}{n}\mathbb{E}\left[\left\langle\sigma_{U,t}W_{U,t},\sigma_{U,t-1}W_{U,t-1}\right\rangle\right]\right),

and

\displaystyle\plim_{d\to\infty}\frac{1}{d}\left\|e_{2}^{t}\right\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle=\lim_{d\to\infty}\frac{1}{d}\mathbb{E}\left[\left\langle V_{t+1},V_{t+1}\right\rangle\right]+\lim_{d\to\infty}\frac{1}{d}\mathbb{E}\left[\left\langle V_{t},V_{t}\right\rangle\right]-2\lim_{d\to\infty}\frac{1}{d}\mathbb{E}\left[\left\langle V_{t+1},V_{t}\right\rangle\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[\overline{\Sigma}\right]\chi_{t+1}^{2}+\sigma_{V,t+1}^{2}+\mathbb{E}\left[\overline{\Sigma}\right]\chi_{t}^{2}+\sigma_{V,t}^{2}

\displaystyle\phantom{=}~-2\left(\mathbb{E}\left[\overline{\Sigma}\right]\chi_{t+1}\chi_{t}+\lim_{d\to\infty}\frac{1}{d}\mathbb{E}\left[\left\langle\sigma_{V,t+1}W_{V,t+1},\sigma_{V,t}W_{V,t}\right\rangle\right]\right).

By
Lemma

5.3
, the values of
\mu_{t},\sigma_{U,t},\chi_{t+1},\sigma_{V,t+1}
do not change with
t
and are equal to
\mu,\sigma_{U},\chi,\sigma_{V}
.
Therefore, to show
Equation

A.65
, it suffices to show

\displaystyle\lim_{t\to\infty}\lim_{n\to\infty}\frac{1}{n}\mathbb{E}\left[\left\langle\sigma_{U,t}W_{U,t},\sigma_{U,t-1}W_{U,t-1}\right\rangle\right]

\displaystyle=\sigma_{U}^{2},\quad\lim_{t\to\infty}\lim_{d\to\infty}\frac{1}{d}\mathbb{E}\left[\left\langle\sigma_{V,t+1}W_{V,t+1},\sigma_{V,t}W_{V,t}\right\rangle\right]=\sigma_{V}^{2}.

From the state evolution, we have

\displaystyle(\Phi_{t})_{t+1,t}

\displaystyle=\lim_{n\to\infty}\frac{1}{n}\mathbb{E}\left[\left\langle\sigma_{U,t}W_{U,t},\sigma_{U,t-1}W_{U,t-1}\right\rangle\right]

\displaystyle=\lim_{n\to\infty}\frac{1}{n}\mathbb{E}\left[\left\langle f_{t}(V_{t})-\mu_{t}\widetilde{\mathfrak{B}}^{*},f_{t-1}(V_{t-1})-\mu_{t-1}\widetilde{\mathfrak{B}}^{*}\right\rangle\right]

\displaystyle=\lim_{n\to\infty}\frac{1}{n}\mathbb{E}\left[\left\langle f_{t}(V_{t}),f_{t-1}(V_{t-1})\right\rangle\right]-\mu_{t}\lim_{n\to\infty}\frac{1}{n}\mathbb{E}\left[\left\langle f_{t-1}(V_{t-1}),\widetilde{\mathfrak{B}}^{*}\right\rangle\right]

\displaystyle\phantom{=}~-\mu_{t-1}\lim_{n\to\infty}\frac{1}{n}\mathbb{E}\left[\left\langle f_{t}(V_{t}),\widetilde{\mathfrak{B}}^{*}\right\rangle\right]+\mu_{t}\mu_{t-1}\lim_{n\to\infty}\frac{1}{n}\mathbb{E}\left[\left\langle\widetilde{\mathfrak{B}}^{*},\widetilde{\mathfrak{B}}^{*}\right\rangle\right]

\displaystyle=\lim_{n\to\infty}\frac{1}{n}\mathbb{E}\left[\left\langle f_{t}(V_{t}),f_{t-1}(V_{t-1})\right\rangle\right]-\frac{\mathbb{E}\left[\overline{\Sigma}\right]}{\delta}\mu_{t}\mu_{t-1},

(A.76)

where the last equality is by
Equation

5.17
;
and

\displaystyle(\Psi_{t})_{t+1,t}

\displaystyle=\lim_{d\to\infty}\frac{1}{d}\mathbb{E}\left[\left\langle\sigma_{V,t+1}W_{V,t+1},\sigma_{V,t}W_{V,t}\right\rangle\right]=\lim_{n\to\infty}\frac{1}{n}\mathbb{E}\left[\left\langle g_{t}(U_{t};Y),g_{t-1}(U_{t-1};Y)\right\rangle\right].

(A.77)

Recall from
Equation

5.26
that
g_{t}(U_{t};Y)=FU_{t}
and
f_{t+1}(V_{t+1})=B_{t+1}V_{t+1}
.
Therefore we have

\displaystyle\phantom{=}~\lim_{n\to\infty}\frac{1}{n}\mathbb{E}\left[\left\langle f_{t}(V_{t}),f_{t-1}(V_{t-1})\right\rangle\right]

\displaystyle=\lim_{n\to\infty}\frac{1}{n}\mathbb{E}\left[(\chi_{t}\widetilde{\mathfrak{B}}^{*}+\sigma_{V,t}W_{V,t})^{\top}B_{t}^{\top}B_{t-1}(\chi_{t-1}\widetilde{\mathfrak{B}}^{*}+\sigma_{V,t-1}W_{V,t-1})\right]

\displaystyle=\chi_{t}\chi_{t-1}\lim_{n\to\infty}\frac{1}{n}\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathfrak{B}^{*}}^{\top}\Sigma^{1/2}B_{t}^{\top}B_{t-1}\Sigma^{1/2}\mathfrak{B}^{*}\right]+\lim_{n\to\infty}\frac{1}{n}\mathbb{E}\left[(\sigma_{V,t}W_{V,t})^{\top}B_{t}^{\top}B_{t-1}(\sigma_{V,t-1}W_{V,t-1})\right]

\displaystyle=\chi_{t}\chi_{t-1}\frac{1}{\delta}\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{\overline{\Sigma}^{3}}{(\gamma_{t}-c\overline{\Sigma})(\gamma_{t-1}-c\overline{\Sigma})}\right]+\frac{1}{\delta}\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{\overline{\Sigma}^{2}}{(\gamma_{t}-c\overline{\Sigma})(\gamma_{t-1}-c\overline{\Sigma})}\right]\lim_{d\to\infty}\frac{1}{d}\mathbb{E}\left[\left\langle\sigma_{V,t}W_{V,t},\sigma_{V,t-1}W_{V,t-1}\right\rangle\right],

(A.78)

where we use
Proposition

G.3
in the last step. Similarly, we have

\displaystyle\phantom{=}~\lim_{n\to\infty}\frac{1}{n}\mathbb{E}\left[\left\langle g_{t}(U_{t};Y),g_{t-1}(U_{t-1};Y)\right\rangle\right]

\displaystyle=\lim_{n\to\infty}\frac{1}{n}\mathbb{E}\left[(\mu_{t}G+\sigma_{U,t}W_{U,t})^{\top}F^{2}(\mu_{t-1}G+\sigma_{U,t-1}W_{U,t-1})\right]

\displaystyle=\mu_{t}\mu_{t-1}\lim_{n\to\infty}\frac{1}{n}\mathbb{E}\left[G^{\top}F^{2}G\right]+\lim_{n\to\infty}\frac{1}{n}\mathbb{E}\left[(\sigma_{U,t}W_{U,t})^{\top}F^{2}(\sigma_{U,t-1}W_{U,t-1})\right]

\displaystyle=\mu_{t}\mu_{t-1}\mathbb{E}\left[\overline{G}^{2}{\mathcal{F}}(\overline{Y})^{2}\right]+\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathcal{F}}(\overline{Y})^{2}\right]\lim_{n\to\infty}\frac{1}{n}\mathbb{E}\left[\left\langle\sigma_{U,t}W_{U,t},\sigma_{U,t-1}W_{U,t-1}\right\rangle\right].

(A.79)

Letting

\displaystyle\tau_{t}

\displaystyle\coloneqq\lim_{n\to\infty}\frac{1}{n}\mathbb{E}\left[\left\langle\sigma_{U,t}W_{U,t},\sigma_{U,t-1}W_{U,t-1}\right\rangle\right],\quad\omega_{t}\coloneqq\lim_{d\to\infty}\frac{1}{d}\mathbb{E}\left[\left\langle\sigma_{V,t}W_{V,t},\sigma_{V,t-1}W_{V,t-1}\right\rangle\right]

and using
Equations

A.78
and
A.79
in
Equations

A.76
and
A.77
,
we obtain a pair of recursions for
\tau_{t},\omega_{t}
:

\displaystyle\tau_{t}

\displaystyle=\chi_{t}\chi_{t-1}\frac{1}{\delta}\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{\overline{\Sigma}^{3}}{(\gamma_{t}-c\overline{\Sigma})(\gamma_{t-1}-c\overline{\Sigma})}\right]-\frac{\mathbb{E}\left[\overline{\Sigma}\right]}{\delta}\mu_{t}\mu_{t-1}+\frac{1}{\delta}\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{\overline{\Sigma}^{2}}{(\gamma_{t}-c\overline{\Sigma})(\gamma_{t-1}-c\overline{\Sigma})}\right]\omega_{t},

(A.80)

\displaystyle\omega_{t+1}

\displaystyle=\mu_{t}\mu_{t-1}\mathbb{E}\left[\overline{G}^{2}{\mathcal{F}}(\overline{Y})^{2}\right]+\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathcal{F}}(\overline{Y})^{2}\right]\tau_{t}.

(A.81)

Using
Equation

A.80
in
Equation

A.81
, we further obtain

\displaystyle\omega_{t+1}

\displaystyle=\frac{\mathbb{E}\left[\overline{\Sigma}\right]}{\delta}\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\frac{\delta}{\mathbb{E}\left[\overline{\Sigma}\right]}\overline{G}^{2}-1\right){\mathcal{F}}(\overline{Y})^{2}\right]\mu_{t}\mu_{t-1}

\displaystyle+\chi_{t}\chi_{t-1}\frac{\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathcal{F}}(\overline{Y})^{2}\right]}{\delta}\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{\overline{\Sigma}^{3}}{(\gamma_{t}-c\overline{\Sigma})(\gamma_{t-1}-c\overline{\Sigma})}\right]+\frac{\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathcal{F}}(\overline{Y})^{2}\right]}{\delta}\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{\overline{\Sigma}^{2}}{(\gamma_{t}-c\overline{\Sigma})(\gamma_{t-1}-c\overline{\Sigma})}\right]\omega_{t}.

We would like to show

\displaystyle\lim_{t\to\infty}\omega_{t+1}

\displaystyle=\sigma_{V}^{2}.

(A.82)

To this end, we will upper bound the
\limsup
and lower bound the
\liminf
both by
\sigma_{V}^{2}
. Let

\displaystyle p_{t}

\displaystyle\coloneqq\frac{\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathcal{F}}(\overline{Y})^{2}\right]}{\delta}\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{\overline{\Sigma}^{2}}{(\gamma_{t}-c\overline{\Sigma})(\gamma_{t-1}-c\overline{\Sigma})}\right],

\displaystyle q_{t}

\displaystyle\coloneqq\frac{\mathbb{E}\left[\overline{\Sigma}\right]}{\delta}\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\frac{\delta}{\mathbb{E}\left[\overline{\Sigma}\right]}\overline{G}^{2}-1\right){\mathcal{F}}(\overline{Y})^{2}\right]\mu_{t}\mu_{t-1}+\chi_{t}\chi_{t-1}\frac{\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathcal{F}}(\overline{Y})^{2}\right]}{\delta}\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{\overline{\Sigma}^{3}}{(\gamma_{t}-c\overline{\Sigma})(\gamma_{t-1}-c\overline{\Sigma})}\right],

and

\displaystyle\underline{\omega}

\displaystyle=\liminf_{t\to\infty}\omega_{t+1},\quad\overline{\omega}=\limsup_{t\to\infty}\omega_{t+1}.

Then by subadditivity of
\limsup
,

\displaystyle\overline{\omega}

\displaystyle=\limsup_{t\to\infty}q_{t}+p_{t}\omega_{t}

\displaystyle\leq\lim_{t\to\infty}q_{t}+\left(\lim_{t\to\infty}p_{t}\right)\left(\limsup_{t\to\infty}\omega_{t}\right)=\frac{\mathbb{E}\left[\overline{\Sigma}\right]}{\delta}\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\frac{\delta}{\mathbb{E}\left[\overline{\Sigma}\right]}\overline{G}^{2}-1\right){\mathcal{F}}(\overline{Y})^{2}\right]\mu^{2}

\displaystyle\hskip 50.00008pt+\frac{\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathcal{F}}(\overline{Y})^{2}\right]}{\delta}\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{\overline{\Sigma}^{3}}{(\gamma^{*}-c\overline{\Sigma})^{2}}\right]\chi^{2}+\frac{\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathcal{F}}(\overline{Y})^{2}\right]}{\delta}\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{\overline{\Sigma}^{2}}{(\gamma^{*}-c\overline{\Sigma})^{2}}\right]\overline{\omega},

where the inequality holds since
\lim\limits_{t\to\infty}p_{t}\geq 0
.
Rearranging terms on both sides gives

\displaystyle\overline{\omega}

\displaystyle\leq\left(1-\frac{\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathcal{F}}(\overline{Y})^{2}\right]}{\delta}\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{\overline{\Sigma}^{2}}{(\gamma^{*}-c\overline{\Sigma})^{2}}\right]\right)^{-1}\Bigg(\frac{\mathbb{E}\left[\overline{\Sigma}\right]}{\delta}\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\frac{\delta}{\mathbb{E}\left[\overline{\Sigma}\right]}\overline{G}^{2}-1\right){\mathcal{F}}(\overline{Y})^{2}\right]\mu^{2}

\displaystyle\hskip 180.00027pt+\frac{\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathcal{F}}(\overline{Y})^{2}\right]}{\delta}\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{\overline{\Sigma}^{3}}{(\gamma^{*}-c\overline{\Sigma})^{2}}\right]\chi^{2}\Bigg).

Note that the term in the first parentheses is positive since it is nothing but
1-w_{2}
which is positive whenever
a^{*}>a^{\circ}
.
We claim that the right-hand side is equal to
\sigma_{V}^{2}
.
This can be seen from the fixed point equations of the state evolution recursion.
Indeed, from
Equations

A.38
and
A.32
, we have the following identity for
\sigma_{V}^{2}
:

\displaystyle\sigma_{V}^{2}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[\overline{G}^{2}{\mathcal{F}}(\overline{Y})^{2}\right]\mu^{2}+\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathcal{F}}(\overline{Y})^{2}\right]\sigma_{U}^{2}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[\overline{G}^{2}{\mathcal{F}}(\overline{Y})^{2}\right]\mu^{2}+\frac{\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathcal{F}}(\overline{Y})^{2}\right]}{\delta}\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{\overline{\Sigma}^{3}}{(\gamma^{*}-\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathcal{F}}(\overline{Y})\right]\overline{\Sigma})^{2}}\right]\chi^{2}

\displaystyle\phantom{=}~+\frac{\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathcal{F}}(\overline{Y})^{2}\right]}{\delta}\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{\overline{\Sigma}^{2}}{(\gamma^{*}-\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathcal{F}}(\overline{Y})\right]\overline{\Sigma})^{2}}\right]\sigma_{V}^{2}-\frac{\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathcal{F}}(\overline{Y})^{2}\right]}{\delta}\mathbb{E}\left[\overline{\Sigma}\right]\mu^{2}.

(A.83)

Solving for
\sigma_{V}^{2}
, we obtain exactly the upper bound on
\overline{\omega}
.

Analogously, a lower bound on
\underline{\omega}
can be derived using superadditivity of
\liminf
:

\displaystyle\underline{\omega}

\displaystyle=\liminf_{t\to\infty}q_{t}+p_{t}\omega_{t}

\displaystyle\geq\lim_{t\to\infty}q_{t}+\left(\lim_{t\to\infty}p_{t}\right)\left(\liminf_{t\to\infty}\omega_{t}\right)=\frac{\mathbb{E}\left[\overline{\Sigma}\right]}{\delta}\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\frac{\delta}{\mathbb{E}\left[\overline{\Sigma}\right]}\overline{G}^{2}-1\right){\mathcal{F}}(\overline{Y})^{2}\right]\mu^{2}

\displaystyle\hskip 40.00006pt+\frac{\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathcal{F}}(\overline{Y})^{2}\right]}{\delta}\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{\overline{\Sigma}^{3}}{(\gamma^{*}-c\overline{\Sigma})^{2}}\right]\chi^{2}+\frac{\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathcal{F}}(\overline{Y})^{2}\right]}{\delta}\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{\overline{\Sigma}^{2}}{(\gamma^{*}-c\overline{\Sigma})^{2}}\right]\underline{\omega}.

Rearranging and using
Equation

A.83
gives
\underline{\omega}\geq\sigma_{V}^{2}
.
This establishes
Equation

A.82
.

Next, using
Equation

A.82
in
Equation

A.80
, we get

\displaystyle\lim_{t\to\infty}\tau_{t}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\delta}\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{\overline{\Sigma}^{3}}{(\gamma^{*}-c\overline{\Sigma})^{2}}\right]\chi^{2}-\frac{\mathbb{E}\left[\overline{\Sigma}\right]}{\delta}\mu^{2}+\frac{1}{\delta}\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{\overline{\Sigma}^{2}}{(\gamma^{*}-c\overline{\Sigma})^{2}}\right]\sigma_{V}^{2}.

By
Equation

A.32
, the right-hand side is precisely
\sigma_{U}^{2}
.
Therefore, we conclude

\displaystyle\lim_{t\to\infty}\tau_{t}

\displaystyle=\sigma_{U}^{2},

which, together with
Equation

A.82
, completes the proof of
Equation

A.65
.

A.5.3
Bounding
\widehat{e}^{t}

Let us now prove
Equation

A.66
.
Recall from
Equations

5.51
and
5.47
that
\widehat{e}^{t}
comprises the following terms:

\displaystyle\widehat{e}^{t}

\displaystyle=\widehat{e}^{t}_{1}+\widehat{e}^{t}_{2}+\widehat{e}^{t}_{3}+\widehat{e}^{t}_{4}+\widehat{e}^{t}_{5}+\widehat{e}^{t}_{6},

where

\displaystyle\widehat{e}^{t}_{1}

\displaystyle=\frac{\ell}{\lambda_{1}+\ell}\widetilde{B}e_{2}^{t}+\frac{a^{*}c}{\lambda_{1}+\ell}\Sigma^{1/2}Be_{2}^{t},

\displaystyle\widehat{e}^{t}_{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{a^{*}}{\lambda_{1}+\ell}(b-b_{t})\Sigma^{1/2}\widetilde{X}^{\top}F(b_{t}F+I_{n})^{-1}(bF+I_{n})^{-1}\widetilde{X}B_{t}v^{t},

\displaystyle\widehat{e}^{t}_{3}

\displaystyle=\frac{a^{*}}{\lambda_{1}+\ell}(\gamma^{*}-\gamma_{t})\Sigma^{1/2}\widetilde{X}^{\top}F(bF+I_{n})^{-1}\widetilde{X}(\gamma_{t}I_{d}-c\Sigma)^{-1}(\gamma^{*}I_{d}-c\Sigma)^{-1}\Sigma v^{t},

\displaystyle\widetilde{e}^{t}_{4}

\displaystyle\coloneqq\frac{a^{*}(c_{t}-c)}{\lambda_{1}+\ell}B_{t}v^{t},

\displaystyle\widehat{e}^{t}_{5}

\displaystyle=\frac{a^{*}c}{\lambda_{1}+\ell}(\gamma_{t}-\gamma^{*})\Sigma^{1/2}(\gamma_{t}I_{d}-c\Sigma)^{-1}(\gamma^{*}I_{d}-c\Sigma)^{-1}\Sigma v^{t},

\displaystyle\widehat{e}^{t}_{6}

\displaystyle=\frac{a^{*}b_{t}}{\lambda_{1}+\ell}\Sigma^{1/2}\widetilde{X}^{\top}F^{2}(b_{t}F+I_{n})^{-1}e_{1}^{t}.

Since the AMP is initialized so that the state evolution parameters stay fixed (see
Lemma

5.3
), for every
t\geq 1
,
\gamma_{t}=\gamma^{*}
and we immediately get

\displaystyle\widehat{e}_{3}^{t}=\widehat{e}_{5}^{t}=0_{d}.

(A.84)

By convergence of the empirical spectral distribution of
\Sigma
(see
Item

(A2)
), for every
t\geq 1
,

\displaystyle\lim_{d\to\infty}b_{t}

\displaystyle=\lim_{d\to\infty}\frac{d}{n}\tr((\gamma_{t}I_{d}-c\Sigma)^{-1}\Sigma)=\frac{1}{\delta}\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{\overline{\Sigma}}{\gamma_{t}-c\overline{\Sigma}}\right]=b,

and consequently

\displaystyle\plim_{d\to\infty}\frac{1}{\sqrt{d}}\left\|\widehat{e}^{t}_{2}\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle=0.

(A.85)

By convergence of the noise sequence
\varepsilon=(\varepsilon_{1},\cdots,\varepsilon_{n})
(see
Item

(A3)
) and independence of covariate vectors
(x_{1},\cdots,x_{n})
(see
Item

(A2)
),

\displaystyle\plim_{n\to\infty}c_{t}

\displaystyle=\plim_{n\to\infty}\frac{1}{n}\tr(F)=\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathcal{F}}(\overline{Y})\right]=c,

and consequently,

\displaystyle\plim_{d\to\infty}\frac{1}{\sqrt{d}}\left\|\widehat{e}^{t}_{4}\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle=0.

(A.86)

We use the bounds developed in the previous sections to bound
\widehat{e}_{1}^{t}
and
\widehat{e}_{6}^{t}
.
Specifically,

\displaystyle\lim_{t\to\infty}\plim_{d\to\infty}\frac{1}{\sqrt{d}}\left\|\widehat{e}_{1}^{t}\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\lim_{t\to\infty}\plim_{d\to\infty}\left|\frac{\ell}{\lambda_{1}+\ell}\right|\left\|\widetilde{B}\right\|_{2}\frac{\left\|e_{2}^{t}\right\|_{2}}{\sqrt{d}}+\left|\frac{a^{*}c}{\lambda_{1}+\ell}\right|\left\|\Sigma\right\|_{2}^{1/2}\left\|B\right\|_{2}\frac{\left\|e_{2}^{t}\right\|_{2}}{\sqrt{d}}

\displaystyle\leq\left(\left|\frac{\ell}{\lambda_{1}+\ell}\right|C_{\widetilde{B}}+\left|\frac{a^{*}c}{\lambda_{1}+\ell}\right|\sqrt{C_{\Sigma}}C_{B}\right)\lim_{t\to\infty}\plim_{d\to\infty}\frac{\left\|e_{2}^{t}\right\|_{2}}{\sqrt{d}}=0,

(A.87)

\displaystyle\lim_{t\to\infty}\plim_{d\to\infty}\frac{1}{\sqrt{d}}\left\|\widehat{e}_{6}^{t}\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\lim_{t\to\infty}\plim_{d\to\infty}\left|\frac{a^{*}b_{t}}{\lambda_{1}+\ell}\right|\left\|\Sigma\right\|_{2}^{1/2}\left\|\widetilde{X}\right\|_{2}\left\|F\right\|_{2}^{2}\left\|(b_{t}F+I_{n})^{-1}\right\|_{2}\frac{\left\|e_{1}^{t}\right\|_{2}}{\sqrt{d}}

\displaystyle=\lim_{t\to\infty}\plim_{d\to\infty}\left|\frac{a^{*}b_{t}}{\lambda_{1}+\ell}\right|\left\|\Sigma\right\|_{2}^{1/2}\left\|\widetilde{X}\right\|_{2}\left\|F\right\|_{2}^{2}\left\|I_{n}-\frac{T}{a^{*}}\right\|_{2}\frac{\left\|e_{1}^{t}\right\|_{2}}{\sqrt{d}}

(A.88)

\displaystyle=\frac{\sqrt{C_{\Sigma}}C_{\widetilde{X}}C_{F}^{2}(a^{*}-\widecheck{C}_{T})}{\left|\lambda_{1}+\ell\right|}\lim_{t\to\infty}\plim_{d\to\infty}\frac{\left\|e_{1}^{t}\right\|_{2}}{\sqrt{d}}=0.

(A.89)

To obtain
Equation

A.88
, it is useful to recall
F=T(a^{*}I_{n}-T)^{-1}
(see
Equation

5.25
) and observe from
Equations

5.29
and
5.7
that
b_{t}=1
for every
t\geq 1
(where we use
\gamma_{t}=\gamma^{*}
for every
t\geq 1
from
Lemma

5.3
).

Combining
Equations

A.84
,
A.87
,
A.89
,
A.85
and
A.86
yields
Equation

A.66
, as required.

A.5.4
Bounding
\widehat{e}^{t,t^{\prime}}

Finally, we prove
Equation

5.57
.
Recalling the definition of
\widehat{e}^{t,t^{\prime}}
in
Equation

5.54
and using the triangle inequality and the sub-multiplicativity of norms, we have

\displaystyle\lim_{t^{\prime}\to\infty}\lim_{t\to\infty}\plim_{d\to\infty}\frac{1}{\sqrt{d}}\left\|\widehat{e}^{t,t^{\prime}}\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\lim_{t^{\prime}\to\infty}\lim_{t\to\infty}\plim_{d\to\infty}\frac{1}{\sqrt{d}}\sum_{s=1}^{t^{\prime}}\left\|\widehat{M}\right\|_{2}^{t^{\prime}-s}\left\|\widehat{e}^{t+s-1}\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle=\lim_{t^{\prime}\to\infty}\lim_{t\to\infty}\sum_{s=1}^{t^{\prime}}\left(\lim_{d\to\infty}\left\|\widehat{M}\right\|_{2}^{t^{\prime}-s}\right)\left(\plim_{d\to\infty}\frac{1}{\sqrt{d}}\left\|\widehat{e}^{t+s-1}\right\|_{2}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\lim_{t^{\prime}\to\infty}\sum_{s=1}^{t^{\prime}}C_{\widehat{M}}^{t^{\prime}-s}\left(\lim_{t\to\infty}\plim_{d\to\infty}\frac{1}{\sqrt{d}}\left\|\widehat{e}^{t+s-1}\right\|_{2}\right)=0,

which implies
Equation

5.57
.
The inequality in the penultimate line is by
Equation

A.75
and the last equality is by
Equation

A.66
.

Appendix B
Proof of Theorem
3.2

We first prove
Item

2
of
Theorem

3.2
.
Suppose that the condition
a^{*}>a^{\circ}
holds for some
{\mathcal{T}}\in{\mathscr{T}}
.
If
\varphi
is strictly decreasing on
(\sup\supp({\mathcal{T}}(\overline{Y})),\infty)
, this condition is equivalent to the following one

\displaystyle 1

\displaystyle<\frac{1}{\mathbb{E}\left[\overline{\Sigma}\right]}\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\frac{\delta}{\mathbb{E}\left[\overline{\Sigma}\right]}\overline{G}^{2}-1\right)\frac{{\mathcal{T}}(\overline{Y})}{a^{\circ}-{\mathcal{T}}(\overline{Y})}\right]\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{\overline{\Sigma}^{2}}{\gamma^{\circ}-\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{{\mathcal{T}}(\overline{Y})}{a^{\circ}-{\mathcal{T}}(\overline{Y})}\right]\overline{\Sigma}}\right],

(B.1)

by
Item

4
of
Proposition

D.6
.
We assume
a^{\circ}=1
.
This assumption is without loss of generality due to scaling invariance.
Indeed, the threshold condition for
\delta
(i.e.,
Equation

B.1
above) and the self-consistent equations for
(a^{\circ},\gamma^{\circ})
(see
Equation

D.15
and
Lemma

D.5
) only depend on
(a^{\circ},{\mathcal{T}})
through
\frac{{\mathcal{T}}(\overline{Y})}{a^{\circ}-{\mathcal{T}}(\overline{Y})}
.
Therefore, they continue to hold if
(a^{\circ},{\mathcal{T}})
is replaced
4
4

4

Note that
a^{\circ}>\sup\supp({\mathcal{T}}(\overline{Y}))>0
.
with
(1,{\mathcal{T}}/a^{\circ})
.

Let
{\mathcal{J}}(y)=\frac{{\mathcal{T}}(y)}{1-{\mathcal{T}}(y)}
for notational convenience.
The definition of
(a^{\circ},\gamma^{\circ})
in
Equation

D.15
can then be written as

\displaystyle 1

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\delta}\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathcal{J}}(\overline{Y})^{2}\right]\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\frac{\overline{\Sigma}}{\gamma^{\circ}-\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathcal{J}}(\overline{Y})\right]\overline{\Sigma}}\right)^{2}\right],\quad 1=\frac{1}{\delta}\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{\overline{\Sigma}}{\gamma^{\circ}-\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathcal{J}}(\overline{Y})\right]\overline{\Sigma}}\right].

(B.2)

Let
p_{\overline{G}}
denote the density of
\overline{G}\sim{\mathcal{N}}(0,\mathbb{E}\left[\overline{\Sigma}\right]/\delta)
, and
p(\cdot\,|\,g)
the conditional density of
y=q(g,\varepsilon)\in{\mathbb{R}}
given
g\in{\mathbb{R}}
where
\varepsilon\sim P_{\varepsilon}
. Then, using the Cauchy–Schwarz inequality, the second factor on the right-hand side of
Equation

B.1
can be bounded as follows:

\displaystyle\phantom{=}~\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\frac{\delta}{\mathbb{E}\left[\overline{\Sigma}\right]}\overline{G}^{2}-1\right)\frac{{\mathcal{T}}(\overline{Y})}{a^{\circ}-{\mathcal{T}}(\overline{Y})}\right]=\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\frac{\delta}{\mathbb{E}\left[\overline{\Sigma}\right]}\overline{G}^{2}-1\right){\mathcal{J}}(\overline{Y})\right]

\displaystyle=\int_{\supp(\overline{Y})}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}}p_{\overline{G}}(g)p(y\,|\,g)\left(\frac{\delta}{\mathbb{E}\left[\overline{\Sigma}\right]}g^{2}-1\right){\mathcal{J}}(y)\,\mathrm{d}g\,\mathrm{d}y

\displaystyle=\int_{\supp(\overline{Y})}\mathbb{E}\left[p(y\,|\,\overline{G})\left(\frac{\delta}{\mathbb{E}\left[\overline{\Sigma}\right]}\overline{G}^{2}-1\right)\right]{\mathcal{J}}(y)\,\mathrm{d}y\,

\displaystyle=\int_{\supp(\overline{Y})}\frac{\mathbb{E}\left[p(y\,|\,\overline{G})\left(\frac{\delta}{\mathbb{E}\left[\overline{\Sigma}\right]}\overline{G}^{2}-1\right)\right]}{\sqrt{\mathbb{E}\left[p(y\,|\,\overline{G})\right]}}\cdot\sqrt{\mathbb{E}\left[p(y\,|\,\overline{G})\right]}\,{\mathcal{J}}(y)\,\mathrm{d}y

\displaystyle\leq\left(\int_{\supp(\overline{Y})}\frac{\mathbb{E}\left[p(y\,|\,\overline{G})\left(\frac{\delta}{\mathbb{E}\left[\overline{\Sigma}\right]}\overline{G}^{2}-1\right)\right]^{2}}{\mathbb{E}\left[p(y\,|\,\overline{G})\right]}\,\mathrm{d}y\right)^{1/2}\left(\int_{\supp(\overline{Y})}\mathbb{E}\left[p(y\,|\,\overline{G})\right]{\mathcal{J}}(y)^{2}\,\mathrm{d}y\right)^{1/2}

\displaystyle=\left(\int_{\supp(\overline{Y})}\frac{\mathbb{E}\left[p(y\,|\,\overline{G})\left(\frac{\delta}{\mathbb{E}\left[\overline{\Sigma}\right]}\overline{G}^{2}-1\right)\right]^{2}}{\mathbb{E}\left[p(y\,|\,\overline{G})\right]}\,\mathrm{d}y\right)^{1/2}\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathcal{J}}(\overline{Y})^{2}\right]^{1/2}.

(B.3)

Applying the Cauchy–Schwarz inequality to the third factor on the right-hand side of
Equation

B.1
, we obtain

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\mathbb{E}\left[\overline{\Sigma}\right]}\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{\overline{\Sigma}^{2}}{\gamma^{\circ}-\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{{\mathcal{T}}(\overline{Y})}{a^{\circ}-{\mathcal{T}}(\overline{Y})}\right]\overline{\Sigma}}\right]

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\mathbb{E}\left[\overline{\Sigma}\right]}\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{\overline{\Sigma}^{2}}{\gamma^{\circ}-\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathcal{J}}(\overline{Y})\right]\overline{\Sigma}}\right]

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\mathbb{E}\left[\overline{\Sigma}\right]}\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{\overline{\Sigma}}{\gamma^{\circ}-\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathcal{J}}(\overline{Y})\right]\overline{\Sigma}}\cdot\overline{\Sigma}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\mathbb{E}\left[\overline{\Sigma}^{2}\right]^{1/2}}{\mathbb{E}\left[\overline{\Sigma}\right]}\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\frac{\overline{\Sigma}}{\gamma^{\circ}-\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathcal{J}}(\overline{Y})\right]\overline{\Sigma}}\right)^{2}\right]^{1/2}.

(B.4)

Combining
Equations

B.3
and
B.4
, we have that the right-hand side of
Equation

B.1
is bounded from above by

\frac{\mathbb{E}\left[\overline{\Sigma}^{2}\right]^{1/2}}{\mathbb{E}\left[\overline{\Sigma}\right]}\left(\int_{\supp(\overline{Y})}\frac{\mathbb{E}\left[p(y\,|\,\overline{G})\left(\frac{\delta}{\mathbb{E}\left[\overline{\Sigma}\right]}\overline{G}^{2}-1\right)\right]^{2}}{\mathbb{E}\left[p(y\,|\,\overline{G})\right]}\,\mathrm{d}y\right)^{1/2}\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathcal{J}}(\overline{Y})^{2}\right]^{1/2}\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\frac{\overline{\Sigma}}{\gamma^{\circ}-\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathcal{J}}(\overline{Y})\right]\overline{\Sigma}}\right)^{2}\right]^{1/2}\\
=\frac{\mathbb{E}\left[\overline{\Sigma}^{2}\right]^{1/2}}{\mathbb{E}\left[\overline{\Sigma}\right]}\left(\int_{\supp(\overline{Y})}\frac{\mathbb{E}\left[p(y\,|\,\overline{G})\left(\frac{\delta}{\mathbb{E}\left[\overline{\Sigma}\right]}\overline{G}^{2}-1\right)\right]^{2}}{\mathbb{E}\left[p(y\,|\,\overline{G})\right]}\,\mathrm{d}y\right)^{1/2}\sqrt{\delta},

(B.5)

where the equality follows from the first identity in
Equation

B.2
.
Using this in
Equation

B.1
, we have

\displaystyle\delta

\displaystyle>\frac{\mathbb{E}\left[\overline{\Sigma}\right]^{2}}{\mathbb{E}\left[\overline{\Sigma}^{2}\right]}\left(\int_{\supp(\overline{Y})}\frac{\mathbb{E}\left[p(y\,|\,\overline{G})\left(\frac{\delta}{\mathbb{E}\left[\overline{\Sigma}\right]}\overline{G}^{2}-1\right)\right]^{2}}{\mathbb{E}\left[p(y\,|\,\overline{G})\right]}\,\mathrm{d}y\right)^{-1}.

(B.6)

In words, the condition above (which is independent of the choice of
{\mathcal{T}}
) holds for any
{\mathcal{T}}
that satisfies
Equation

B.1
and therefore achieves a positive overlap.

In the following, we show that the condition above is tight by proving
Item

1
of
Theorem

3.2
.
Specifically, whenever
Equation

B.6
holds, we exhibit a preprocessing function
{\mathcal{T}}^{*}\colon{\mathbb{R}}\to{\mathbb{R}}
that meets
Equation

B.1
and therefore must induce a positive overlap.

Suppose that
Equation

B.6
holds.
As before, we choose the scaling such that
a^{\circ}=1
. Constructing
{\mathcal{T}}^{*}(y)
is equivalent to constructing

\displaystyle{\mathcal{J}}^{*}(y)=\frac{{\mathcal{T}}^{*}(y)}{1-{\mathcal{T}}^{*}(y)}.

(B.7)

We require the following notation.
Denote the right-hand side of
Equation

B.6
by
\Delta(\delta)
.
Moreover,

\displaystyle m_{0}(y)

\displaystyle\coloneqq\mathbb{E}\left[p(y\,|\,\overline{G})\right],\quad m_{2}(y)\coloneqq\mathbb{E}\left[p(y\,|\,\overline{G})\cdot\frac{\delta}{\mathbb{E}\left[\overline{\Sigma}\right]}\overline{G}^{2}\right],

(B.8)

Before presenting the construction of
{\mathcal{J}}^{*}
, we first observe that the integrals of both
m_{0}
and
m_{2}
are equal to
1
.

\displaystyle\begin{split}\int_{\supp(\overline{Y})}m_{0}(y)\,\mathrm{d}y&=\mathbb{E}\left[\int_{\supp(\overline{Y})}p(y\,|\,\overline{G})\,\mathrm{d}y\right]=1,\\
\int_{\supp(\overline{Y})}m_{2}(y)\,\mathrm{d}y&=\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\int_{\supp(\overline{Y})}p(y\,|\,\overline{G})\,\mathrm{d}y\right)\frac{\delta}{\mathbb{E}\left[\overline{\Sigma}\right]}\overline{G}^{2}\right]=\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{\delta}{\mathbb{E}\left[\overline{\Sigma}\right]}\overline{G}^{2}\right]=1.\end{split}

(B.9)

Now, consider

\displaystyle{\mathcal{J}}^{*}(y)

\displaystyle\coloneqq\sqrt{\frac{\Delta(\delta)}{\delta}}\left(\frac{m_{2}(y)}{m_{0}(y)}-1\right).

(B.10)

We claim that
{\mathcal{J}}^{*}
satisfies
Equations

B.1
and
B.2
and therefore attains positive overlap.
In fact, we claim that
{\mathcal{J}}^{*}
satisfies a stronger condition than
Equation

B.1
which is displayed below in conjunction with
Equation

B.2
:

\displaystyle\begin{split}\sqrt{\frac{\delta}{\Delta(\delta)}}&=\frac{1}{\mathbb{E}\left[\overline{\Sigma}\right]}\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\frac{\delta}{\mathbb{E}\left[\overline{\Sigma}\right]}\overline{G}^{2}-1\right){\mathcal{J}}^{*}(\overline{Y})\right]\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{\overline{\Sigma}^{2}}{\gamma^{\circ}-\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathcal{J}}^{*}(\overline{Y})\right]\overline{\Sigma}}\right],\\
1&=\frac{1}{\delta}\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathcal{J}}^{*}(\overline{Y})^{2}\right]\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\frac{\overline{\Sigma}}{\gamma^{\circ}-\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathcal{J}}^{*}(\overline{Y})\right]\overline{\Sigma}}\right)^{2}\right],\end{split}

(B.11)

where

\displaystyle 1=\frac{1}{\delta}\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{\overline{\Sigma}}{\gamma^{\circ}-\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathcal{J}}^{*}(\overline{Y})\right]\overline{\Sigma}}\right].

(B.12)

Note that the first identity in
Equation

B.11
implies
Equation

B.1
since
\delta>\Delta(\delta)
by
Equation

B.6
.

Let us verify the validity of
Equation

B.11
.
By the construction of
{\mathcal{J}}^{*}
(see
Equation

B.10
),

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathcal{J}}^{*}(\overline{Y})\right]

\displaystyle=\int_{\supp(\overline{Y})}m_{0}(y){\mathcal{J}}^{*}(y)\,\mathrm{d}y=\sqrt{\frac{\Delta(\delta)}{\delta}}\int_{\supp(\overline{Y})}m_{2}(y)-m_{0}(y)\,\mathrm{d}y=0,

(B.13)

where the last equality follows from
Equation

B.9
.
Using this in
Equation

B.12
, we can solve
\gamma^{\circ}
explicitly:

\displaystyle\gamma^{\circ}

\displaystyle=\frac{\mathbb{E}\left[\overline{\Sigma}\right]}{\delta}.

(B.14)

Consequently, the first two identities of
Equation

B.11
can be simplified as follows.
First look at the first identity of
Equation

B.11
.
The right-hand side equals

\displaystyle\phantom{=}~\frac{1}{\mathbb{E}\left[\overline{\Sigma}\right]}\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\frac{\delta}{\mathbb{E}\left[\overline{\Sigma}\right]}\overline{G}^{2}-1\right){\mathcal{J}}^{*}(\overline{Y})\right]\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{\overline{\Sigma}^{2}}{\gamma^{\circ}-\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathcal{J}}^{*}(\overline{Y})\right]\overline{\Sigma}}\right]

\displaystyle=\frac{\delta\mathbb{E}\left[\overline{\Sigma}^{2}\right]}{\mathbb{E}\left[\overline{\Sigma}\right]^{2}}\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\frac{\delta}{\mathbb{E}\left[\overline{\Sigma}\right]}\overline{G}^{2}-1\right){\mathcal{J}}^{*}(\overline{Y})\right]

(B.15)

\displaystyle=\frac{\delta\mathbb{E}\left[\overline{\Sigma}^{2}\right]}{\mathbb{E}\left[\overline{\Sigma}\right]^{2}}\int_{\supp(\overline{Y})}(m_{2}(y)-m_{0}(y)){\mathcal{J}}^{*}(y)\,\mathrm{d}y

\displaystyle=\sqrt{\Delta(\delta)\delta}\cdot\frac{\mathbb{E}\left[\overline{\Sigma}^{2}\right]}{\mathbb{E}\left[\overline{\Sigma}\right]^{2}}\int_{\supp(\overline{Y})}\frac{(m_{2}(y)-m_{0}(y))^{2}}{m_{0}(y)}\,\mathrm{d}y.

(B.16)

Equation

B.15
is by
Equations

B.13
and
B.14
.

Equation

B.16
is by
Equation

B.10
.
Therefore, the first identity of
Equation

B.11
is equivalent to:

\displaystyle\Delta(\delta)

\displaystyle=\frac{\mathbb{E}\left[\overline{\Sigma}\right]^{2}}{\mathbb{E}\left[\overline{\Sigma}^{2}\right]}\left(\int_{\supp(\overline{Y})}\frac{(m_{2}(y)-m_{0}(y))^{2}}{m_{0}(y)}\,\mathrm{d}y\right)^{-1}.

The right-hand side is the same as that of
Equation

B.6
, hence the first identity of
Equation

B.11
indeed holds by the definition of
\Delta(\delta)
.

Next, we move to the second identity of
Equation

B.11
.
Using
Equations

B.13
and
B.14
again, the right-hand side equals:

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\delta}\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathcal{J}}^{*}(\overline{Y})^{2}\right]\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\frac{\overline{\Sigma}}{\gamma^{\circ}-\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathcal{J}}^{*}(\overline{Y})\right]\overline{\Sigma}}\right)^{2}\right]=\frac{1}{\delta}\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathcal{J}}^{*}(\overline{Y})^{2}\right]\frac{\mathbb{E}\left[\overline{\Sigma}^{2}\right]}{(\gamma^{\circ})^{2}}

\displaystyle=\delta\frac{\mathbb{E}\left[\overline{\Sigma}^{2}\right]}{\mathbb{E}\left[\overline{\Sigma}\right]^{2}}\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathcal{J}}^{*}(\overline{Y})^{2}\right]=\Delta(\delta)\frac{\mathbb{E}\left[\overline{\Sigma}^{2}\right]}{\mathbb{E}\left[\overline{\Sigma}\right]^{2}}\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\frac{m_{2}(\overline{Y})}{m_{0}(\overline{Y})}-1\right)^{2}\right]

\displaystyle=\Delta(\delta)\frac{\mathbb{E}\left[\overline{\Sigma}^{2}\right]}{\mathbb{E}\left[\overline{\Sigma}\right]^{2}}\int_{\supp(\overline{Y})}m_{0}(y)\left(\frac{m_{2}(y)}{m_{0}(y)}-1\right)^{2}\mathrm{d}y=1,

which verifies the second identity of
Equation

B.11
.
The second line uses the definition of
{\mathcal{J}}^{*}
in
Equation

B.10
and the last equality is by the definition of
\Delta(\delta)
(see the right-hand side of
Equation

B.6
).

To complete the proof, it remains to verify that
{\mathcal{T}}^{*}
satisfies
Item

(A5)
.
Recalling
Equations

B.7
and
B.10
, we have

\displaystyle{\mathcal{T}}^{*}(y)

\displaystyle=\frac{{\mathcal{J}}^{*}(y)}{1+{\mathcal{J}}^{*}(y)}=\frac{\sqrt{\frac{\Delta(\delta)}{\delta}}\left(\frac{m_{2}(y)}{m_{0}(y)}-1\right)}{1+\sqrt{\frac{\Delta(\delta)}{\delta}}\left(\frac{m_{2}(y)}{m_{0}(y)}-1\right)}=1-\frac{1}{\sqrt{\frac{\Delta(\delta)}{\delta}}\frac{m_{2}(y)}{m_{0}(y)}+1-\sqrt{\frac{\Delta(\delta)}{\delta}}}.

(B.17)

By definitions, both
m_{2}
and
m_{0}
are non-negative functions.
Therefore

\displaystyle\inf_{y\in\supp(\overline{Y})}{\mathcal{T}}^{*}(y)

\displaystyle\geq 1-\frac{1}{1-\sqrt{\frac{\Delta(\delta)}{\delta}}}>-\infty,

(B.18)

where the last inequality holds since
\delta>\Delta(\delta)
by the assumption in
Equation

B.6
.
Also, it trivially holds that

\displaystyle\sup_{y\in\supp(\overline{Y})}{\mathcal{T}}^{*}(y)

\displaystyle\leq 1<\infty.

(B.19)

It is easy to see that
{\mathcal{T}}^{*}(y)>0
if and only if
m_{2}(y)>m_{0}(y)
.
We first claim that
m_{2}
and
m_{0}
are not identically equal.
Otherwise,
\Delta(\delta)
(i.e., the right-hand side of
Equation

B.6
) is infinity and
\delta
satisfying
Equation

B.6
is also infinity,
violating
Item

(A4)
.
Moreover, by
Equation

B.9
,

\displaystyle\int_{\supp(\overline{Y})}m_{2}(y)-m_{0}(y)\mathrm{d}y

\displaystyle=0.

It follows from the mean value theorem for definite integrals that there exists
y\in\supp(\overline{Y})
such that
m_{2}(y)>m_{0}(y)
which implies

\displaystyle\sup_{y\in\supp(\overline{Y})}{\mathcal{T}}^{*}(y)

\displaystyle>0.

(B.20)

Since
{\mathcal{T}}^{*}
is assumed to be pseudo-Lipschitz of finite order, putting
Equations

B.18
,
B.19
and
B.20
together verifies
Item

(A5)
.

Note that, by the arguments in
Appendix

C
,
{\mathcal{T}}^{*}
does not need to satisfy
Item

(A7)
to have positive limiting overlap. In fact, if
Equation

3.13
holds and
{\mathcal{T}}^{*}
does not have a point mass at the boundaries of its support (otherwise
Item

(A7)
automatically holds), we can create such point masses via a perturbation. Now, the perturbed function satisfies
Item

(A7)
and it has positive limiting overlap for all sufficiently small perturbations. Then, an application of
the Davis–Kahan theorem shows that we can set the perturbation to
0
, and obtain the desired result for
{\mathcal{T}}^{*}
.
This concludes the proof.

Appendix C
Removing Assumptions
(A6)
and
(A7)

We show that the conclusions of
Theorem

3.1
remain valid even if
\Sigma
and/or
{\mathcal{T}}
fail to satisfy
Item

(A6)
and/or
(A7)
.
To do so, we create
\widetilde{\Sigma},\widetilde{{\mathcal{T}}}
that closely approximate
\Sigma,{\mathcal{T}}
and satisfy
Items

(A6)
and
(A7)
.

Theorem

3.1
then applies to
\widetilde{\Sigma},\widetilde{{\mathcal{T}}}
. We then show using a perturbation analysis that the same characterizations also hold for
\Sigma,{\mathcal{T}}
once the perturbation is sent to zero.
The detailed proof is presented below where we assume that both
Items

(A6)
and
(A7)
are violated.
The proof when only one of them holds is analogous and is omitted.

We first construct
\widetilde{\Sigma}
.
Note that if

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\overline{\Sigma}=\inf\supp(\overline{\Sigma})\right)>0,\quad\mathbb{P}\left(\overline{\Sigma}=\sup\supp(\overline{\Sigma})\right)>0,

(C.1)

then
Item

(A6)
is automatically satisfied and one can take
\widetilde{\Sigma}=\Sigma
.
In what follows, we assume that both probabilities in
Equation

C.1
are zero.
(Again, the case where exactly one of the probabilities is zero can be handled verbatim and the details are omitted.)
Write the eigendecomposition of
\Sigma
as

\Sigma=\sum_{i=1}^{d}\lambda_{i}(\Sigma)v_{i}(\Sigma)v_{i}(\Sigma)^{\top}
.
By the convergence of the empirical spectral distribution of
\Sigma
(see
Item

(A2)
), we have that for any sufficiently small
\varsigma>0
, there exists
\xi>0
(depending on
\varsigma
) such that for every sufficiently large
d
,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{d}\left|\left\{i\in\{1,\ldots,d\}:\lambda_{i}(\Sigma)\geq\left(\sqrt{\lambda_{1}(\Sigma)}-\xi\right)^{2}\right\}\right|

\displaystyle\in[\varsigma/2,\varsigma],

\displaystyle\frac{1}{d}\left|\left\{i\in\{1,\ldots,d\}:\lambda_{i}(\Sigma)\leq\left(\sqrt{\lambda_{d}(\Sigma)}+\xi\right)^{2}\right\}\right|

\displaystyle\in[\varsigma/2,\varsigma].

Let
\widetilde{\Sigma}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{d\times d}
be the matrix obtained by truncating the spectrum of
\Sigma
:

\displaystyle\widetilde{\Sigma}

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=1}^{d}\lambda_{i}(\widetilde{\Sigma})v_{i}(\Sigma)v_{i}(\Sigma)^{\top},

where

\displaystyle\lambda_{i}(\widetilde{\Sigma})

\displaystyle=\begin{cases}\left(\sqrt{\lambda_{1}(\Sigma)}-\xi\right)^{2},&\lambda_{i}(\Sigma)\geq\left(\sqrt{\lambda_{1}(\Sigma)}-\xi\right)^{2}\\
\left(\sqrt{\lambda_{d}(\Sigma)}+\xi\right)^{2},&\lambda_{i}(\Sigma)\leq\left(\sqrt{\lambda_{d}(\Sigma)}+\xi\right)^{2}\\
\lambda_{i}(\Sigma),&\mathrm{otherwise}\end{cases}.

It is easy to check that
\widetilde{\Sigma}
still satisfies
Item

(A2)
if
\Sigma
does.
Moreover, upon truncation, the limiting spectral distribution of
\widetilde{\Sigma}
has positive mass on both the left and right edges and hence obviously satisfies
Item

(A6)
.

Let us then construct
\widetilde{{\mathcal{T}}}
.
Clearly, if

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left({\mathcal{T}}(\overline{Y})=\sup\supp({\mathcal{T}}(\overline{Y}))\right)

\displaystyle>0,

(C.2)

then
Equation

2.7
is satisfied.
We therefore assume that the above equation holds with equality.
In this case, we truncate
{\mathcal{T}}
slightly below its supremum to create
\widetilde{{\mathcal{T}}}
which satisfies
Equation

2.7
.
Specifically, for any
\varsigma>0
, there exists
\xi>0
(depending on
\varsigma
) such that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left({\mathcal{T}}(\overline{Y})\in[\sup\supp({\mathcal{T}}(\overline{Y}))-\xi,\sup\supp({\mathcal{T}}(\overline{Y}))]\right)

\displaystyle\in[\varsigma/2,\varsigma].

Define
\widetilde{{\mathcal{T}}}
as

\displaystyle\widetilde{{\mathcal{T}}}(y)

\displaystyle\coloneqq\min\left\{{\mathcal{T}}(y),\sup\supp({\mathcal{T}}(\overline{Y}))-\xi\right\}.

(C.3)

Note that
\widetilde{{\mathcal{T}}}
depends on
\varsigma
.
Also, it satisfies
Equation

C.2
and therefore
Equation

2.7
.
It is easy to see that
Item

(A5)
will not be violated after the truncation.

Now the conclusions of
Theorem

3.1
hold for
\widetilde{\Sigma},\widetilde{{\mathcal{T}}}
.
In particular,
\widetilde{a}^{*},\widetilde{a}^{\circ}
can be defined using
Equations

3.4
and
3.6
but with
\widetilde{{\mathcal{T}}}
and the limiting spectral distribution of
\widetilde{\Sigma}
.
It then suffices to show that as long as
\widetilde{a}^{*}>\widetilde{a}^{\circ}
, the difference between the spectral statistics under
\Sigma,{\mathcal{T}}
and those under
\widetilde{\Sigma},\widetilde{{\mathcal{T}}}
is vanishing as
\varsigma\to 0
.
Let

\displaystyle D\coloneqq\Sigma^{1/2}\widetilde{X}^{\top}T\widetilde{X}\Sigma^{1/2},\quad\widetilde{D}\coloneqq\widetilde{\Sigma}^{1/2}\widetilde{X}^{\top}\widetilde{T}\widetilde{X}\widetilde{\Sigma}^{1/2},

where

\displaystyle T

\displaystyle\coloneqq\mathrm{diag}({\mathcal{T}}(y)),\quad\widetilde{T}\coloneqq\mathrm{diag}(\widetilde{{\mathcal{T}}}(y)).

Then

\displaystyle\left\|D-\widetilde{D}\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle=\left\|\Sigma^{1/2}\widetilde{X}^{\top}T\widetilde{X}\Sigma^{1/2}-\widetilde{\Sigma}^{1/2}\widetilde{X}^{\top}\widetilde{T}\widetilde{X}\widetilde{\Sigma}^{1/2}\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\left\|\Sigma^{1/2}\widetilde{X}^{\top}T\widetilde{X}\Sigma^{1/2}-\widetilde{\Sigma}^{1/2}\widetilde{X}^{\top}T\widetilde{X}\Sigma^{1/2}\right\|_{2}+\left\|\widetilde{\Sigma}^{1/2}\widetilde{X}^{\top}T\widetilde{X}\Sigma^{1/2}-\widetilde{\Sigma}^{1/2}\widetilde{X}^{\top}\widetilde{T}\widetilde{X}\Sigma^{1/2}\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle\phantom{=}~+\left\|\widetilde{\Sigma}^{1/2}\widetilde{X}^{\top}\widetilde{T}\widetilde{X}\Sigma^{1/2}-\widetilde{\Sigma}^{1/2}\widetilde{X}^{\top}\widetilde{T}\widetilde{X}\widetilde{\Sigma}^{1/2}\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\left\|\Sigma^{1/2}-\widetilde{\Sigma}^{1/2}\right\|_{2}\left\|\widetilde{X}\right\|_{2}^{2}\left\|T\right\|_{2}\left\|\Sigma^{1/2}\right\|_{2}+\left\|\widetilde{\Sigma}^{1/2}\right\|_{2}\left\|\widetilde{X}\right\|_{2}^{2}\left\|T-\widetilde{T}\right\|_{2}\left\|\Sigma^{1/2}\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle\phantom{=}~+\left\|\widetilde{\Sigma}^{1/2}\right\|_{2}\left\|\widetilde{X}\right\|_{2}^{2}\left\|\widetilde{T}\right\|_{2}\left\|\Sigma^{1/2}-\widetilde{\Sigma}^{1/2}\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq 2\left\|\Sigma^{1/2}-\widetilde{\Sigma}^{1/2}\right\|_{2}\left\|\widetilde{X}\right\|_{2}^{2}\left\|T\right\|_{2}\left\|\Sigma^{1/2}\right\|_{2}+\left\|\Sigma^{1/2}\right\|_{2}^{2}\left\|\widetilde{X}\right\|_{2}^{2}\left\|T-\widetilde{T}\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq 2\xi\left(1+1/\sqrt{\delta}+0.01\right)^{2}\left(\sup\supp({\mathcal{T}}(\overline{Y}))+0.01\right)\left(\supp(\overline{\Sigma})+0.01\right)

\displaystyle\phantom{=}~+\left(\supp(\overline{\Sigma})+0.01\right)\left(1+1/\sqrt{\delta}+0.01\right)^{2}\xi

\displaystyle\leq c_{1}\xi,

(C.4)

where the bound on the penultimate line holds almost surely for every sufficiently large
d
, and
c_{1}>0
in the last line is a constant independent of
d
.
The
+0.01
terms are to exclude deviations for small
d
.
Furthermore, if
\widetilde{a}^{*}>\widetilde{a}^{\circ}
,
Theorem

3.1
guarantees that there exists a constant
c_{2}>0
such that for every sufficiently large
d
, with probability
1
,

\displaystyle\lambda_{1}(\widetilde{D})-\lambda_{2}(\widetilde{D})\geq c_{2}.

(C.5)

Using
Equations

C.4
and
C.5
in the Davis–Kahan theorem (
Proposition

G.4
), we obtain

\displaystyle\min\left\{\left\|v_{1}(D)-v_{1}(\widetilde{D})\right\|_{2},\left\|v_{1}(D)+v_{1}(\widetilde{D})\right\|_{2}\right\}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{4\left\|D-\widetilde{D}\right\|_{2}}{\lambda_{1}(D)-\lambda_{2}(D)}\leq 4c_{1}\xi/c_{2},

which implies

\displaystyle\left|\left|\left\langle v_{1}(D),\frac{\beta^{*}}{\sqrt{d}}\right\rangle\right|-\left|\left\langle v_{1}(\widetilde{D}),\frac{\beta^{*}}{\sqrt{d}}\right\rangle\right|\right|

\displaystyle=\min_{\sigma\in\{-1,1\}}\left|\left\langle v_{1}(D)-\sigma v_{1}(\widetilde{D}),\frac{\beta^{*}}{\sqrt{d}}\right\rangle\right|

\displaystyle\leq\min_{\sigma\in\{-1,1\}}\left\|v_{1}(D)-v_{1}(\widetilde{D})\right\|_{2}\leq 4c_{1}\xi/c_{2}.

(C.6)

By
Theorem

3.1
, the condition
\widetilde{a}^{*}>\widetilde{a}^{\circ}
also implies that the overlap between
v_{1}(\widetilde{D})
and
\beta^{*}
converges in probability to
\eta>0
.
Since
\varsigma>0
(and therefore
\xi
) can be made arbitrarily small,
Equation

C.6
then allows us to conclude that the overlap between
v_{1}(D)
and
\beta^{*}
also converges to
\eta
.
This proves
Equation

3.12
for
D
.

Using
Equation

C.4
and Weyl’s inequality, we have for any
i\in\{1,\ldots,d\}
,

\displaystyle\left|\lambda_{i}(D)-\lambda_{i}(\widetilde{D})\right|

\displaystyle\leq\left\|D-\widetilde{D}\right\|_{2}\leq c_{1}\xi,

which in particular establishes
Equation

3.11
for
D
.
This completes the proof.

Appendix D
Properties of auxiliary functions and parameters

D.1
Existence and uniqueness of
a^{*}

Recall the functions
\varphi,\psi\colon(\sup\supp({\mathcal{T}}(\overline{Y})),\infty)\to{\mathbb{R}}
defined in
Equation

3.2
.

Proposition D.1

(Existence of
a^{*}
)
.

Let
Item

(A7)
hold.
Then, the equation
\varphi(a^{*})=\zeta(a^{*})
has at least one solution in
(\sup\supp({\mathcal{T}}(\overline{Y})),\infty)
.

Proof.

Recall that both
\varphi
and
\zeta
are defined on
(\sup\supp({\mathcal{T}}(\overline{Y})),\infty)
.
It is not hard to see from
Equation

3.3
that
\gamma
is a continuous function.
Therefore
\varphi,\psi,\zeta
are also continuous.
We will show

\displaystyle\lim_{a\searrow\sup\supp({\mathcal{T}}(\overline{Y}))}\varphi(a)>\lim_{a\searrow\sup\supp({\mathcal{T}}(\overline{Y}))}\zeta(a),\qquad\lim_{a\nearrow\infty}\varphi(a)<\lim_{a\nearrow\infty}\zeta(a).

(D.1)

Then by the intermediate value theorem, this immediately implies the result.

We will explicitly evaluate the four limits.
To this end, let us first study the limiting values of
\gamma(a)
defined through
Equation

3.3
.

Limiting values of
\gamma
.

By inspecting the defining equation, it is clear that

\displaystyle\lim_{a\to\infty}\frac{1}{\delta}\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{\overline{\Sigma}}{\gamma-\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{{\mathcal{T}}(\overline{Y})}{a-{\mathcal{T}}(\overline{Y})}\right]\overline{\Sigma}}\right]

\displaystyle=\frac{\mathbb{E}\left[\overline{\Sigma}\right]}{\delta\gamma},

and hence

\displaystyle\lim_{a\to\infty}\gamma(a)

\displaystyle=\frac{\mathbb{E}\left[\overline{\Sigma}\right]}{\delta},

(D.2)

which is positive and finite.
We also claim that

\displaystyle\lim_{a\searrow\sup\supp({\mathcal{T}}(\overline{Y}))}\gamma(a)

\displaystyle=\infty.

(D.3)

Otherwise, for any finite
\gamma
, by
(d)
in
Equation

2.7
,

\displaystyle\lim_{a\searrow\sup\supp({\mathcal{T}}(\overline{Y}))}\frac{1}{\delta}\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{\overline{\Sigma}}{\gamma-\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{{\mathcal{T}}(\overline{Y})}{a-{\mathcal{T}}(\overline{Y})}\right]\overline{\Sigma}}\right]

\displaystyle=0,

which violates
Equation

3.3
.
The possibility of
\lim\limits_{a\searrow\sup\supp({\mathcal{T}}(\overline{Y}))}\gamma(a)=-\infty
can be similarly excluded.

Limiting values of
\varphi
.

We claim that

\displaystyle\lim_{a\searrow\sup\supp({\mathcal{T}}(\overline{Y}))}\varphi(a)

\displaystyle=\infty,\quad\lim_{a\to\infty}\varphi(a)=\delta\mathbb{E}\left[\overline{G}^{2}{\mathcal{T}}(\overline{Y})\right]\frac{\mathbb{E}\left[\overline{\Sigma}^{2}\right]}{\mathbb{E}\left[\overline{\Sigma}\right]^{2}}<\infty.

(D.4)

The limit towards the right boundary of the domain is easy to verify:

\displaystyle\lim_{a\to\infty}\varphi(a)

\displaystyle=\lim_{a\to\infty}\frac{1}{\mathbb{E}\left[\overline{\Sigma}\right]}\mathbb{E}\left[\overline{G}^{2}\frac{{\mathcal{T}}(\overline{Y})}{1-{\mathcal{T}}(\overline{Y})/a}\right]\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{\overline{\Sigma}^{2}}{\gamma(a)-\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{{\mathcal{T}}(\overline{Y})}{a-{\mathcal{T}}(\overline{Y})}\right]\overline{\Sigma}}\right]

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\mathbb{E}\left[\overline{\Sigma}\right]}\mathbb{E}\left[\overline{G}^{2}{\mathcal{T}}(\overline{Y})\right]\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{\overline{\Sigma}^{2}}{\mathbb{E}\left[\overline{\Sigma}\right]/\delta}\right]

\displaystyle=\delta\mathbb{E}\left[\overline{G}^{2}{\mathcal{T}}(\overline{Y})\right]\frac{\mathbb{E}\left[\overline{\Sigma}^{2}\right]}{\mathbb{E}\left[\overline{\Sigma}\right]^{2}},

where we use
Equation

D.3
in the second equality.
To show the first equality in
Equation

D.4
, let us start by observing that for any
a>\sup\supp({\mathcal{T}}(\overline{Y}))
,

\displaystyle 0<\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{1}{\gamma(a)-\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{{\mathcal{T}}(\overline{Y})}{a-{\mathcal{T}}(\overline{Y})}\right]\overline{\Sigma}}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{\inf\supp(\overline{\Sigma})}\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{\overline{\Sigma}}{\gamma(a)-\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{{\mathcal{T}}(\overline{Y})}{a-{\mathcal{T}}(\overline{Y})}\right]\overline{\Sigma}}\right]=\frac{\delta}{\inf\supp(\overline{\Sigma})}.

(D.5)

The second inequality is valid since
\inf\supp(\overline{\Sigma})>0
by
Item

(A2)
and hence
\frac{\overline{\Sigma}}{\inf\supp(\overline{\Sigma})}\geq 1
almost surely.
The last equality is by the definition of
\gamma(\cdot)
(see
Equation

3.3
).
On the other hand, a simple application of the Cauchy–Schwarz inequality yields:

\displaystyle\delta^{2}=\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{\overline{\Sigma}}{\gamma(a)-\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{{\mathcal{T}}(\overline{Y})}{a-{\mathcal{T}}(\overline{Y})}\right]\overline{\Sigma}}\right]^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{\overline{\Sigma}}{\left(\gamma(a)-\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{{\mathcal{T}}(\overline{Y})}{a-{\mathcal{T}}(\overline{Y})}\right]\overline{\Sigma}\right)^{1/2}}\cdot\frac{1}{\left(\gamma(a)-\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{{\mathcal{T}}(\overline{Y})}{a-{\mathcal{T}}(\overline{Y})}\right]\overline{\Sigma}\right)^{1/2}}\right]^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{\overline{\Sigma}^{2}}{\gamma(a)-\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{{\mathcal{T}}(\overline{Y})}{a-{\mathcal{T}}(\overline{Y})}\right]\overline{\Sigma}}\right]\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{1}{\gamma(a)-\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{{\mathcal{T}}(\overline{Y})}{a-{\mathcal{T}}(\overline{Y})}\right]\overline{\Sigma}}\right].

Rearranging and using
Equation

D.5
gives:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{\overline{\Sigma}^{2}}{\gamma(a)-\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{{\mathcal{T}}(\overline{Y})}{a-{\mathcal{T}}(\overline{Y})}\right]\overline{\Sigma}}\right]

\displaystyle\geq\frac{\delta^{2}}{\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{1}{\gamma(a)-\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{{\mathcal{T}}(\overline{Y})}{a-{\mathcal{T}}(\overline{Y})}\right]\overline{\Sigma}}\right]}\geq\delta\cdot\inf\supp(\overline{\Sigma}),

the right-hand side of which is a strictly positive lower bound independent of
a
.
From here, we conclude

\displaystyle\lim_{a\searrow\sup\supp({\mathcal{T}}(\overline{Y}))}\varphi(a)

\displaystyle=\lim_{a\searrow\sup\supp({\mathcal{T}}(\overline{Y}))}\frac{a}{\mathbb{E}\left[\overline{\Sigma}\right]}\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{\overline{G}^{2}{\mathcal{T}}(\overline{Y})}{a-{\mathcal{T}}(\overline{Y})}\right]\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{\overline{\Sigma}^{2}}{\gamma(a)-\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{{\mathcal{T}}(\overline{Y})}{a-{\mathcal{T}}(\overline{Y})}\right]\overline{\Sigma}}\right]=\infty,

since the middle term converges to
\infty
by
(e)
in
Equation

2.7
and the remaining terms are lower bounded by some positive constant as
a\searrow\sup\supp({\mathcal{T}}(\overline{Y}))
.

Limiting values of
\zeta
.

By definition,

\displaystyle\lim_{a\searrow\sup\supp({\mathcal{T}}(\overline{Y}))}\zeta(a)

\displaystyle=\zeta(a^{\circ})=\psi(a^{\circ})<\infty.

(D.6)

Using
Equation

D.2
, we obtain

\displaystyle\lim_{a\to\infty}\zeta(a)=\lim_{a\to\infty}\psi(a)=\lim_{a\to\infty}a\gamma(a)=\infty.

(D.7)

Finally, combining
Equations

D.4
,
D.6
and
D.7
gives
Equation

D.1
which completes the proof of the proposition.
∎

Proposition D.2

(Monotonicity of
\varphi
)
.

Let
Item

(A5)
hold.
Suppose

\displaystyle\inf_{y\in\supp(\overline{Y})}{\mathcal{T}}(y)\geq 0.

(D.8)

Then, the function
\varphi
is strictly decreasing.

Proof.

We show that
\varphi
is strictly decreasing by proving
\varphi^{\prime}<0
.
