Let us start by computing
\varphi^{\prime}
.
Recall

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\overline{\Sigma}\right]\varphi(a)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[\overline{G}^{2}\frac{a{\mathcal{T}}(\overline{Y})}{a-{\mathcal{T}}(\overline{Y})}\right]\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{\overline{\Sigma}^{2}}{\gamma(a)-\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{{\mathcal{T}}(\overline{Y})}{a-{\mathcal{T}}(\overline{Y})}\right]\overline{\Sigma}}\right].

Using the chain rule, we obtain:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\overline{\Sigma}\right]\varphi^{\prime}(a)=-\mathbb{E}\left[\overline{G}^{2}\frac{{\mathcal{T}}(\overline{Y})^{2}}{\left(a-{\mathcal{T}}(\overline{Y})\right)^{2}}\right]\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{\overline{\Sigma}^{2}}{\gamma(a)-\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{{\mathcal{T}}(\overline{Y})}{a-{\mathcal{T}}(\overline{Y})}\right]\overline{\Sigma}}\right]

\displaystyle\phantom{=}~-\mathbb{E}\left[\overline{G}^{2}\frac{a{\mathcal{T}}(\overline{Y})}{a-{\mathcal{T}}(\overline{Y})}\right]\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{\overline{\Sigma}^{2}}{\left(\gamma(a)-\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{{\mathcal{T}}(\overline{Y})}{a-{\mathcal{T}}(\overline{Y})}\right]\overline{\Sigma}\right)^{2}}\left(\gamma^{\prime}(a)+\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{{\mathcal{T}}(\overline{Y})}{\left(a-{\mathcal{T}}(\overline{Y})\right)^{2}}\right]\overline{\Sigma}\right)\right].

(D.9)

The derivative of
\gamma
can be accessed via the implicit function theorem.
Let

\displaystyle H(a,\gamma)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\delta}\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{\overline{\Sigma}}{\gamma-\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{{\mathcal{T}}(\overline{Y})}{a-{\mathcal{T}}(\overline{Y})}\right]\overline{\Sigma}}\right]-1.

Recalling
Equation

3.3
, we see that
\gamma(a)
is the solution
\gamma
to
H(a,\gamma)=0
.
We have

\displaystyle\frac{\partial}{\partial a}H(a,\gamma)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\delta}\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{-\overline{\Sigma}}{\left(\gamma-\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{{\mathcal{T}}(\overline{Y})}{a-{\mathcal{T}}(\overline{Y})}\right]\overline{\Sigma}\right)^{2}}\cdot(-\overline{\Sigma})\cdot\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{-{\mathcal{T}}(\overline{Y})}{(a-{\mathcal{T}}(\overline{Y}))^{2}}\right]\right]

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{\delta}\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{{\mathcal{T}}(\overline{Y})}{(a-{\mathcal{T}}(\overline{Y}))^{2}}\right]\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{\overline{\Sigma}^{2}}{\left(\gamma-\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{{\mathcal{T}}(\overline{Y})}{a-{\mathcal{T}}(\overline{Y})}\right]\overline{\Sigma}\right)^{2}}\right],

and

\displaystyle\frac{\partial}{\partial\gamma}H(a,\gamma)

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{\delta}\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{\overline{\Sigma}}{\left(\gamma-\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{{\mathcal{T}}(\overline{Y})}{a-{\mathcal{T}}(\overline{Y})}\right]\overline{\Sigma}\right)^{2}}\right].

By the implicit function theorem,

\displaystyle\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}a}\gamma(a)

\displaystyle=-\frac{\frac{\partial}{\partial a}H(a,\gamma(a))}{\frac{\partial}{\partial\gamma}H(a,\gamma(a))}=-\frac{\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{{\mathcal{T}}(\overline{Y})}{(a-{\mathcal{T}}(\overline{Y}))^{2}}\right]\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{\overline{\Sigma}^{2}}{\left(\gamma(a)-\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{{\mathcal{T}}(\overline{Y})}{a-{\mathcal{T}}(\overline{Y})}\right]\overline{\Sigma}\right)^{2}}\right]}{\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{\overline{\Sigma}}{\left(\gamma(a)-\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{{\mathcal{T}}(\overline{Y})}{a-{\mathcal{T}}(\overline{Y})}\right]\overline{\Sigma}\right)^{2}}\right]}.

(D.10)

Using this, we simplify the second term of
Equation

D.9
:

\displaystyle\phantom{=}~-\mathbb{E}\left[\overline{G}^{2}\frac{a{\mathcal{T}}(\overline{Y})}{a-{\mathcal{T}}(\overline{Y})}\right]\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{\overline{\Sigma}^{2}}{\left(\gamma(a)-\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{{\mathcal{T}}(\overline{Y})}{a-{\mathcal{T}}(\overline{Y})}\right]\overline{\Sigma}\right)^{2}}\left(\gamma^{\prime}(a)+\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{{\mathcal{T}}(\overline{Y})}{\left(a-{\mathcal{T}}(\overline{Y})\right)^{2}}\right]\overline{\Sigma}\right)\right]

\displaystyle=-\mathbb{E}\left[\overline{G}^{2}\frac{a{\mathcal{T}}(\overline{Y})}{a-{\mathcal{T}}(\overline{Y})}\right]\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{\overline{\Sigma}^{2}}{\left(\gamma(a)-\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{{\mathcal{T}}(\overline{Y})}{a-{\mathcal{T}}(\overline{Y})}\right]\overline{\Sigma}\right)^{2}}\right]\gamma^{\prime}(a)

\displaystyle\phantom{=}~-\mathbb{E}\left[\overline{G}^{2}\frac{a{\mathcal{T}}(\overline{Y})}{a-{\mathcal{T}}(\overline{Y})}\right]\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{{\mathcal{T}}(\overline{Y})}{\left(a-{\mathcal{T}}(\overline{Y})\right)^{2}}\right]\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{\overline{\Sigma}^{3}}{\left(\gamma(a)-\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{{\mathcal{T}}(\overline{Y})}{a-{\mathcal{T}}(\overline{Y})}\right]\overline{\Sigma}\right)^{2}}\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[\overline{G}^{2}\frac{a{\mathcal{T}}(\overline{Y})}{a-{\mathcal{T}}(\overline{Y})}\right]\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{\overline{\Sigma}^{2}}{\left(\gamma(a)-\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{{\mathcal{T}}(\overline{Y})}{a-{\mathcal{T}}(\overline{Y})}\right]\overline{\Sigma}\right)^{2}}\right]^{2}\frac{\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{{\mathcal{T}}(\overline{Y})}{(a-{\mathcal{T}}(\overline{Y}))^{2}}\right]}{\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{\overline{\Sigma}}{\left(\gamma(a)-\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{{\mathcal{T}}(\overline{Y})}{a-{\mathcal{T}}(\overline{Y})}\right]\overline{\Sigma}\right)^{2}}\right]}

\displaystyle\phantom{=}~-\mathbb{E}\left[\overline{G}^{2}\frac{a{\mathcal{T}}(\overline{Y})}{a-{\mathcal{T}}(\overline{Y})}\right]\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{{\mathcal{T}}(\overline{Y})}{\left(a-{\mathcal{T}}(\overline{Y})\right)^{2}}\right]\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{\overline{\Sigma}^{3}}{\left(\gamma(a)-\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{{\mathcal{T}}(\overline{Y})}{a-{\mathcal{T}}(\overline{Y})}\right]\overline{\Sigma}\right)^{2}}\right].

(D.11)

Let us argue that the right-hand side is negative.
First note that since
(i)

a>\sup\supp({\mathcal{T}}(\overline{Y}))>0
,
(ii)

\inf\supp({\mathcal{T}}(\overline{Y}))\geq 0
by
Equation

D.8
,
(iii)

{\mathcal{T}}(\overline{Y})
is not almost surely zero by
Item

(A5)
, the common factors are positive:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\overline{G}^{2}\frac{a{\mathcal{T}}(\overline{Y})}{a-{\mathcal{T}}(\overline{Y})}\right]\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{{\mathcal{T}}(\overline{Y})}{(a-{\mathcal{T}}(\overline{Y}))^{2}}\right]>0.

(D.12)

Then we apply the Cauchy–Schwarz inequality to obtain:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{\overline{\Sigma}^{2}}{\left(\gamma(a)-\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{{\mathcal{T}}(\overline{Y})}{a-{\mathcal{T}}(\overline{Y})}\right]\overline{\Sigma}\right)^{2}}\right]^{2}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{\overline{\Sigma}^{1/2}}{\gamma(a)-\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{{\mathcal{T}}(\overline{Y})}{a-{\mathcal{T}}(\overline{Y})}\right]\overline{\Sigma}}\cdot\frac{\overline{\Sigma}^{3/2}}{\gamma(a)-\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{{\mathcal{T}}(\overline{Y})}{a-{\mathcal{T}}(\overline{Y})}\right]\overline{\Sigma}}\right]^{2}

(D.13)

\displaystyle\hskip-40.00006pt\leq\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{\overline{\Sigma}}{\left(\gamma(a)-\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{{\mathcal{T}}(\overline{Y})}{a-{\mathcal{T}}(\overline{Y})}\right]\overline{\Sigma}\right)^{2}}\right]\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{\overline{\Sigma}^{3}}{\left(\gamma(a)-\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{{\mathcal{T}}(\overline{Y})}{a-{\mathcal{T}}(\overline{Y})}\right]\overline{\Sigma}\right)^{2}}\right].

(D.14)

Equation

D.13
is valid since
\overline{\Sigma}
is positive and
\gamma(a)>s(a)
.

Equations

D.12
and
D.14
jointly imply that the right-hand side of
Equation

D.11
, i.e., the second term of
Equation

D.9
, is non-positive, as claimed.
Moreover, the first term of
Equation

D.9
is strictly negative.
We therefore conclude that
\varphi^{\prime}(a)<0
for any
a>\sup\supp({\mathcal{T}}(\overline{Y}))
.
∎

Remark D.1

(Monotonicity of
\varphi
)
.

The monotonicity property of
\varphi
relies on the non-negativity of
{\mathcal{T}}
in
Equation

D.8
.
We believe that this assumption can be relaxed.
In fact, numerical evidence suggests that
\varphi
is monotone: we report in
Figure

5
that in the setting of noiseless phase retrieval
q(g,\varepsilon)=\left|g\right|
with optimal preprocessing function
{\mathcal{T}}(y)=\max\left\{1-\frac{1}{\delta y^{2}},-10\right\}
(where
\delta=0.1
), the function
\varphi
is strictly decreasing and convex in
(1,\infty)
(note that
\sup\supp({\mathcal{T}}(\overline{Y}))=1
) when
\overline{\Sigma}
is Toeplitz with
\rho=0.9
or circulant with
c_{0}=1,c_{1}=0.1,\ell=17
. Note that the function
{\mathcal{T}}
here is not everywhere non-negative.

Figure 5
:
Plots of the function
\varphi
defined in
Equation

3.2
with parameters specified in
Remark

D.1
.

Proposition D.3

(Uniqueness of
a^{*}
)
.

Let
Item

(A5)
hold.
Suppose that
\varphi
is strictly decreasing.
Then,
\varphi(a^{*})=\zeta(a^{*})
has a unique solution in
(\sup\supp({\mathcal{T}}(\overline{Y})),\infty)
.

Proof.

The uniqueness of
a^{*}
follows from several properties that have been proved for
\varphi
and
\zeta
.
Recall the assumption that
\varphi
is strictly decreasing and that
\zeta
is non-decreasing by
Lemma

E.1
.
Furthermore, from the proof of
Proposition

D.1
(in particular
Equations

D.4
,
D.6
and
D.7
), we know that in the interval
(\sup\supp({\mathcal{T}}(\overline{Y})),\infty)
,
\varphi
strictly decreases from
\infty
to a finite constant, whereas
\zeta
increases from a finite constant to
\infty
.
By the intermediate value theorem, the solution to
\varphi(a^{*})=\zeta(a^{*})
must exist and is unique.
∎

D.2
Equivalent definitions of
a^{\circ},a^{*}
and equivalent description of
\sup\supp(\overline{\mu}_{\widehat{D}})

Let
{\mathcal{A}}\subset{\mathbb{R}}^{2}
be the domain on which the potential solutions to various self-consistent equations of interest are to be considered:

\displaystyle{\mathcal{A}}

\displaystyle\coloneqq\left\{(a,\gamma):a>\sup\supp({\mathcal{T}}(\overline{Y})),\,\gamma>s(a)\right\},

where
s(a)
is defined in
Equation

3.1
.

Proposition D.4

(Equivalent definitions of
a^{\circ},a^{*}
)
.

•

In the domain
{\mathcal{A}}
, the unique solution
(a^{\circ},\gamma^{\circ})
to

\displaystyle\begin{split}1&=\frac{1}{\delta}\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\frac{{\mathcal{T}}(\overline{Y})}{a^{\circ}-{\mathcal{T}}(\overline{Y})}\right)^{2}\right]\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\frac{\overline{\Sigma}}{\gamma^{\circ}-\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{{\mathcal{T}}(\overline{Y})}{a^{\circ}-{\mathcal{T}}(\overline{Y})}\right]\overline{\Sigma}}\right)^{2}\right],\quad 1=\frac{1}{\delta}\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{\overline{\Sigma}}{\gamma^{\circ}-\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{{\mathcal{T}}(\overline{Y})}{a^{\circ}-{\mathcal{T}}(\overline{Y})}\right]\overline{\Sigma}}\right]\end{split}

(D.15)

is the same as the unique solution to the following equations:

\displaystyle\psi^{\prime}(a^{\circ})

\displaystyle=0,\quad\gamma^{\circ}=\gamma(a^{\circ}).

(D.16)

•

Let
(a^{*},\gamma^{*})
be the solution in
{\mathcal{A}}
to

\displaystyle\zeta(a^{*})

\displaystyle=\varphi(a^{*}),\quad\gamma^{*}=\gamma(a^{*}),

(D.17)

such that
a^{*}
is the largest among all solutions.
If
a^{*}>a^{\circ}
, then
(a^{*},\gamma^{*})
is also a solution to
Equation

5.7
.

Proof.

We start by showing the equivalence between
Equations

D.16
and
D.15
.
We will argue that
\psi^{\prime}(a)=0
if and only if
Equation

D.15
holds.
The derivative of
\psi^{\prime}
is

\displaystyle\psi^{\prime}(a)

\displaystyle=\gamma(a)+a\gamma^{\prime}(a)=\gamma(a)-a\cdot\frac{\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{{\mathcal{T}}(\overline{Y})}{(a-{\mathcal{T}}(\overline{Y}))^{2}}\right]\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{\overline{\Sigma}^{2}}{\left(\gamma(a)-\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{{\mathcal{T}}(\overline{Y})}{a-{\mathcal{T}}(\overline{Y})}\right]\overline{\Sigma}\right)^{2}}\right]}{\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{\overline{\Sigma}}{\left(\gamma(a)-\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{{\mathcal{T}}(\overline{Y})}{a-{\mathcal{T}}(\overline{Y})}\right]\overline{\Sigma}\right)^{2}}\right]},

(D.18)

where the formula for
\gamma^{\prime}
has been derived in
Equation

D.10
.
Using the above expression and rearranging terms, we can write the equation
\psi^{\prime}(a)=0
as

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{\gamma(a)\overline{\Sigma}}{\left(\gamma(a)-\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{{\mathcal{T}}(\overline{Y})}{a-{\mathcal{T}}(\overline{Y})}\right]\overline{\Sigma}\right)^{2}}\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{a{\mathcal{T}}(\overline{Y})}{(a-{\mathcal{T}}(\overline{Y}))^{2}}\right]\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{\overline{\Sigma}^{2}}{\left(\gamma(a)-\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{{\mathcal{T}}(\overline{Y})}{a-{\mathcal{T}}(\overline{Y})}\right]\overline{\Sigma}\right)^{2}}\right].

(D.19)

We rewrite the first two terms in the above equation in the following way:

\displaystyle\begin{split}\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{\gamma(a)\overline{\Sigma}}{\left(\gamma(a)-\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{{\mathcal{T}}(\overline{Y})}{a-{\mathcal{T}}(\overline{Y})}\right]\overline{\Sigma}\right)^{2}}\right]&=\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{\overline{\Sigma}}{\gamma(a)-\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{{\mathcal{T}}(\overline{Y})}{a-{\mathcal{T}}(\overline{Y})}\right]\overline{\Sigma}}\right]\\
&\phantom{=}~+\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{\overline{\Sigma}^{2}}{\left(\gamma(a)-\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{{\mathcal{T}}(\overline{Y})}{a-{\mathcal{T}}(\overline{Y})}\right]\overline{\Sigma}\right)^{2}}\right]\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{{\mathcal{T}}(\overline{Y})}{a-{\mathcal{T}}(\overline{Y})}\right],\\
\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{a{\mathcal{T}}(\overline{Y})}{(a-{\mathcal{T}}(\overline{Y}))^{2}}\right]&=\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{{\mathcal{T}}(\overline{Y})}{a-{\mathcal{T}}(\overline{Y})}\right]+\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{{\mathcal{T}}(\overline{Y})^{2}}{(a-{\mathcal{T}}(\overline{Y}))^{2}}\right].\end{split}

(D.20)

Using the right-hand sides above in place of the left-hand sides in
Equation

D.19
, we see that the term
\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{\overline{\Sigma}^{2}}{\left(\gamma(a)-\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{{\mathcal{T}}(\overline{Y})}{a-{\mathcal{T}}(\overline{Y})}\right]\overline{\Sigma}\right)^{2}}\right]\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{{\mathcal{T}}(\overline{Y})}{a-{\mathcal{T}}(\overline{Y})}\right]
cancels on both sides and
Equation

D.19
becomes

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{\overline{\Sigma}}{\gamma(a)-\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{{\mathcal{T}}(\overline{Y})}{a-{\mathcal{T}}(\overline{Y})}\right]\overline{\Sigma}}\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{{\mathcal{T}}(\overline{Y})^{2}}{(a-{\mathcal{T}}(\overline{Y}))^{2}}\right]\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{\overline{\Sigma}^{2}}{\left(\gamma(a)-\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{{\mathcal{T}}(\overline{Y})}{a-{\mathcal{T}}(\overline{Y})}\right]\overline{\Sigma}\right)^{2}}\right].

The left-hand side equals
\delta
since
\gamma(a)
satisfies
Equation

3.3
.
Therefore the above equation matches
Equation

D.15
.

Next, assuming that
Equation

D.17
holds, we verify
Equation

5.7
.
For any
a>a^{\circ}
,
\zeta(a)=\psi(a)
, hence
Equation

D.17
can be written as

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\mathbb{E}\left[\overline{\Sigma}\right]}\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{\overline{G}^{2}{\mathcal{T}}(\overline{Y})}{a-{\mathcal{T}}(\overline{Y})}\right]\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{\overline{\Sigma}^{2}}{\gamma(a)-\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{{\mathcal{T}}(\overline{Y})}{a-{\mathcal{T}}(\overline{Y})}\right]\overline{\Sigma}}\right]

\displaystyle=\gamma(a),

or equivalently,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\mathbb{E}\left[\overline{\Sigma}\right]}\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{\delta}{\mathbb{E}\left[\overline{\Sigma}\right]}\overline{G}^{2}\frac{{\mathcal{T}}(\overline{Y})}{a-{\mathcal{T}}(\overline{Y})}\right]\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{\overline{\Sigma}^{2}}{\gamma(a)-\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{{\mathcal{T}}(\overline{Y})}{a-{\mathcal{T}}(\overline{Y})}\right]\overline{\Sigma}}\right]

\displaystyle=\frac{\delta\gamma(a)}{\mathbb{E}\left[\overline{\Sigma}\right]}.

To show that the above equation is the same as
Equation

5.7
, it suffices to verify

\displaystyle\frac{\delta\gamma(a)}{\mathbb{E}\left[\overline{\Sigma}\right]}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\mathbb{E}\left[\overline{\Sigma}\right]}\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{{\mathcal{T}}(\overline{Y})}{a-{\mathcal{T}}(\overline{Y})}\right]\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{\overline{\Sigma}^{2}}{\gamma(a)-\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{{\mathcal{T}}(\overline{Y})}{a-{\mathcal{T}}(\overline{Y})}\right]\overline{\Sigma}}\right]+1.

(D.21)

We rewrite the first term on the right-hand side as

\displaystyle\phantom{=}~\frac{1}{\mathbb{E}\left[\overline{\Sigma}\right]}\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{{\mathcal{T}}(\overline{Y})}{a-{\mathcal{T}}(\overline{Y})}\right]\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{\overline{\Sigma}^{2}}{\gamma(a)-\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{{\mathcal{T}}(\overline{Y})}{a-{\mathcal{T}}(\overline{Y})}\right]\overline{\Sigma}}\right]

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\mathbb{E}\left[\overline{\Sigma}\right]}\frac{1}{\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{{\mathcal{T}}(\overline{Y})}{a-{\mathcal{T}}(\overline{Y})}\right]}\left(\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{{\mathcal{T}}(\overline{Y})}{a-{\mathcal{T}}(\overline{Y})}\right]^{2}\overline{\Sigma}^{2}-\gamma(a)\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{{\mathcal{T}}(\overline{Y})}{a-{\mathcal{T}}(\overline{Y})}\right]\overline{\Sigma}}{\gamma(a)-\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{{\mathcal{T}}(\overline{Y})}{a-{\mathcal{T}}(\overline{Y})}\right]\overline{\Sigma}}\right]+\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{\gamma(a)\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{{\mathcal{T}}(\overline{Y})}{a-{\mathcal{T}}(\overline{Y})}\right]\overline{\Sigma}}{\gamma(a)-\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{{\mathcal{T}}(\overline{Y})}{a-{\mathcal{T}}(\overline{Y})}\right]\overline{\Sigma}}\right]\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\mathbb{E}\left[\overline{\Sigma}\right]}\frac{1}{\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{{\mathcal{T}}(\overline{Y})}{a-{\mathcal{T}}(\overline{Y})}\right]}\left(-\mathbb{E}\left[\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{{\mathcal{T}}(\overline{Y})}{a-{\mathcal{T}}(\overline{Y})}\right]\overline{\Sigma}\right]+\gamma(a)\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{{\mathcal{T}}(\overline{Y})}{a-{\mathcal{T}}(\overline{Y})}\right]\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{\overline{\Sigma}}{\gamma(a)-\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{{\mathcal{T}}(\overline{Y})}{a-{\mathcal{T}}(\overline{Y})}\right]\overline{\Sigma}}\right]\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\mathbb{E}\left[\overline{\Sigma}\right]}\left(\gamma(a)\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{\overline{\Sigma}}{\gamma(a)-\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{{\mathcal{T}}(\overline{Y})}{a-{\mathcal{T}}(\overline{Y})}\right]\overline{\Sigma}}\right]-\mathbb{E}\left[\overline{\Sigma}\right]\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{\gamma(a)}{\mathbb{E}\left[\overline{\Sigma}\right]}\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{\overline{\Sigma}}{\gamma(a)-\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{{\mathcal{T}}(\overline{Y})}{a-{\mathcal{T}}(\overline{Y})}\right]\overline{\Sigma}}\right]-1.

Noting that
\gamma(a)
satisfies
Equation

3.3
, we further obtain

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\mathbb{E}\left[\overline{\Sigma}\right]}\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{{\mathcal{T}}(\overline{Y})}{a-{\mathcal{T}}(\overline{Y})}\right]\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{\overline{\Sigma}^{2}}{\gamma(a)-\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{{\mathcal{T}}(\overline{Y})}{a-{\mathcal{T}}(\overline{Y})}\right]\overline{\Sigma}}\right]

\displaystyle=\frac{\delta\gamma(a)}{\mathbb{E}\left[\overline{\Sigma}\right]}-1.

This then implies
Equation

D.21
and hence
Equation

5.7
.
∎

Finally, we derive an alternative form of
Equation

5.40
in terms of
a^{\circ},\gamma^{\circ}
defined through a pair of self-consistent equations. The proof follows from verifying that
\psi^{\prime}(a^{\circ})=0
is algebraically equivalent to
Equation

D.15
, as shown in
Proposition

D.4
above.

Lemma D.5
.

The description of
\sup\supp(\overline{\mu}_{\widehat{D}})
in
Lemma

5.5
is equivalent to

\sup\supp(\overline{\mu}_{\widehat{D}})=a^{\circ}\gamma^{\circ}
where
(a^{\circ},\gamma^{\circ})\in{\mathcal{A}}
solves
Equation

D.15
,
and
a^{\circ}
is the largest among all such solutions.

D.3
Alternative formulations of
a^{*}>a^{\circ}

The following proposition is a direct consequence of the monotonicity properties of
\psi,\varphi
(see
Proposition

D.2
and
Lemma

E.1
).

Proposition D.6
.

The following conditions are equivalent.

1.

a^{*}>a^{\circ}
;

2.

\zeta(a^{*})>\zeta(a^{\circ})
;

3.

\psi^{\prime}(a^{*})>0
, or more explicitly

\displaystyle 1

\displaystyle>\frac{1}{\delta}\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\frac{{\mathcal{T}}(\overline{Y})}{a^{*}-{\mathcal{T}}(\overline{Y})}\right)^{2}\right]\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{\overline{\Sigma}^{2}}{\left(\gamma^{*}-\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{{\mathcal{T}}(\overline{Y})}{a^{*}-{\mathcal{T}}(\overline{Y})}\right]\overline{\Sigma}\right)^{2}}\right],

(D.22)

i.e.,
1>w_{2}
by recalling the definition of
w_{2}
in
Equation

3.10
;

4.

If the function
\varphi\colon(\sup\supp({\mathcal{T}}(\overline{Y})),\infty)\to{\mathbb{R}}
defined in
Equation

3.2
is strictly decreasing, the above conditions are further equivalent to
\psi(a^{\circ})<\varphi(a^{\circ})
, or more explicitly

\displaystyle 1

\displaystyle<\frac{1}{\mathbb{E}\left[\overline{\Sigma}\right]}\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\frac{\delta}{\mathbb{E}\left[\overline{\Sigma}\right]}\overline{G}^{2}-1\right)\frac{{\mathcal{T}}(\overline{Y})}{a^{\circ}-{\mathcal{T}}(\overline{Y})}\right]\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{\overline{\Sigma}^{2}}{\gamma^{\circ}-\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{{\mathcal{T}}(\overline{Y})}{a^{\circ}-{\mathcal{T}}(\overline{Y})}\right]\overline{\Sigma}}\right].

(D.23)

Appendix E
Proof of Lemma
5.5

Recall from
Equation

5.38
the definition of
\widehat{D}\in\mathbb{R}^{d\times d}
:

\widehat{D}=\Sigma^{1/2}\widehat{X}^{\top}T\widehat{X}\Sigma^{1/2}.

We already know that both
\lambda_{1}(\widehat{D})
and
\lambda_{3}(\widehat{D})
converge to the upper edge
\lambda^{\circ}=\sup\supp\overline{\mu}_{\widehat{D}}
of the limiting spectrum (see
Lemmas

A.3
and
A.4
).
The main goal of this section is to prove the characterization of the upper edge
\lambda^{\circ}
in
Lemma

5.5
. We deduce
Lemma

5.5
from the following lemma. We present the proofs of
Lemmas

5.5
and
E.1
at the end of this appendix.

Lemma E.1
.

Let
a\in(\sup\supp\overline{\mu}_{T},\infty)
. Then, the following holds:

1.

If
\psi(\widetilde{a})>\lambda^{\circ}
for all
\widetilde{a}\geq a
, then
\psi^{\prime}(a)>0
;

2.

If
\psi^{\prime}(a)>0
, then
\psi(a)\notin\supp\overline{\mu}_{\widehat{D}}
.

We will see in
Lemma

E.3
that
a^{\circ}
is indeed well-defined. More precisely,
\psi
is an analytic function with at least one critical point, and
\psi^{\prime}(a)
converges to a positive number as
a\to\infty
.

E.1
Properties of
\psi

Recall that
\psi:(\sup\supp\overline{\mu}_{T},\infty)\to\mathbb{R}
is defined by
\psi(a)=a\gamma(a)
. With a slight modification to the definition of
\gamma(a)
, we have the following result.

Lemma E.2
.

1.

The sets
{\mathcal{S}},{\mathcal{S}}^{\prime}\subset\mathbb{R}
defined by

\displaystyle{\mathcal{S}}

\displaystyle\coloneqq\left\{a>\sup\supp\overline{\mu}_{T}:\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{{\mathcal{T}}(\overline{Y})}{{\mathcal{T}}(\overline{Y})-a}\right]=0\right\},

\displaystyle{\mathcal{S}}^{\prime}

\displaystyle\coloneqq\left\{a>\sup\supp\overline{\mu}_{T}:\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{{\mathcal{T}}(\overline{Y})}{{\mathcal{T}}(\overline{Y})-a}\right]=\frac{1}{\delta}\right\}

are finite.

2.

For each
a\in(\sup\supp\overline{\mu}_{T},\infty)\setminus{\mathcal{S}}
, there exists a unique
\omega\equiv\omega(a)\in\mathbb{R}\setminus(\inf\supp\overline{\mu}_{\Sigma},\sup\supp\overline{\mu}_{\Sigma})
such that

\delta\int_{\mathbb{R}}\frac{t}{t-a}\mathrm{d}\overline{\mu}_{T}(t)=\int_{\mathbb{R}}\frac{s}{s-\omega}\mathrm{d}\overline{\mu}_{\Sigma}(s).

(E.1)

3.

The map
\omega:(\sup\supp\overline{\mu}_{T},\infty)\setminus{\mathcal{S}}\to\mathbb{R}
defined in
Item

2
extends meromorphically to an open set in
\mathbb{C}
containing
(\sup\supp\overline{\mu}_{T},\infty)
. The extension is analytic at each
a\in(\sup\supp\overline{\mu}_{T},\infty)\setminus{\mathcal{S}}
, has a pole at each
a\in{\mathcal{S}}
and a zero at each
a\in{\mathcal{S}}^{\prime}
.

4.

The function
\psi:(\sup\supp\overline{\mu}_{T},\infty)\to\mathbb{R}
defined by
\psi(a)=a\gamma(a)
satisfies

\psi(a)=-\frac{a}{\delta}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\frac{s\omega(a)}{s-\omega(a)}\mathrm{d}\overline{\mu}_{\Sigma}(s),\qquad\forall a\in(\sup\supp\overline{\mu}_{T},\infty)\setminus{\mathcal{S}}.

(E.2)

Furthermore,
\psi
extends analytically to an open set in
\mathbb{C}
containing
(\sup\supp\overline{\mu}_{T},\infty)
, and has zeros precisely at
{\mathcal{S}}^{\prime}
.

Proof.

Note that the function
a\mapsto\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{{\mathcal{T}}(\overline{Y})}{{\mathcal{T}}(\overline{Y})-a}\right]
is analytic in
(\sup\supp\overline{\mu}_{T},\infty)
, so both
{\mathcal{S}}
and
{\mathcal{S}}^{\prime}
cannot have accumulating points in
(\sup\supp\overline{\mu}_{T},\infty)
. Thus, in order to prove
Item

1
, it suffices to prove that
{\mathcal{S}},{\mathcal{S}}^{\prime}
are contained in a compact subset of
(\sup\supp\overline{\mu}_{T},\infty)
. By the assumptions on
{\mathcal{T}}
(
(d)
in
Equation

2.7
) we have

\lim_{a\searrow\sup\supp\overline{\mu}_{T}}\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{{\mathcal{T}}(\overline{Y})}{{\mathcal{T}}(\overline{Y})-a}\right]=-\infty,

hence
{\mathcal{S}}
and
{\mathcal{S}}^{\prime}
are contained in
[x,\infty)
for some
x>\sup\supp\overline{\mu}_{T}
. Also, we have the series expansion

\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{{\mathcal{T}}(\overline{Y})}{{\mathcal{T}}(\overline{Y})-a}\right]=-\frac{\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathcal{T}}(\overline{Y})\right]}{a}-\frac{\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathcal{T}}(\overline{Y})^{2}\right]}{a^{2}}+{\mathcal{O}}(a^{-3}),\qquad\text{as }a\to\infty,

where
\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathcal{T}}(\overline{Y})^{2}\right]>0
by the assumption in
Equation

2.4
.
This already proves that
{\mathcal{S}}^{\prime}
is bounded, as
\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{{\mathcal{T}}(\overline{Y})}{{\mathcal{T}}(\overline{Y})-a}\right]
converges to
0
as
a\to\infty
. Similarly, the same expansion implies that for large enough
x>\sup\supp\overline{\mu}_{T}
we have

\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{{\mathcal{T}}(\overline{Y})}{{\mathcal{T}}(\overline{Y})-a}\right]\in\begin{cases}(0,\infty),&\text{if }\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathcal{T}}(\overline{Y})\right]<0,\\
(-\infty,0),&\text{if }\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathcal{T}}(\overline{Y})\right]\geq 0,\end{cases}\qquad\forall a>x.

Thus,
{\mathcal{S}}\cap[x,\infty)=\emptyset
. This concludes
Item

1
.

For
Item

2
, we only need to notice that the right-hand side of
Equation

E.1
is a bijection between
\mathbb{R}\setminus(\inf\supp\overline{\mu}_{\Sigma},\sup\supp\overline{\mu}_{\Sigma})
and
\mathbb{R}\setminus\{0\}
. Notice further that the right-hand side is analytic in
\omega
with strictly positive derivative whenever
\omega
is well-defined;

\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}\omega}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\frac{s}{s-\omega}\mathrm{d}\overline{\mu}_{\Sigma}=\int_{\mathbb{R}}\frac{s}{(s-\omega)^{2}}\mathrm{d}\overline{\mu}_{\Sigma}.

We now turn to
Item

3
. Since the left-hand side of
Equation

E.1
is an analytic function of
a
, it immediately follows from analytic inverse function theorem that
\omega
extends analytically to a neighborhood of
(\sup\supp\overline{\mu}_{T},\infty)\setminus{\mathcal{S}}
. Similarly, for each
a>\sup\supp{\mathcal{T}}(\overline{Y})
with
a\notin{\mathcal{S}}\cup{\mathcal{S}}^{\prime}
, we find that
\widetilde{\omega}(a)\coloneqq 1/\omega(a)
solves

\delta\int_{\mathbb{R}}\frac{t}{t-a}\mathrm{d}\overline{\mu}_{T}(t)=-\widetilde{\omega}(a)\int_{\mathbb{R}}\frac{s}{1-s\widetilde{\omega}(a)}\mathrm{d}\overline{\mu}_{\Sigma}.

Defining
\widetilde{\omega}(a)\equiv 0
for
a\in{\mathcal{S}}
and following the same reasoning as for
\omega
, one easily finds that
\widetilde{\omega}
extends analytically to a neighborhood of
(\sup\supp\overline{\mu}_{T},\infty)\setminus{\mathcal{S}}^{\prime}
. By analytic continuation,
\omega
extends to a meromorphic function on a neighborhood of
(\sup\supp\overline{\mu}_{T},\infty)
with poles at
{\mathcal{S}}
. From
Equation

E.1
we immediately find that the zeros of
\omega
are exactly at
{\mathcal{S}}^{\prime}
.

Finally, for
Item

4
, note that by a trivial rescaling we have

-\omega(a)\int_{\mathbb{R}}\frac{t}{t-a}\mathrm{d}\overline{\mu}_{T}(t)=\gamma(a),

which implies

\psi(a)=-a\omega(a)\int_{\mathbb{R}}\frac{t}{t-a}\mathrm{d}\overline{\mu}_{T}(t),\qquad a\notin{\mathcal{S}}.

(E.3)

Using the definition of
\omega
, we immediately have
Equation

E.2
from
Equation

E.3
. Also,
Equation

E.3
already shows that
\psi
is a meromorphic function on a neighborhood of
(\sup\supp\overline{\mu}_{T},\infty)
by
Item

2
, with possible poles at
{\mathcal{S}}
. Hence we only need to check that each
a\in{\mathcal{S}}
is a removable singularity for
\psi
. Recall that
\omega(z)\to\infty
as
z\to a\in{\mathcal{S}}
, so that by dominated convergence

\displaystyle\psi(z)

\displaystyle=-\frac{z}{\delta}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\frac{s\omega(z)}{s-\omega(z)}\mathrm{d}\overline{\mu}_{\Sigma}(s)=-\frac{z}{\delta}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\frac{s}{s/\omega(z)-1}\mathrm{d}\overline{\mu}_{\Sigma}(s)\to\frac{a}{\delta}\mathbb{E}\left[\overline{\Sigma}\right].

∎

Lemma E.3
.

We have

\lim_{a\to\infty}\psi^{\prime}(a)=\frac{\mathbb{E}\left[\overline{\Sigma}\right]}{\delta}=\lim_{\re a\to\infty}\frac{\im\psi(a)}{\im a},

(E.4)

where we identified
\psi
with its analytic extension. We also have

\lim_{a\to\infty}\psi(a)=\infty=\lim_{a\searrow\sup\supp\overline{\mu}_{T}}\psi(a).

(E.5)

In particular, the set of critical points of
\psi
is nonempty and bounded from above (as a subset of
{\mathbb{R}}
).

Proof.

We compute the derivative of
\psi
as

\displaystyle\begin{split}\delta\psi^{\prime}(a)&=-\int_{\mathbb{R}}\frac{s\omega(a)}{s-\omega(a)}\mathrm{d}\overline{\mu}_{\Sigma}(s)-a\omega^{\prime}(a)\int_{\mathbb{R}}\frac{s^{2}}{(s-\omega(a))^{2}}\mathrm{d}\overline{\mu}_{\Sigma}(s)\\
&=-\int_{\mathbb{R}}\frac{s\omega(a)}{s-\omega(a)}\mathrm{d}\overline{\mu}_{\Sigma}(s)\\
&\phantom{=}~-a\delta\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}}\frac{s}{(s-\omega(a))^{2}}\mathrm{d}\overline{\mu}_{\Sigma}(s)\right)^{-1}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\frac{t}{(t-a)^{2}}\mathrm{d}\overline{\mu}_{T}(t)\int_{\mathbb{R}}\frac{s^{2}}{(s-\omega(a))^{2}}\mathrm{d}\overline{\mu}_{\Sigma}(s).\end{split}

(E.6)

Furthermore, notice from
Item

2
of
Lemma

E.2
that
|\omega(a)|\to\infty
as
a\to\infty
, so that the second term in
Equation

E.6
satisfies

\displaystyle\lim_{a\to\infty}\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}}\frac{s\omega(a)^{2}}{(s-\omega(a))^{2}}\mathrm{d}\overline{\mu}_{\Sigma}(s)\right)^{-1}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\frac{s^{2}\omega(a)^{2}}{(s-\omega(a))^{2}}\mathrm{d}\overline{\mu}_{\Sigma}(s)\cdot\int_{\mathbb{R}}\frac{ta}{(t-a)^{2}}\mathrm{d}\overline{\mu}_{T}(t)

\displaystyle=\frac{\mathbb{E}\left[\overline{\Sigma}^{2}\right]}{\mathbb{E}\left[\overline{\Sigma}\right]}\lim_{a\to\infty}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\frac{ta}{(t-a)^{2}}\mathrm{d}\overline{\mu}_{T}(t)=0.

Therefore, we conclude that the first equality in
Equation

E.4
holds as

\lim_{a\to\infty}\psi^{\prime}(a)=\frac{1}{\delta}\lim_{a\to\infty}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\frac{-s\omega(a)}{s-\omega(a)}\mathrm{d}\overline{\mu}_{\Sigma}(s)=\frac{1}{\delta}\mathbb{E}\left[\overline{\Sigma}\right].

The second equality can be proved analogously, except that the following identity replaces
Equation

E.6
:

\displaystyle\delta\frac{\im\psi(a)}{\im a}

\displaystyle=-\re\left[\int_{\mathbb{R}}\frac{s\omega(a)}{s-\omega(a)}\mathrm{d}\overline{\mu}_{\Sigma}(s)\right]

\displaystyle-\delta\re[a]\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}}\frac{s}{|s-\omega(a)|^{2}}\mathrm{d}\overline{\mu}_{\Sigma}(s)\right)^{-1}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\frac{t}{|t-a|^{2}}\mathrm{d}\overline{\mu}_{T}(t)\int_{\mathbb{R}}\frac{s^{2}}{|s-\omega(a)|^{2}}\mathrm{d}\overline{\mu}_{\Sigma}(s),

where we used

\frac{\im\omega(a)}{\im a}=\delta\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}}\frac{s}{|s-\omega(a)|^{2}}\mathrm{d}\overline{\mu}_{\Sigma}(s)\right)^{-1}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\frac{t}{|t-a|^{2}}\mathrm{d}\overline{\mu}_{T}(t),

from
Equation

E.1
.

Notice that the first equality in
Equation

E.5
follows from the first equality in
Equation

E.4
. For the second equality in
Equation

E.5
, recall from the assumption
(d)
in
Equation

2.7
that

\lim_{a\searrow\sup\supp\overline{\mu}_{T}}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\frac{t}{t-a}\mathrm{d}\overline{\mu}_{T}(t)=-\infty,

which implies
\lim_{a\searrow\sup\supp\overline{\mu}_{T}}\omega(a)=\sup\supp\overline{\mu}_{\Sigma}
via
Item

2
of
Lemma

E.2
. Plugging these in the definition of
\psi
in
Equation

E.2
and using
\sup\supp\overline{\mu}_{T}>0
prove
\psi(a)\to\infty
.
∎

E.2
Complex analytic characterization of
\overline{\mu}_{\widehat{D}}

Lemma E.4

(
[
Zha07
, Theorem 1.2.1]
)
.

Let
m_{\overline{\mu}_{\widehat{D}}}
denote the Stieltjes transform of the limiting eigenvalue distribution
\overline{\mu}_{\widehat{D}}
.
For each
z\in{\mathbb{H}}\coloneqq\left\{z\in{\mathbb{C}}:\Im(z)>0\right\}
,
m=m_{\overline{\mu}_{\widehat{D}}}(z)
is characterized as the unique solution
(m,m_{1},m_{2})
of the following system of equations:

\begin{cases}\displaystyle-zm=(1-\delta)+\delta\int_{\mathbb{R}}\frac{1}{1+m_{1}t}\mathrm{d}\overline{\mu}_{T}(t),\\
\displaystyle-zm=\int_{\mathbb{R}}\frac{1}{1+m_{2}s}\mathrm{d}\overline{\mu}_{\Sigma}(s),\\
\displaystyle-zm=1+\delta zm_{1}m_{2},\end{cases}

(E.7)

subject to the constraint
m,m_{1},zm_{2}\in{\mathbb{H}}
. All of
m,m_{1},m_{2}
are analytic in
{\mathbb{H}}
as a function of
z
.

We adopt the notation
m(\overline{z})=\overline{m}(z)
and
m_{i}(\overline{z})=\overline{m_{i}(z)}
(
i\in\{1,2\}
).
The major difference from the case of positive
{\mathcal{T}}
is that
m_{2}
might not be in
{\mathbb{H}}
; still the second equation in
Equation

E.7
is well-defined as
m_{2}(z)\in\{z^{-1}w:w\in{\mathbb{H}}\}\subset\mathbb{C}\setminus(-\infty,0]
. (Cf., when
{\mathcal{T}}
is positive then
m_{i}\in{\mathbb{H}}
and
zm_{i}\in{\mathbb{H}}
for both
i\in\{1,2\}
.)
Alternatively, using the last equation in
Equation

E.7
to substitute
m
in the first two equations, we may write the system of two equations for
m_{1},m_{2}
:

\begin{cases}\displaystyle-zm_{1}=\frac{1}{\delta}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\frac{s}{1+m_{2}s}\mathrm{d}\overline{\mu}_{\Sigma}(s),\\
\displaystyle-zm_{2}=\int_{\mathbb{R}}\frac{t}{1+m_{1}t}\mathrm{d}\overline{\mu}_{T}(t).\end{cases}

(E.8)

For later purposes, we define for all
z,w\in{\mathbb{C}}\setminus\mathbb{R}
,

\displaystyle I_{1}(z,w)

\displaystyle\coloneqq\int_{\mathbb{R}}\frac{t^{2}}{(1+m_{1}(z)t)(1+m_{1}(w)t)}\mathrm{d}\overline{\mu}_{T}(t),

(E.9)

\displaystyle I_{2}(z,w)

\displaystyle\coloneqq\int_{\mathbb{R}}\frac{s^{2}}{(1+m_{2}(z)s)(1+m_{2}(w)s)}\mathrm{d}\overline{\mu}_{\Sigma}(s),

so that
I_{1}(z,\overline{z})
and
I_{2}(z,\overline{z})
are positive since
m_{i}(\overline{z})=\overline{m_{i}(z)}
. Note also that

\displaystyle|zm_{1}(z)|\leq\delta^{-1}I_{2}(z,\overline{z})^{1/2},\qquad|zm_{2}(z)|\leq I_{1}(z,\overline{z})^{1/2},

(E.10)

by Cauchy–Schwarz.

Lemma E.5
.

For all
z\in{\mathbb{H}}
,

\frac{1}{\delta|z|^{2}}I_{1}(z,\overline{z})I_{2}(z,\overline{z})<1.

(E.11)

Consequently,

\displaystyle|m_{1}(z)|^{2}I_{1}(z,\overline{z})<\frac{1}{\delta},\qquad|m_{2}(z)|^{2}I_{2}(z,\overline{z})<\delta.

(E.12)

Proof.

Dividing the first line of
Equation

E.8
by
z
and then taking imaginary parts, we get

\Im m_{1}(z)=\frac{1}{\delta}\Im\int_{\mathbb{R}}\frac{s}{-z(1+m_{2}(z)s)}\mathrm{d}\overline{\mu}_{\Sigma}(s)=\frac{1}{\delta}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\frac{s\Im z+s^{2}\Im zm_{2}(z)}{|z|^{2}|1+m_{2}(z)s|^{2}}\mathrm{d}\overline{\mu}_{\Sigma}(t).

(E.13)

Similarly taking the imaginary part of the second line of
Equation

E.8
gives

\Im zm_{2}(z)=-\Im\int_{\mathbb{R}}\frac{t}{1+m_{1}(z)t}\mathrm{d}\overline{\mu}_{T}(t)=\int_{\mathbb{R}}\frac{t^{2}\Im m_{1}(z)}{|1+m_{1}(z)t|^{2}}\mathrm{d}\overline{\mu}_{T}(t).

(E.14)

Combining
Equations

E.13
and
E.14
, we obtain

\displaystyle\begin{split}\delta\Im m_{1}(z)&=\int_{\mathbb{R}}\frac{s\Im z}{|z|^{2}|1+m_{2}(z)s|^{2}}\mathrm{d}\overline{\mu}_{\Sigma}(s)\\
&\phantom{=}~+\frac{\Im m_{1}(z)}{|z|^{2}}\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}}\frac{t^{2}}{|1+m_{1}(z)t|^{2}}\mathrm{d}\overline{\mu}_{T}(t)\right)\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}}\frac{s^{2}}{|1+m_{2}(z)s|^{2}}\mathrm{d}\overline{\mu}_{\Sigma}(s)\right).\end{split}

(E.15)

Since
\im m_{1}(z)
and the first term on the right-hand side of
Equation

E.15
are positive for all
z\in{\mathbb{H}}
, we have proved
Equation

E.11
:

\frac{1}{\delta|z|^{2}}\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}}\frac{t^{2}}{|1+m_{1}(z)t|^{2}}\mathrm{d}\overline{\mu}_{T}(t)\right)\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}}\frac{s^{2}}{|1+m_{2}(z)s|^{2}}\mathrm{d}\overline{\mu}_{\Sigma}(s)\right)<1,\qquad\forall z\in{\mathbb{H}}.

For
Equation

E.12
, we only need to notice from
Equations

E.11
and
E.10
that

|m_{1}|^{2}I_{1}(z,\overline{z})\leq\frac{1}{\delta^{2}|z|^{2}}I_{1}(z,\overline{z})I_{2}(z,\overline{z})<\frac{1}{\delta},

and the second line in
Equation

E.12
follows similarly.
∎

Note also that
Equation

E.11
implies for all
z\in{\mathbb{H}}
that

\displaystyle|zm(z)+1|\leq\frac{\delta}{|z|}|zm_{1}(z)||zm_{2}(z)|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{|z|}\sqrt{I_{1}(z,\overline{z})I_{2}(z,\overline{z})}\leq\sqrt{\delta},

(E.16)

where we used the third line of
Equation

E.7
in the first,
Equation

E.10
in the second, and
Equation

E.11
in the last inequality.

Lemma E.6
.

Let
{\mathcal{D}}\subset{\mathbb{H}}
be bounded. Then, there exists a constant
K>0
depending only on
{\mathcal{D}}
,
\overline{\mu}_{\Sigma}
, and
\overline{\mu}_{T}
such that

|zm_{1}(z)|\leq K,\qquad|zm_{2}(z)|\leq K,\qquad\forall z\in{\mathcal{D}}.

Proof.

We only consider
|zm_{1}(z)|
, and the same argument applies to
|zm_{2}(z)|
. The proof is by contradiction. Suppose that there exists a sequence
z_{k}
in
{\mathcal{D}}
such that
|z_{k}m_{1}(z_{k})|\to\infty
. Then by combining
Equation

E.16
with the third equation in
Equation

E.7
, we have
|m_{2}(z_{k})|\to 0
. Therefore by dominated convergence (together with
\sup\supp\overline{\mu}_{\Sigma}<\infty)
we have

-\delta\lim_{k\to\infty}z_{k}m_{1}(z_{k})=\lim_{k\to\infty}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\frac{s}{1+m_{2}(z_{k})s}\mathrm{d}\overline{\mu}_{\Sigma}(s)=\int_{\mathbb{R}}s\mathrm{d}\overline{\mu}_{\Sigma}(s)\in\mathbb{R},

which gives a contradiction to
|z_{k}m_{1}(z_{k})|\to\infty
.
∎

Lemma E.7
.

For all
z\in{\mathbb{H}}
, we have

0<(\inf\supp\overline{\mu}_{\Sigma})\leq\delta\frac{\im m_{1}(z)}{\im m(z)}\leq(\sup\supp\overline{\mu}_{\Sigma}).

(E.17)

For each bounded
{\mathcal{D}}\subset{\mathbb{H}}
, there exists a constant
K_{1}
depending only on
{\mathcal{D}},\overline{\mu}_{\Sigma}
, and
\overline{\mu}_{T}
such that

\im(zm_{2}(z))\leq K_{1}\im m_{1}(z),\qquad z\in{\mathcal{D}}.

(E.18)

Proof.

To see
Equation

E.17
, note that the second line of
Equation

E.7
implies

\im m(z)=\int_{\mathbb{R}}\im\left[\frac{1}{-z(1+m_{2}(z)s)}\right]\mathrm{d}\overline{\mu}_{\Sigma}(s).

(E.19)

Comparing
Equation

E.19
with
Equation

E.13
proves
Equation

E.17
.

For
Equation

E.18
, we recall from
Equations

E.14
and
E.11
that

\im zm_{2}(z)=\im m_{1}(z)\cdot I_{1}(z,\overline{z})\leq\im m_{1}(z)\cdot\frac{\delta|z|^{2}}{I_{2}(z,\overline{z})}.

By definition of
I_{2}(z,\overline{z})
, we have

\displaystyle\frac{|z|^{2}}{I_{2}(z,\overline{z})}

\displaystyle=\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}}\frac{s^{2}}{|z+zm_{2}(z)s|^{2}}\mathrm{d}\overline{\mu}_{\Sigma}(s)\right)^{-1}

\displaystyle\leq 2\left(\left[(\sup\supp\overline{\mu}_{\Sigma})\cdot|zm_{2}(z)|\right]^{2}+|z|^{2}\right)\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}}s^{2}\mathrm{d}\overline{\mu}_{\Sigma}(s)\right)^{-1}.

(E.20)

Since
{\mathcal{D}}
is bounded, the right-hand side of
Equation

E.20
is bounded by a constant for all
z\in{\mathcal{D}}
. This proves
Equation

E.18
.
∎

Proposition E.8
.

1.

There exist two finite measures
\nu_{1},\nu_{2}
on
\mathbb{R}
such that the following holds; for all
z\in{\mathbb{H}}
we have

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}}\frac{1}{x-z}\mathrm{d}\nu_{1}(x)

\displaystyle=m_{1}(z),

\displaystyle\nu_{1}(\mathbb{R})

\displaystyle=\frac{\mathbb{E}\left[\overline{\Sigma}\right]}{\delta},

(E.21)

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}}\frac{1}{x-z}\mathrm{d}\nu_{2}(x)

\displaystyle=zm_{2}(z)+\int_{\mathbb{R}}t\mathrm{d}\overline{\mu}_{T}(t),

\displaystyle\nu_{2}(\mathbb{R})

\displaystyle=\frac{\mathbb{E}\left[\overline{\Sigma}\right]}{\delta}\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathcal{T}}(\overline{Y})^{2}\right].

Consequently we have

\supp\nu_{1}=\supp\overline{\mu}_{\widehat{D}},\qquad\supp\nu_{2}\subset\supp\overline{\mu}_{\widehat{D}},

(E.22)

so that
m_{1}
and
m_{2}
are respectively analytic and meromorphic functions on
\mathbb{R}\setminus\supp\overline{\mu}_{\widehat{D}}
.

2.

For all
x>\lambda^{\circ}
, we have

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{m_{1}(x)}\in(\sup\supp\overline{\mu}_{T},\infty),\qquad-\frac{1}{m_{2}(x)}\in(\mathbb{R}\cup\{\infty\})\setminus(\inf\supp\overline{\mu}_{\Sigma},\sup\supp\overline{\mu}_{\Sigma}),

(E.23)

\displaystyle\limsup_{z\to x,z\in{\mathbb{H}}}\frac{1}{\delta|z|^{2}}I_{1}(z,\overline{z})I_{2}(z,\overline{z})<1,

where we used the convention
1/0=\infty
in the second assertion.

Proof.

We start with the proof of
Item

1
. First, notice that once
Equation

E.21
is proved,
Equation

E.22
immediately follows from
Lemma

E.7
and Stieltjes inversion.
In order to prove the first identity in
Equation

E.21
, since
m_{1}
is an analytic self-map of
{\mathbb{H}}
, by Nevanlinna–Pick representation theorem it suffices to check

\limsup_{\eta\to\infty}\eta|m_{1}(\mathrm{i}\eta)|<\infty.

(E.24)

Suppose the contrary, so that there exists a sequence
\eta_{k}\to\infty
with
\eta_{k}|m_{1}(\mathrm{i}\eta_{k})|\to\infty
. Then by
Equation

E.16
we find that
|m_{2}(\mathrm{i}\eta_{k})|\to 0
. On the other hand by
Equation

E.8
, we have

-\mathrm{i}\eta m_{1}(\mathrm{i}\eta)=\frac{1}{\delta}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\frac{s}{1+m_{2}(\mathrm{i}\eta)s}\mathrm{d}\overline{\mu}_{\Sigma}(s),

(E.25)

so that the dominated convergence theorem (with
\left\|\Sigma\right\|_{2}={\mathcal{O}}(1)
) leads to a contradiction as

\lim_{k\to\infty}\eta_{k}|m_{1}(\mathrm{i}\eta_{k})|=\frac{1}{\delta}\lim_{k\to\infty}\left|\int_{\mathbb{R}}\frac{s}{1+m_{2}(\mathrm{i}\eta_{k})s}\mathrm{d}\overline{\mu}_{\Sigma}(s)\right|=\frac{1}{\delta}\int_{\mathbb{R}}s\mathrm{d}\overline{\mu}_{\Sigma}(s).

Thus we have proved the first line of
Equation

E.21
.

Next, we prove the corresponding representation for
zm_{2}(z)
, the second line of
Equation

E.21
. As before, it suffices to prove

\limsup_{\eta\to\infty}\eta\left|\mathrm{i}\eta m_{2}(\mathrm{i}\eta)+\int_{\mathbb{R}}t\mathrm{d}\overline{\mu}_{T}(t)\right|<\infty.

(E.26)

To this end, we use
Equation

E.8
to write

z\left(zm_{2}(z)+\int_{\mathbb{R}}t\mathrm{d}\overline{\mu}_{T}(t)\right)=zm_{1}(z)\int_{\mathbb{R}}\frac{t^{2}}{1+m_{1}(z)t}\mathrm{d}\overline{\mu}_{T}(t).

(E.27)

Taking the limit along
z=\mathrm{i}\eta\to\mathrm{i}\infty
, by
Equation

E.21
we have
m_{1}(z)\to 0
and
zm_{1}(z)\to-\nu_{1}(\mathbb{R})
(note that
\nu_{1}(\mathbb{R})
is finite due to
Equation

E.24
), so that

\lim_{\eta\to\infty}\mathrm{i}\eta\left(\mathrm{i}\eta m_{2}(\mathrm{i}\eta)+\int_{\mathbb{R}}t\mathrm{d}\overline{\mu}_{T}(t)\right)=-\nu_{1}(\mathbb{R})\int_{\mathbb{R}}t^{2}\mathrm{d}\overline{\mu}_{T}(t).

Finally, given the two representations in
Equation

E.21
, we have
m_{1}(\mathrm{i}\eta),m_{2}(\mathrm{i}\eta)\to 0
as
\eta\to\infty
. Then
\nu_{1}(\mathbb{R})
and
\nu_{2}(\mathbb{R})
can be computed by taking the limits of
Equations

E.25
and
E.27
as
z=\mathrm{i}\eta\to\mathrm{i}\infty
. This completes the proof of
Item

1
.

Now we prove
Item

2
. Notice that
m_{1}
is analytic, negative-valued, and increasing on
(\lambda^{\circ},\infty)
, and that
\lim_{x\to\infty}m_{1}(x)=0
. Therefore the image of the half line
(\lambda^{\circ},\infty)
under
x\mapsto-1/m_{1}(x)
is again an half-line
(y_{0},\infty)
for some
y_{0}>0
. Next, notice from
Equation

E.12
that for all
x\in\mathbb{R}
,

\limsup_{z\to x,z\in{\mathbb{H}}}|m_{1}(z)|^{2}I_{1}(z,\overline{z})=\limsup_{z\to x,z\in{\mathbb{H}}}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\frac{t^{2}}{|t-(-1/m_{1}(z))|^{2}}\mathrm{d}\overline{\mu}_{T}(t)<\frac{1}{\delta}.

(E.28)

On the other hand, by the assumptions on
{\mathcal{T}}
(see
(d)
in
Equation

2.7
) and Cauchy–Schwarz, there exists an
\varepsilon>0
so that

\lim_{w\to y,w\in{\mathbb{H}}}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\frac{t^{2}}{|t-w|^{2}}\mathrm{d}\overline{\mu}_{T}(t)=\int_{\mathbb{R}}\frac{t^{2}}{|t-y|^{2}}\mathrm{d}\overline{\mu}_{T}(t)>\frac{1}{\delta},\quad\forall y\in(\sup\supp\overline{\mu}_{T},\sup\supp\overline{\mu}_{T}+\varepsilon).

(E.29)

Combining
Equations

E.28
and
E.29
, we conclude that
(y_{0},\infty)
does not intersect with
(\sup\supp\overline{\mu}_{T},\allowbreak\sup\supp\overline{\mu}_{T}+\varepsilon)
, so that
y_{0}\geq\sup\supp\overline{\mu}_{T}+\varepsilon
. This proves the first assertion of
Item

2
.

The proof of the second assertion in
Item

2
follows similar lines, except that we view
x\mapsto-1/m_{2}(x)
as an analytic (instead of meromorphic) function mapping into the Riemann sphere
\mathbb{C}\cup\{\infty\}
. Consequently, the closure of the image of
(\lambda^{\circ},\infty)
under
z\mapsto-1/m_{2}(z)
is a connected real interval in the Riemann sphere; or equivalently, it is the image of a closed connected arc in the unit circle under stereographic projection. Next, notice from the assumptions on
\overline{\Sigma}
(see
(b)
in
Equation

2.6
) that there exists an
\varepsilon>0
so that

\lim_{w\to y,w\in{\mathbb{H}}}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\frac{s^{2}}{|s-w|^{2}}\mathrm{d}\overline{\mu}_{\Sigma}(s)>\delta,

for all
y\in(\inf\supp\overline{\mu}_{\Sigma}-\varepsilon,\inf\supp\overline{\mu}_{\Sigma})\cup(\sup\supp\overline{\mu}_{\Sigma},\sup\supp\overline{\mu}_{\Sigma}+\varepsilon)
.
Therefore
Equation

E.12
implies that the image of
(\lambda^{\circ},\infty)
under
x\mapsto-1/m_{2}(x)
does not intersect with the two segments of length
\varepsilon
, while containing
\infty
in its closure since
m_{2}(x)\to 0
as
x\to\infty
. This proves the second assertion of
Item

2
.

For the final assertion of
Item

2
, recall from
Equation

E.15
that for all
z\in{\mathbb{H}}
,

\displaystyle 1-\frac{1}{\delta|z|^{2}}I_{1}(z,\overline{z})I_{2}(z,\overline{z})

\displaystyle=\frac{\im z}{\delta\im m_{1}(z)}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\frac{s}{|z|^{2}|1+m_{2}(z)s|^{2}}\mathrm{d}\overline{\mu}_{\Sigma}(s)

\displaystyle=\left(\delta\int_{\mathbb{R}}\frac{1}{|y-z|^{2}}\mathrm{d}\nu_{1}(y)\right)^{-1}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\frac{s}{|z|^{2}|1+m_{2}(z)s|^{2}}\mathrm{d}\overline{\mu}_{\Sigma}(s),

where we used
Equation

E.21
in the second equality. Taking the limit
z\to x>\lambda^{\circ}
, we have

\displaystyle 1-\limsup_{z\to x,z\in{\mathbb{H}}}\frac{1}{\delta|z|^{2}}I_{1}(z,\overline{z})I_{2}(z,\overline{z})

\displaystyle=\left(\delta\int_{\mathbb{R}}\frac{1}{|y-x|^{2}}\mathrm{d}\nu_{1}(y)\right)^{-1}

\displaystyle\phantom{=}~\cdot\int_{\mathbb{R}}s\left(x^{2}+s^{2}\limsup_{z\to x,z\in{\mathbb{H}}}|zm_{2}(z)|^{2}\right)^{-1}\mathrm{d}\overline{\mu}_{\Sigma}(s)>0,

where we used Fatou’s lemma in the first equality and
Lemma

E.6
in the last inequality. This concludes the proof of
Proposition

E.8
.
∎

E.3
Proof of
Lemmas

5.5
and
E.1

Proof of
Lemma

5.5
given
Lemma

E.1
.

Notice that since
a^{\circ}
is the largest critical point of
\psi
and
\lim_{a\to\infty}\psi^{\prime}(a)>0
, we find that
\psi^{\prime}(a)>0
for all
a\in(a^{\circ},\infty)
, i.e.
\psi
is strictly increasing on
[a^{\circ},\infty)
.

Next, we prove
\psi(a^{\circ})\leq\lambda^{\circ}
. Note from the contrapositive of
Item

1
of
Lemma

E.1
that if
a>\sup\supp\overline{\mu}_{{\mathcal{T}}}
and
\psi^{\prime}(a)\leq 0
, then there exists an
\widetilde{a}\geq a
such that
\psi(\widetilde{a})\leq\lambda^{\circ}
. We may apply this to the largest critical point
a^{\circ}
since
\psi^{\prime}(a^{\circ})=0
, so that
\psi(\widetilde{a})\leq\lambda^{\circ}
for some
\widetilde{a}\geq a^{\circ}
. As
\psi
is increasing in
[a^{\circ},\infty)
, we conclude
\psi(a^{\circ})\leq\psi(\widetilde{a})\leq\lambda^{\circ}

Conversely,
Item

2
of
Lemma

E.1
implies
(\psi(a^{\circ}),\infty)\cap\supp\overline{\mu}_{\widehat{D}}=\emptyset
, so that
\lambda^{\circ}\leq\psi(a^{\circ})
. Therefore we have
\psi(a^{\circ})=\lambda^{\circ}
.
∎

Proof of
Item

1
of
Lemma

E.1
.

Let
a\in(\sup\supp\overline{\mu}_{T},\infty)
satisfy the assumption of
Item

1
of
Lemma

E.1
, that is,
\psi(\widetilde{a})>\lambda^{\circ}
for all
\widetilde{a}\geq a
. First of all, we prove that there exists a complex neighborhood
U
of
[a,\infty)
such that

w=-1/m_{1}(\psi(w)),\qquad\omega(w)=-1/m_{2}(\psi(w)),\qquad\forall w\in U.

(E.30)

Here we remark that
\psi(a)>\lambda^{\circ}
by assumption, so that all four functions of
w
in
Equation

E.30
are well-defined by
Proposition

E.8
; those in the first and second equalities are analytic and meromorphic, respectively.

Recall from
Lemma

E.3
that for large enough
\widetilde{a}>a
, there exists a neighborhood
V
of
\widetilde{a}
so that
\im\psi(w)/\im w>0
for every
w\in V
. Then, it also follows that, for each
w\in V\cap{\mathbb{H}}
,

\im\left[-\frac{\psi(w)}{\omega(w)}\right]=\im\left[\int_{\mathbb{R}}\frac{tw}{t-w}\mathrm{d}\overline{\mu}_{T}(t)\right]=\im w\int_{\mathbb{R}}\frac{t^{2}}{|t-w|^{2}}\mathrm{d}\overline{\mu}_{T}(t)>0.

(E.31)

Also notice that the triple
(\psi(w),-1/w,-1/\omega(w))
satisfies the same system of equations as in
Equation

E.8
:

\displaystyle-\frac{\psi(w)}{w}=\frac{1}{\delta}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\frac{s\omega(w)}{s-\omega(w)}\mathrm{d}\overline{\mu}_{\Sigma}(s)=-\frac{1}{\delta}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\frac{s}{1+s\cdot(-\omega(w))^{-1}}\mathrm{d}\overline{\mu}_{\Sigma}(s),

(E.32)

\displaystyle\frac{\psi(w)}{\omega(w)}=-\int_{\mathbb{R}}\frac{tw}{t-w}\mathrm{d}\overline{\mu}_{T}(t)=-\int_{\mathbb{R}}\frac{t}{1+t\cdot(-w^{-1})}\mathrm{d}\overline{\mu}_{T}(t).

Therefore, by the uniqueness of the solution of
Equation

E.8
, we conclude

(\psi(w),-1/w,-1/\omega(w))=(\psi(w),m_{1}(\psi(w)),m_{2}(\psi(w))),\qquad w\in V\cap{\mathbb{H}}.

(E.33)

By
Proposition

E.8
and the assumption of
Item

1
, in both sides of
Equation

E.33
are meromorphic functions defined on a neighborhood of
[a,\infty)
, so that the identity holds in the whole (connected) neighborhood.

We now prove
\psi^{\prime}(a)>0
, provided
a\notin{\mathcal{S}}\cup{\mathcal{S}}^{\prime}
. Recall from
Equation

E.6
that

\displaystyle\delta\psi^{\prime}(a)

\displaystyle=\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}}\frac{s}{(s-\omega(a))^{2}}\mathrm{d}\overline{\mu}_{\Sigma}(s)\right)^{-1}

(E.34)

\displaystyle\cdot\left[-\delta\int_{\mathbb{R}}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\frac{t}{t-a}\frac{s\omega(a)}{(s-\omega(a))^{2}}+\frac{ta}{(t-a)^{2}}\frac{s^{2}}{(s-\omega(a))^{2}}\mathrm{d}\overline{\mu}_{\Sigma}(s)\mathrm{d}\overline{\mu}_{T}(t)\right].

Note that the second line in
Equation

E.34
can be written as

\displaystyle\begin{split}&-\delta\int_{\mathbb{R}}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\left[-\frac{t}{t-a}\frac{s}{s-\omega(a)}+\frac{t^{2}}{(t-a)^{2}}\frac{s^{2}}{(s-\omega(a))^{2}}\right]\mathrm{d}\overline{\mu}_{\Sigma}(s)\mathrm{d}\overline{\mu}_{T}(t)\\
&=\frac{\delta^{2}\psi(a)^{2}}{a^{2}\omega(a)^{2}}-\delta\int_{\mathbb{R}}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\frac{t^{2}}{(t-a)^{2}}\frac{s^{2}}{(s-\omega(a))^{2}}\mathrm{d}\overline{\mu}_{\Sigma}(s)\mathrm{d}\overline{\mu}_{T}(t).\end{split}

(E.35)

Then, we use
Equation

E.30
for
w=a
to substitute
a
and
\omega(a)
in
Equation

E.34
to obtain

\displaystyle\psi^{\prime}(a)

\displaystyle=\frac{\delta\psi(a)^{2}}{a^{2}\omega(a)^{2}}\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}}\frac{s}{(s-\omega(a))^{2}}\mathrm{d}\overline{\mu}_{\Sigma}(s)\right)^{-1}

(E.36)

\displaystyle\cdot\left(1-\frac{1}{\delta\psi(a)^{2}}I_{1}(\psi(a),\psi(a))I_{2}(\psi(a),\psi(a))\right)>0,

where we used
0<|m_{2}(\psi(a))|,|\psi(a)|<\infty
for
a\neq{\mathcal{S}}\cup{\mathcal{S}}^{\prime}
and
Equation

E.23
.

It only remains to prove
\psi^{\prime}(a)>0
for
a\in{\mathcal{S}}\cup{\mathcal{S}}^{\prime}
. Since
{\mathcal{S}}
and
{\mathcal{S}}^{\prime}
are both finite, we may consider a sequence
\widetilde{a}_{k}>a
such that
\widetilde{a}_{k}\notin{\mathcal{S}}\cup{\mathcal{S}}^{\prime}
and
\widetilde{a}_{k}\to a
. Since
\psi
is analytic at
a
and the second line of
Equation

E.36
is strictly positive by
Proposition

E.8
, is suffices to prove

\lim_{k\to\infty}\frac{\psi(\widetilde{a}_{k})^{2}}{\omega(\widetilde{a}_{k})^{2}}\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}}\frac{s}{(s-\omega(\widetilde{a}_{k}))^{2}}\mathrm{d}\overline{\mu}_{\Sigma}(s)\right)^{-1}>0.

If
a\in{\mathcal{S}}
so that
\omega(\widetilde{a}_{k})\to\infty
, we have

\displaystyle\lim_{k\to\infty}\frac{\psi(\widetilde{a}_{k})^{2}}{\omega(\widetilde{a}_{k})^{2}}\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}}\frac{s}{(s-\omega(\widetilde{a}_{k}))^{2}}\mathrm{d}\overline{\mu}_{\Sigma}(s)\right)^{-1}

\displaystyle=\psi(a)^{2}\lim_{k\to\infty}\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}}s\left(\frac{\omega(\widetilde{a}_{k})}{s-\omega(\widetilde{a}_{k})}\right)^{2}\mathrm{d}\overline{\mu}_{\Sigma}(s)\right)^{-1}=\frac{\psi(a)^{2}}{\mathbb{E}\left[\overline{\Sigma}\right]}>0,

where in the last inequality we used
a\in{\mathcal{S}}
implies
a\notin{\mathcal{S}}^{\prime}
, which in turn gives
\psi(a)\neq 0
. Finally for
a\in{\mathcal{S}}^{\prime}
, we use
\omega(\widetilde{a}_{k})\to 0
to write

\displaystyle\lim_{k\to\infty}\frac{\psi(\widetilde{a}_{k})^{2}}{\omega(\widetilde{a}_{k})^{2}}\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}}\frac{s}{(s-\omega(\widetilde{a}_{k}))^{2}}\mathrm{d}\overline{\mu}_{\Sigma}(s)\right)^{-1}=\frac{1}{\mathbb{E}\left[\overline{\Sigma}^{-1}\right]}\lim_{k\to\infty}\frac{\psi(\widetilde{a}_{k})^{2}}{\omega(\widetilde{a}_{k})^{2}}

\displaystyle=\frac{a^{2}}{\delta^{2}\mathbb{E}\left[\overline{\Sigma}^{-1}\right]}\lim_{k\to\infty}\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}}\frac{s}{s-\omega(\widetilde{a}_{k})}\mathrm{d}\overline{\mu}_{\Sigma}(s)\right)^{2}=\frac{a^{2}}{\delta^{2}\mathbb{E}\left[\overline{\Sigma}^{-1}\right]}>0,

where we used the definition of
\psi
in the second equality and
\inf\supp\overline{\mu}_{\Sigma}>0
in the last inequality. This concludes the proof of
Item

1
of
Lemma

E.1
.
∎

Proof
Item

2
of
Lemma

E.1
.

Since
\psi^{\prime}(a)>0
, there exist small neighborhoods
U
and
V
respectively of
a
and
\psi(a)
and an analytic inverse function
\psi^{-1}:V\to U
of
\psi
. We first prove that

(z,-1/\psi^{-1}(z),-1/\omega(\psi^{-1}(z)))=(z,m_{1}(z),m_{2}(z)),

(E.37)

for all
z\in V\cap{\mathbb{H}}
. Following
Equation

E.32
, we easily find that
(z,-1/\psi^{-1}(z),-1/\omega(\psi^{-1}(z)))
satisfies
Equation

E.8
. Also, there is an open subset
V^{\prime}\subset V\cap{\mathbb{H}}
so that
\im\psi^{-1}(z)>0
for all
z\in V^{\prime}
; to see this, we write

\im\psi^{-1}(z)=\im\left[(\psi^{-1})^{\prime}(\psi(a))\cdot(z-\psi(a))\right]+{\mathcal{O}}(|z-\psi(a)|^{2})=\frac{1}{\psi^{\prime}(a)}\im z+{\mathcal{O}}(|z-\psi(a)|^{2}).

Hence, it suffices to take
V^{\prime}=\{z:|z-\psi(a)|<2\im z<r\}
with small enough
r>0
in order to have
\psi^{-1}(V^{\prime})\subset{\mathbb{H}}
. Then, by
Equation

E.31
it also follows that
\im[-z/\omega(\psi^{-1}(z))]>0
. As in the proof of
Item

1
of
Lemma

E.1
, the uniqueness of the solution of
Equation

E.8
implies
Equation

E.37
for
z\in V^{\prime}
. Finally the conclusion extends to
V\cap{\mathbb{H}}
by analytic continuation.

Since
\psi
maps
(\sup\supp\overline{\mu}_{T},\infty)
to
\mathbb{R}
, its inverse function
\psi^{-1}
is real-valued on
V\cap\mathbb{R}
. Hence it follows

\lim_{\eta\to 0}\im m_{1}(x+\mathrm{i}\eta)=\lim_{\eta\to 0}\im\left[-\frac{1}{\psi^{-1}(x+\mathrm{i}\eta)}\right]=0,\qquad x\in V\cap\mathbb{R}.

Then, applying Stieltjes inversion to
Equation

E.21
, we have
\supp\nu_{1}\cap V=\emptyset
. Finally by
Equation

E.21
we conclude
\supp\overline{\mu}_{\widehat{D}}\cap V=\emptyset
, so that
\psi(a)\notin\supp\overline{\mu}_{\widehat{D}}
. This completes the proof of
Item

2
in
Lemma

E.1
.
∎

Appendix F
Performance of the whitened spectral estimator

In this section, we characterize the limiting overlap of the whitened spectral estimator, whose definition we recall from
Equation

4.1
:

\displaystyle\beta^{\mathrm{spec}}_{\smalltriangleup}(y,X,\Sigma)

\displaystyle\coloneqq\Sigma^{-1/2}v_{1}(D_{\smalltriangleup}),

(F.1)

where

\displaystyle D_{\smalltriangleup}

\displaystyle\coloneqq\sum_{i=1}^{n}(\Sigma^{-1/2}x_{i})(\Sigma^{-1/2}x_{i})^{\top}{\mathcal{T}}(y_{i})=\Sigma^{-1/2}X^{\top}TX\Sigma^{-1/2}=\widetilde{X}^{\top}T\widetilde{X}=\Sigma^{-1/2}D\Sigma^{-1/2}.

As discussed in
Section

4
, one can think of
\Sigma^{1/2}\beta^{*}
as an auxiliary parameter in the model
y=q(\widetilde{X}\Sigma^{1/2}\beta^{*},\varepsilon)
with design matrix
\widetilde{X}
.
Therefore, the top eigenvector of
D_{\smalltriangleup}=\widetilde{X}^{\top}\mathrm{diag}({\mathcal{T}}(q(\widetilde{X}\Sigma^{1/2}\beta^{*},\varepsilon)))\widetilde{X}
estimates
\Sigma^{1/2}\beta^{*}
and
\Sigma^{-1/2}v_{1}(D_{\smalltriangleup})
estimates
\beta^{*}
. We highlight that computing this spectral estimator requires knowledge of
\Sigma
.

As before, our results concerning
\beta^{\mathrm{spec}}_{\smalltriangleup}
are expressed in terms of a few functions and parameters.
Define
\varphi_{\smalltriangleup},\psi_{\smalltriangleup},\zeta_{\smalltriangleup}\colon(\sup\supp({\mathcal{T}}(\overline{Y})),\infty)\to{\mathbb{R}},a^{\circ}_{\smalltriangleup}\in(\sup\supp({\mathcal{T}}(\overline{Y})),\infty)
as

\displaystyle\varphi_{\smalltriangleup}(a)

\displaystyle=\frac{a\delta}{\mathbb{E}\left[\overline{\Sigma}\right]}\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{\overline{G}^{2}{\mathcal{T}}(\overline{Y})}{a-{\mathcal{T}}(\overline{Y})}\right],\quad\psi_{\smalltriangleup}(a)=a\left(\frac{1}{\delta}+\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{{\mathcal{T}}(\overline{Y})}{a-{\mathcal{T}}(\overline{Y})}\right]\right),

\displaystyle a^{\circ}_{\smalltriangleup}

\displaystyle=\argmin_{a\in(\sup\supp({\mathcal{T}}(\overline{Y})),\infty)}\psi_{\smalltriangleup}(a),\quad\zeta_{\smalltriangleup}(a)=\psi_{\smalltriangleup}(\max\{a,a^{\circ}_{\smalltriangleup}\}),

and
a^{*}_{\smalltriangleup}\in(\sup\supp({\mathcal{T}}(\overline{Y})),\infty)
as the unique solution to

\displaystyle\zeta_{\smalltriangleup}(a^{*}_{\smalltriangleup})=\varphi_{\smalltriangleup}(a^{*}_{\smalltriangleup}).

Both
a^{\circ}_{\smalltriangleup}
and
a^{*}_{\smalltriangleup}
are uniquely defined, as shown in
[
LL20
, Item 1 of Theorem 2.1]
and
[
MM19
, Item 1 of Lemma 2]
. In fact,
\sqrt{\frac{\delta}{\mathbb{E}\left[\overline{\Sigma}\right]}}\,\overline{G}\sim{\mathcal{N}}(0,1)
, so our functions
\varphi_{\smalltriangleup},\psi_{\smalltriangleup},\zeta_{\smalltriangleup}
match
\varphi,\psi,\zeta
in
[
LL20
]
by taking
\kappa
in
[
LL20
]
to be
\sqrt{\frac{\mathbb{E}\left[\overline{\Sigma}\right]}{\delta}}
.
The formula of the asymptotic overlap
\eta_{\smalltriangleup}
is:

\displaystyle\eta_{\smalltriangleup}

\displaystyle\coloneqq\left(\frac{1-\delta\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\frac{{\mathcal{T}}(\overline{Y})}{a^{*}_{\smalltriangleup}-{\mathcal{T}}(\overline{Y})}\right)^{2}\right]}{1+\delta\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{\delta}{\overline{\Sigma}}\right]\overline{G}^{2}-1\right)\left(\frac{{\mathcal{T}}(\overline{Y})}{a^{*}_{\smalltriangleup}-{\mathcal{T}}(\overline{Y})}\right)^{2}\right]}\right)^{1/2}.

Theorem F.1

(Whitened spectral estimator)
.

Consider the above setting and let
Items

(A1)
,
(A2)
,
(A3)
,
(A4)
and
(A5)
hold.
Suppose
a^{*}_{\smalltriangleup}>a^{\circ}_{\smalltriangleup}
.
Then, the top two eigenvalues
\lambda_{1}(D),\lambda_{2}(D)
of
D
satisfy

\displaystyle\plim_{d\to\infty}\lambda_{1}(D)

\displaystyle=\zeta(a^{*}_{\smalltriangleup}),\qquad\lim_{d\to\infty}\lambda_{2}(D)=\zeta(a^{\circ}_{\smalltriangleup})\quad\text{almost surely},

and
\zeta(a^{*}_{\smalltriangleup})>\zeta(a^{\circ}_{\smalltriangleup})
.
Furthermore, the limiting overlap between the spectral estimator
\beta^{\mathrm{spec}}_{\smalltriangleup}=\Sigma^{-1/2}v_{1}(D_{\smalltriangleup})
and
\beta^{*}
equals

\displaystyle\plim_{d\to\infty}\frac{\left|\left\langle\beta^{\mathrm{spec}}_{\smalltriangleup},\beta^{*}\right\rangle\right|}{\left\|\beta^{\mathrm{spec}}_{\smalltriangleup}\right\|_{2}\left\|\beta^{*}\right\|_{2}}

\displaystyle=\eta_{\smalltriangleup}>0.

We emphasize that, even if the spectral estimator is now computed with respect to
\widetilde{X}
whose rows have identity covariance, the observation
y
still depends on
\Sigma
through
y=q(\widetilde{X}\Sigma^{1/2}\beta^{*},\varepsilon)
and there is no easy way to further invert out
\Sigma^{1/2}
therein.
Thus, we cannot reduce to the
\Sigma=I_{d}
case studied in
[
LL20
,
MM19
]
, and we follow a strategy similar to that described in
Section

5
to prove
Theorem

3.1
.

Proof of
Theorem

F.1
.

Let us consider the generic GAMP iteration in
Equation

5.1
.
Let
{\mathcal{F}}_{\smalltriangleup}\colon{\mathbb{R}}\to{\mathbb{R}}
be an auxiliary preprocessing function to be chosen later.
Set

\displaystyle f_{t+1}(v^{t+1})

\displaystyle=\frac{v^{t+1}}{\beta_{t+1}},\quad t\geq 0,

(F.2)

for a sequence
(\beta_{t+1})_{t\geq 0}
to be specified later via state evolution.
One should think of the normalization
\beta_{t+1}>0
as

\beta_{t+1}=\lim_{d\to\infty}\left\|v^{t+1}\right\|_{2}/\sqrt{d}
,
so that
\lim_{d\to\infty}\left\|f_{t+1}(v^{t+1})\right\|_{2}/\sqrt{d}=1
,
as in
Equation

5.6
.
Furthermore, we set

\displaystyle g_{t}(u^{t};y)

\displaystyle=F_{\smalltriangleup}u^{t},\quad t\geq 0,

(F.3)

where
F_{\smalltriangleup}=\mathrm{diag}({\mathcal{F}}_{\smalltriangleup}(y))\in{\mathbb{R}}^{n\times n}
and
{\mathcal{F}}_{\smalltriangleup}(y)\in{\mathbb{R}}^{n}
is obtained by applying
{\mathcal{F}}_{\smalltriangleup}
to each entry of
y
.
The coefficients
b_{t+1},c_{t}
specialize to

\displaystyle b_{t+1}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\delta\beta_{t+1}},\quad c_{t}=\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathcal{F}}_{\smalltriangleup}(\overline{Y})\right]\eqqcolon c.

Following the argument of
Section

5.5
, we can show that
u^{t},v^{t+1},\beta_{t+1}
converge respectively to
u\in{\mathbb{R}}^{n},v\in{\mathbb{R}}^{d},\beta\in{\mathbb{R}}
in the following sense

\displaystyle\lim_{t\to\infty}\lim_{n\to\infty}\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\left\|u^{t}-u\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle=0,\quad\lim_{t\to\infty}\lim_{d\to\infty}\frac{1}{\sqrt{d}}\left\|v^{t+1}-v\right\|_{2}=0,\quad\lim_{t\to\infty}\left|\beta_{t+1}-\beta\right|=0.

Then in the
t\to\infty
limit, the GAMP iteration becomes

\displaystyle u

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\beta}\widetilde{X}v-bF_{\smalltriangleup}u,\quad v=\widetilde{X}^{\top}F_{\smalltriangleup}u-\frac{1}{\beta}cv,

where
b=\frac{1}{\delta\beta}
is the limit of
b_{t+1}
as
t\to\infty
.
Solving
u
in terms of
v
from the first equation, we get

\displaystyle u

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\beta}(I_{n}+bF_{\smalltriangleup})^{-1}\widetilde{X}v.

We then use this to eliminate
u
from the equation for
v
and obtain:

\displaystyle(\beta+c)v

\displaystyle=\widetilde{X}^{\top}F_{\smalltriangleup}(I_{n}+bF_{\smalltriangleup})^{-1}\widetilde{X}v.

(F.4)

Our aim is to choose
{\mathcal{F}}_{\smalltriangleup}
judiciously to turn the above equation into an eigenequation for
D_{\smalltriangleup}=\widetilde{X}^{\top}T\widetilde{X}
.
First, to simplify the derivation, we require
b=1
which will be the case if
\beta=\frac{1}{\delta}
.
Next, we choose

\displaystyle{\mathcal{F}}_{\smalltriangleup}(\cdot)

\displaystyle=\frac{{\mathcal{T}}(\cdot)}{a^{*}_{\smalltriangleup}-{\mathcal{T}}(\cdot)},

(F.5)

where
a^{*}_{\smalltriangleup}
is to be specified later.
With these choices,
Equation

F.4
becomes

\displaystyle\left(\frac{1}{\delta}+c\right)v

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{a^{*}_{\smalltriangleup}}\widetilde{X}^{\top}T\widetilde{X}v=\frac{1}{a^{*}_{\smalltriangleup}}D_{\smalltriangleup}v,

which, upon multiplying by
a^{*}_{\smalltriangleup}
on both sides, is an eigenequation of
D_{\smalltriangleup}
with respect to the eigenvalue

\displaystyle a^{*}_{\smalltriangleup}\left(\frac{1}{\delta}+c\right)=a^{*}_{\smalltriangleup}\left(\frac{1}{\delta}+\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{{\mathcal{T}}(\overline{Y})}{a^{*}_{\smalltriangleup}-{\mathcal{T}}(\overline{Y})}\right]\right),

and the corresponding eigenvector (up to scaling)
v
. The value of
a^{*}_{\smalltriangleup}
is fixed when we enforce
\beta=\frac{1}{\delta}
which in turn enforces
b=1
.
From the state evolution analysis presented below,
\beta
can be derived and therefore
a^{*}_{\smalltriangleup}
is defined as the solution to

\displaystyle\beta=\lim_{t\to\infty}\beta_{t+1}=\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\frac{\delta}{\mathbb{E}\left[\overline{\Sigma}\right]}\overline{G}^{2}-1\right)\frac{{\mathcal{T}}(\overline{Y})}{a^{*}_{\smalltriangleup}-{\mathcal{T}}(\overline{Y})}\right]=\frac{1}{\delta}.

(F.6)

Consider the unique solution
a^{*}_{\smalltriangleup}
to
Equation

F.6
in
\left(\sup\supp({\mathcal{T}}(\overline{Y})),\infty\right)
and let
{\mathcal{F}}_{\smalltriangleup}\colon{\mathbb{R}}\to{\mathbb{R}}
be defined in
Equation

F.5
.
Set the denoisers
(f_{t+1},g_{t})_{t\geq 0}
in
Equation

5.1
to those given in
Equations

F.2
and
F.3
and initialize the GAMP iteration with

\displaystyle\widetilde{u}^{-1}

\displaystyle=0_{n},\quad\widetilde{v}^{0}=\mu\widetilde{\beta}^{*}+\sqrt{1-\mu^{2}\mathbb{E}\left[\overline{\Sigma}\right]}w\in{\mathbb{R}}^{d},

(F.7)

where
w\sim{\mathcal{N}}(0_{d},I_{d})
is independent of everything else and
\mu
is given in
Equation

F.8
below.
Given all these configurations, the state evolution recursion specializes to

\displaystyle\mu_{t}

\displaystyle=\frac{\delta}{\mathbb{E}\left[\overline{\Sigma}\right]}\lim_{n\to\infty}\frac{1}{n}\mathbb{E}\left[(\widetilde{\mathfrak{B}}^{*})^{\top}V_{t}/\beta_{t}\right]=\frac{\delta}{\mathbb{E}\left[\overline{\Sigma}\right]}\lim_{n\to\infty}\frac{1}{n}\mathbb{E}\left[(\widetilde{\mathfrak{B}}^{*})^{\top}\widetilde{\mathfrak{B}}^{*}\right]\chi_{t}/\beta_{t}=\chi_{t}/\beta_{t},

\displaystyle\sigma_{U,t}^{2}

\displaystyle=\lim_{n\to\infty}\frac{1}{n}\mathbb{E}\left[V_{t}^{\top}V_{t}/\beta_{t}^{2}\right]-\frac{\mathbb{E}\left[\overline{\Sigma}\right]}{\delta}\mu_{t}^{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\beta_{t}^{2}}\lim_{n\to\infty}\frac{1}{n}\mathbb{E}\left[(\widetilde{\mathfrak{B}}^{*})^{\top}\widetilde{\mathfrak{B}}^{*}\right]\chi_{t}^{2}+\frac{1}{\beta_{t}^{2}}\lim_{n\to\infty}\frac{1}{n}\mathbb{E}\left[W_{V,t}^{\top}W_{V,t}\right]\sigma_{V,t}^{2}-\frac{\mathbb{E}\left[\overline{\Sigma}\right]}{\delta}\mu_{t}^{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\beta_{t}^{2}}\frac{\mathbb{E}\left[\overline{\Sigma}\right]}{\delta}\chi_{t}^{2}+\frac{1}{\beta_{t}^{2}}\frac{1}{\delta}\sigma_{V,t}^{2}-\frac{\mathbb{E}\left[\overline{\Sigma}\right]}{\delta}\mu_{t}^{2}=\frac{\sigma_{V,t}^{2}}{\delta\beta_{t}^{2}},

\displaystyle\chi_{t+1}

\displaystyle=\frac{\delta}{\mathbb{E}\left[\overline{\Sigma}\right]}\lim_{n\to\infty}\frac{1}{n}\mathbb{E}\left[G^{\top}F_{\smalltriangleup}U_{t}\right]-\mu_{t}\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathcal{F}}_{\smalltriangleup}(\overline{Y})\right]=\frac{\delta}{\mathbb{E}\left[\overline{\Sigma}\right]}\lim_{n\to\infty}\frac{1}{n}\mathbb{E}\left[G^{\top}F_{\smalltriangleup}G\right]\mu_{t}-\mu_{t}\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathcal{F}}_{\smalltriangleup}(\overline{Y})\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\frac{\delta}{\mathbb{E}\left[\overline{\Sigma}\right]}\overline{G}^{2}-1\right){\mathcal{F}}_{\smalltriangleup}(\overline{Y})\right]\mu_{t}=\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\frac{\delta}{\mathbb{E}\left[\overline{\Sigma}\right]}\overline{G}^{2}-1\right){\mathcal{F}}_{\smalltriangleup}(\overline{Y})\right]\frac{\chi_{t}}{\beta_{t}},

\displaystyle\sigma_{V,t+1}^{2}

\displaystyle=\lim_{n\to\infty}\frac{1}{n}\mathbb{E}\left[U_{t}^{\top}F_{\smalltriangleup}^{2}U_{t}\right]=\lim_{n\to\infty}\frac{1}{n}\mathbb{E}\left[G^{\top}F_{\smalltriangleup}^{2}G\right]\mu_{t}^{2}+\lim_{n\to\infty}\frac{1}{n}\mathbb{E}\left[W_{U,t}^{\top}F_{\smalltriangleup}^{2}W_{U,t}\right]\sigma_{U,t}^{2}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[\overline{G}^{2}{\mathcal{F}}_{\smalltriangleup}(\overline{Y})^{2}\right]\mu_{t}^{2}+\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathcal{F}}_{\smalltriangleup}(\overline{Y})^{2}\right]\sigma_{U,t}^{2}=\mathbb{E}\left[\overline{G}^{2}{\mathcal{F}}_{\smalltriangleup}(\overline{Y})^{2}\right]\frac{\chi_{t}^{2}}{\beta_{t}^{2}}+\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathcal{F}}_{\smalltriangleup}(\overline{Y})^{2}\right]\frac{\sigma_{V,t}^{2}}{\delta\beta_{t}^{2}},

\displaystyle\beta_{t+1}^{2}

\displaystyle=\lim_{d\to\infty}\frac{1}{d}\mathbb{E}\left[V_{t+1}^{\top}V_{t+1}\right]=\lim_{d\to\infty}\frac{1}{d}\mathbb{E}\left[(\widetilde{\mathfrak{B}}^{*})^{\top}\widetilde{\mathfrak{B}}^{*}\right]\chi_{t+1}^{2}+\lim_{d\to\infty}\frac{1}{d}\mathbb{E}\left[W_{V,t+1}^{\top}W_{V,t+1}\right]\sigma_{V,t+1}^{2}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[\overline{\Sigma}\right]\chi_{t+1}^{2}+\sigma_{V,t+1}^{2}.

There are
3
fixed points of
(\mu_{t},\sigma_{U,t},\chi_{t+1},\sigma_{V,t+1},\beta_{t+1})
:

\displaystyle\mathsf{FP}_{+}

\displaystyle=(\mu,\sigma_{U},\chi,\sigma_{V},\beta),\quad\mathsf{FP}_{-}=(-\mu,\sigma_{U},-\chi,\sigma_{V},\beta),

\displaystyle\mathsf{FP}_{0}

\displaystyle=\left(0,\frac{1}{\sqrt{\delta}},0,\frac{1}{\sqrt{\delta}}\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\frac{{\mathcal{T}}(\overline{Y})}{a^{*}_{\smalltriangleup}-{\mathcal{T}}(\overline{Y})}\right)^{2}\right]^{1/2},\frac{1}{\sqrt{\delta}}\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\frac{{\mathcal{T}}(\overline{Y})}{a^{*}_{\smalltriangleup}-{\mathcal{T}}(\overline{Y})}\right)^{2}\right]^{1/2}\right),

where
\mu,\sigma_{U},\chi,\sigma_{V},\beta
are given by

\displaystyle\beta

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\frac{\delta}{\mathbb{E}\left[\overline{\Sigma}\right]}\overline{G}^{2}-1\right)\frac{{\mathcal{T}}(\overline{Y})}{a^{*}_{\smalltriangleup}-{\mathcal{T}}(\overline{Y})}\right]=\frac{1}{\delta},

\displaystyle\chi

\displaystyle=\left(\frac{\beta^{2}-\frac{1}{\delta}\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathcal{F}}_{\smalltriangleup}(\overline{Y})^{2}\right]}{\mathbb{E}\left[\overline{\Sigma}\right]-\frac{\mathbb{E}\left[\overline{\Sigma}\right]}{\delta\beta^{2}}\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathcal{F}}_{\smalltriangleup}(\overline{Y})^{2}\right]+\frac{1}{\beta^{2}}\mathbb{E}\left[\overline{G}^{2}{\mathcal{F}}_{\smalltriangleup}(\overline{Y})^{2}\right]}\right)^{1/2}

\displaystyle=\left(\frac{\frac{1}{\delta^{2}}-\frac{1}{\delta}\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\frac{{\mathcal{T}}(\overline{Y})}{a^{*}_{\smalltriangleup}-{\mathcal{T}}(\overline{Y})}\right)^{2}\right]}{\mathbb{E}\left[\overline{\Sigma}\right]-\delta\mathbb{E}\left[\overline{\Sigma}\right]\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\frac{{\mathcal{T}}(\overline{Y})}{a^{*}_{\smalltriangleup}-{\mathcal{T}}(\overline{Y})}\right)^{2}\right]+\delta^{2}\mathbb{E}\left[\overline{G}^{2}\left(\frac{{\mathcal{T}}(\overline{Y})}{a^{*}_{\smalltriangleup}-{\mathcal{T}}(\overline{Y})}\right)^{2}\right]}\right)^{1/2},

\displaystyle\sigma_{V}

\displaystyle=\left(\frac{\delta^{2}\mathbb{E}\left[\overline{G}^{2}{\mathcal{F}}_{\smalltriangleup}(\overline{Y})^{2}\right]\chi^{2}}{1-\frac{1}{\delta\beta^{2}}\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathcal{F}}_{\smalltriangleup}(\overline{Y})^{2}\right]}\right)^{1/2}

\displaystyle=\left(\frac{\mathbb{E}\left[\overline{G}^{2}\left(\frac{{\mathcal{T}}(\overline{Y})}{a^{*}_{\smalltriangleup}-{\mathcal{T}}(\overline{Y})}\right)^{2}\right]}{\mathbb{E}\left[\overline{\Sigma}\right]-\delta\mathbb{E}\left[\overline{\Sigma}\right]\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\frac{{\mathcal{T}}(\overline{Y})}{a^{*}_{\smalltriangleup}-{\mathcal{T}}(\overline{Y})}\right)^{2}\right]+\delta^{2}\mathbb{E}\left[\overline{G}^{2}\left(\frac{{\mathcal{T}}(\overline{Y})}{a^{*}_{\smalltriangleup}-{\mathcal{T}}(\overline{Y})}\right)^{2}\right]}\right)^{1/2},

\displaystyle\mu

\displaystyle=\frac{\chi}{\beta}=\left(\frac{1-\delta\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\frac{{\mathcal{T}}(\overline{Y})}{a^{*}_{\smalltriangleup}-{\mathcal{T}}(\overline{Y})}\right)^{2}\right]}{\mathbb{E}\left[\overline{\Sigma}\right]-\delta\mathbb{E}\left[\overline{\Sigma}\right]\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\frac{{\mathcal{T}}(\overline{Y})}{a^{*}_{\smalltriangleup}-{\mathcal{T}}(\overline{Y})}\right)^{2}\right]+\delta^{2}\mathbb{E}\left[\overline{G}^{2}\left(\frac{{\mathcal{T}}(\overline{Y})}{a^{*}_{\smalltriangleup}-{\mathcal{T}}(\overline{Y})}\right)^{2}\right]}\right)^{1/2},

(F.8)

\displaystyle\sigma_{U}

\displaystyle=\frac{\sigma_{V}}{\sqrt{\delta}\beta}=\left(\frac{\delta\mathbb{E}\left[\overline{G}^{2}\left(\frac{{\mathcal{T}}(\overline{Y})}{a^{*}_{\smalltriangleup}-{\mathcal{T}}(\overline{Y})}\right)^{2}\right]}{\mathbb{E}\left[\overline{\Sigma}\right]-\delta\mathbb{E}\left[\overline{\Sigma}\right]\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\frac{{\mathcal{T}}(\overline{Y})}{a^{*}_{\smalltriangleup}-{\mathcal{T}}(\overline{Y})}\right)^{2}\right]+\delta^{2}\mathbb{E}\left[\overline{G}^{2}\left(\frac{{\mathcal{T}}(\overline{Y})}{a^{*}_{\smalltriangleup}-{\mathcal{T}}(\overline{Y})}\right)^{2}\right]}\right)^{1/2}.

Furthermore, the initialization scheme in
Equation

F.7
guarantees that
(\mu_{t},\sigma_{U,t},\chi_{t+1},\sigma_{V,t+1},\beta_{t+1})
stays at
\mathsf{FP}_{+}
for every
t\geq 0
.

Executing similar arguments in the proofs of
Lemma

5.4
and of
Equation

5.41
gives

\displaystyle\lim_{t\to\infty}\plim_{d\to\infty}\frac{\left\langle v^{t+1},v_{1}(D_{\smalltriangleup})\right\rangle^{2}}{\left\|v^{t+1}\right\|_{2}^{2}\left\|v_{1}(D_{\smalltriangleup})\right\|_{2}^{2}}

\displaystyle=1,\quad\plim_{d\to\infty}\lambda_{1}(D_{\smalltriangleup})=\zeta(a^{*}_{\smalltriangleup})>\zeta(a^{\circ}_{\smalltriangleup})=\lim_{d\to\infty}\lambda_{2}(D_{\smalltriangleup}).

(F.9)

Recall from
Equation

F.1
that the whitened spectral estimator is defined as
\beta_{\smalltriangleup}^{\mathrm{spec}}=\Sigma^{-1/2}v_{1}(D_{\smalltriangleup})
.
Given the result in
Equation

F.9
, the overlap between
\beta_{\smalltriangleup}^{\mathrm{spec}}
and
\beta^{*}
is asymptotically the same as that between
\Sigma^{-1/2}v^{t+1}
and
\beta^{*}
which we compute below:

\displaystyle\lim_{t\to\infty}\plim_{d\to\infty}\frac{\left\langle\Sigma^{-1/2}v^{t+1},\beta^{*}\right\rangle^{2}}{\left\|\Sigma^{-1/2}v^{t+1}\right\|_{2}^{2}\left\|\beta^{*}\right\|_{2}^{2}}

\displaystyle=\frac{\lim\limits_{t\to\infty}\plim\limits_{d\to\infty}\frac{1}{d^{2}}\left\langle\Sigma^{-1/2}v^{t+1},\beta^{*}\right\rangle^{2}}{\lim\limits_{t\to\infty}\plim\limits_{d\to\infty}\frac{1}{d}\left\|\Sigma^{-1/2}v^{t+1}\right\|_{2}^{2}},

the numerator and denominator of which are given respectively as follows:

\displaystyle\lim_{t\to\infty}\plim_{d\to\infty}\frac{1}{d^{2}}\left\langle\Sigma^{-1/2}v^{t+1},\beta^{*}\right\rangle^{2}=\lim_{t\to\infty}\lim_{d\to\infty}\frac{1}{d^{2}}\mathbb{E}\left[(\widetilde{\mathfrak{B}}^{*})^{\top}\Sigma^{-1/2}\mathfrak{B}^{*}\right]^{2}\chi_{t+1}^{2}=\chi^{2},

\displaystyle\lim_{t\to\infty}\plim_{d\to\infty}\frac{1}{d}\left\|\Sigma^{-1/2}v^{t+1}\right\|_{2}^{2}=\lim_{t\to\infty}\lim_{d\to\infty}\frac{1}{d}\mathbb{E}\left[(\widetilde{\mathfrak{B}}^{*})^{\top}\Sigma^{-1}\widetilde{\mathfrak{B}}^{*}\right]\chi_{t+1}^{2}+\frac{1}{d}\mathbb{E}\left[W_{V,t+1}^{\top}\Sigma^{-1}W_{V,t+1}\right]\sigma_{V,t+1}^{2}

\displaystyle\hskip 106.00021pt=\chi^{2}+\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{1}{\overline{\Sigma}}\right]\sigma_{V}^{2}.

Using the expressions of
\chi,\sigma_{V}
, we obtain

\displaystyle\plim_{d\to\infty}\frac{\left\langle\beta^{\mathrm{spec}}_{\smalltriangleup},\beta^{*}\right\rangle^{2}}{\left\|\beta^{\mathrm{spec}}_{\smalltriangleup}\right\|_{2}^{2}\left\|\beta^{*}\right\|_{2}^{2}}

\displaystyle=\frac{\chi^{2}}{\chi^{2}+\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{1}{\overline{\Sigma}}\right]\sigma_{V}^{2}}=\frac{\frac{1}{\delta^{2}}-\frac{1}{\delta}\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\frac{{\mathcal{T}}(\overline{Y})}{a^{*}_{\smalltriangleup}-{\mathcal{T}}(\overline{Y})}\right)^{2}\right]}{\frac{1}{\delta^{2}}-\frac{1}{\delta}\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\frac{{\mathcal{T}}(\overline{Y})}{a^{*}_{\smalltriangleup}-{\mathcal{T}}(\overline{Y})}\right)^{2}\right]+\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{1}{\overline{\Sigma}}\right]\mathbb{E}\left[\overline{G}^{2}\left(\frac{{\mathcal{T}}(\overline{Y})}{a^{*}_{\smalltriangleup}-{\mathcal{T}}(\overline{Y})}\right)^{2}\right]}

\displaystyle=\frac{1-\delta\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\frac{{\mathcal{T}}(\overline{Y})}{a^{*}_{\smalltriangleup}-{\mathcal{T}}(\overline{Y})}\right)^{2}\right]}{1+\delta\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{\delta}{\overline{\Sigma}}\right]\overline{G}^{2}-1\right)\left(\frac{{\mathcal{T}}(\overline{Y})}{a^{*}_{\smalltriangleup}-{\mathcal{T}}(\overline{Y})}\right)^{2}\right]}=\eta_{\smalltriangleup},

which concludes the proof.
∎

Appendix G
Auxiliary results

Proposition G.1

(
w_{1}>0
)
.

Let
w_{1}
be defined in
Equation

3.9
.
Then
w_{1}>0
.

Proof.

By definition, we have

\displaystyle w_{1}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\mathbb{E}\left[\overline{\Sigma}\right]^{2}}\mathbb{E}\left[\overline{G}^{2}\left(\frac{{\mathcal{T}}(\overline{Y})}{a^{*}-{\mathcal{T}}(\overline{Y})}\right)^{2}\right]\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{\overline{\Sigma}^{2}}{\gamma^{*}-\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{{\mathcal{T}}(\overline{Y})}{a^{*}-{\mathcal{T}}(\overline{Y})}\right]\overline{\Sigma}}\right]^{2}

\displaystyle\phantom{=}~-\frac{1}{\delta\mathbb{E}\left[\overline{\Sigma}\right]}\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\frac{{\mathcal{T}}(\overline{Y})}{a^{*}-{\mathcal{T}}(\overline{Y})}\right)^{2}\right]\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{\overline{\Sigma}^{2}}{\gamma^{*}-\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{{\mathcal{T}}(\overline{Y})}{a^{*}-{\mathcal{T}}(\overline{Y})}\right]\overline{\Sigma}}\right]^{2}

\displaystyle\phantom{=}~+\frac{1}{\delta}\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\frac{{\mathcal{T}}(\overline{Y})}{a^{*}-{\mathcal{T}}(\overline{Y})}\right)^{2}\right]\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{\overline{\Sigma}^{3}}{\left(\gamma^{*}-\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{{\mathcal{T}}(\overline{Y})}{a^{*}-{\mathcal{T}}(\overline{Y})}\right]\overline{\Sigma}\right)^{2}}\right].

The first term is strictly positive.
It suffices to show that the sum of the last two terms is non-negative.
This follows from the Cauchy–Schwarz inequality:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{\overline{\Sigma}^{2}}{\gamma^{*}-\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{{\mathcal{T}}(\overline{Y})}{a^{*}-{\mathcal{T}}(\overline{Y})}\right]\overline{\Sigma}}\right]^{2}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[\overline{\Sigma}^{1/2}\cdot\frac{\overline{\Sigma}^{3/2}}{\gamma^{*}-\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{{\mathcal{T}}(\overline{Y})}{a^{*}-{\mathcal{T}}(\overline{Y})}\right]\overline{\Sigma}}\right]^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}\left[\overline{\Sigma}\right]\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{\overline{\Sigma}^{3}}{\left(\gamma^{*}-\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{{\mathcal{T}}(\overline{Y})}{a^{*}-{\mathcal{T}}(\overline{Y})}\right]\overline{\Sigma}\right)^{2}}\right].

(G.1)

Rearranging terms and noting that the common factor
\frac{1}{\delta}\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\frac{{\mathcal{T}}(\overline{Y})}{a^{*}-{\mathcal{T}}(\overline{Y})}\right)^{2}\right]
in the last two terms is positive, the proof is complete.
∎

Proposition G.2
.

Let
W\sim P^{\otimes d}
where
P
is a distribution on
{\mathbb{R}}
with mean
0
and variance
\sigma^{2}
.
Let
B\in{\mathbb{R}}^{d\times d}
denote a sequence of deterministic matrices such that the empirical spectral distribution of
\frac{1}{d}B
converges to the law of a random variable
\overline{\Sigma}
.
Then

\displaystyle\lim_{d\to\infty}\frac{1}{d}\mathbb{E}\left[W^{\top}BW\right]

\displaystyle=\sigma^{2}\mathbb{E}\left[\overline{\Sigma}\right].

Proof.

The proof follows from a straightforward calculation:

\displaystyle\lim_{d\to\infty}\frac{1}{d}\mathbb{E}\left[W^{\top}BW\right]

\displaystyle=\lim_{d\to\infty}\frac{1}{d}\sum_{i,j}\mathbb{E}\left[B_{i,j}W_{i}W_{j}\right]

\displaystyle=\lim_{d\to\infty}\frac{1}{d}\sum_{i}\mathbb{E}\left[W_{i}^{2}\right]B_{i,i}=\lim_{d\to\infty}\frac{\sigma^{2}}{d}\tr(B)=\sigma^{2}\mathbb{E}\left[\overline{\Sigma}\right].

∎

Proposition G.3
.

Let

(G,H)\sim{\mathcal{N}}\left(\begin{bmatrix}0_{d}\\
0_{d}\end{bmatrix},\begin{bmatrix}\sigma^{2}&\rho\\
\rho&\tau^{2}\end{bmatrix}\otimes I_{d}\right)
. Let
B\in{\mathbb{R}}^{d\times d}
denote a sequence of deterministic matrices such that the empirical spectral distribution of
\frac{1}{d}B
converges to the law of a random variable
\overline{\Sigma}
.
Then

\displaystyle\lim_{d\to\infty}\,\frac{1}{d}\mathbb{E}\left[G^{\top}BH\right]

\displaystyle=\rho\mathbb{E}\left[\overline{\Sigma}\right].

Proof.

The proof follows from a straightforward calculation:

\displaystyle\lim_{d\to\infty}\frac{1}{d}\mathbb{E}\left[G^{\top}BH\right]

\displaystyle=\lim_{d\to\infty}\frac{1}{d}\sum_{i,j}\mathbb{E}\left[B_{i,j}G_{i}H_{j}\right]

\displaystyle=\lim_{d\to\infty}\frac{1}{d}\sum_{i}\mathbb{E}\left[G_{i}H_{i}\right]B_{i,i}=\lim_{d\to\infty}\frac{\rho}{d}\tr(B)=\rho\mathbb{E}\left[\overline{\Sigma}\right].

∎

Proposition G.4

(Davis–Kahan
[
DK70
]
)
.

Let
A,B\in{\mathbb{R}}^{d\times d}
be symmetric matrices.
Then

\displaystyle\min\left\{\left\|v_{1}(A)-v_{1}(B)\right\|_{2},\left\|v_{1}(A)+v_{1}(B)\right\|_{2}\right\}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{4\left\|A-B\right\|_{2}}{\max\left\{\lambda_{1}(A)-\lambda_{2}(A),\lambda_{1}(B)-\lambda_{2}(B)\right\}}.

Note that the minimum on the left-hand side is to resolve the sign ambiguity since
v
is an eigenvector if and only if
-v
is.

Remark G.1

(Spectral threshold with right rotationally invariant designs)
.

The optimal spectral threshold for phase retrieval with right rotationally invariant designs was derived by Maillard et al. in
[
MLKZ20
, Equation (11)]
, and this expression coincides with
Equation

3.13
.
To see this, note that
Equation

3.13
involves the limiting spectral distribution of
\Sigma
only through its first two moments.
One can then express the same result using the limiting spectral distribution
\overline{\mu}_{X^{\top}X}
of
X^{\top}X=\Sigma^{1/2}\widetilde{X}^{\top}\widetilde{X}\Sigma^{1/2}
, which equals the free multiplicative convolution between the Marchenko–Pastur law
\mathsf{MP}_{\lambda}
(with
\lambda=1/\delta
)
and
\mathrm{law}(\overline{\Sigma})
.
In particular, let
\overline{\Lambda}
be the random variable with law
\overline{\mu}_{X^{\top}X}
. By using the moment-cumulant relation
[
Nov14
, Section 2.5]
and an identity relating the square free cumulants of
\mathrm{law}(\overline{\Sigma})
to the rectangular free cumulants of
\mathrm{law}(\overline{\Sigma})\boxtimes\mathsf{MP}_{1/\delta}

[
BG10
, Remark 2]
, we have that
\mathbb{E}\left[\overline{\Lambda}\right]=\mathbb{E}\left[\overline{\Sigma}\right]
and
\mathbb{E}\left[\overline{\Lambda}^{2}\right]=\mathbb{E}\left[\overline{\Sigma}^{2}\right]+\frac{1}{\delta}\mathbb{E}\left[\overline{\Sigma}\right]^{2}
. Using these identities to write
Equation

3.13
in terms of the first two moments of
\overline{\Lambda}
